0x4C TIM_DMAR DMA address for full transfer
0x50 TIM_VERSION adv_timer version.
0x54 TIM_IC_PSC_REP input capture prescalar for larger prescale.
0x58 TIM_AF1 alternate function option register 1.
0x5C TIM_AF2 alternate function option register 2.
0x60 TIM_TISEL timer input selection register.
0x64 TIM_OUTPUT_DELAY timer output delay register.
7.5.1.1 TIM CONTROL REGISTER 1
Address offset: 0x0.
Table 7.3: Register Format of TIM_CR1
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
CEN 0 RW 0 Counter enable
• 1’b0: Counter disabled
• 1’b1: Counter enabled
UDIS 1 RW 0 Update disable
This bit is set and cleared by software to enable/disable UEV event genera tion.
• 1’b0: UEV enabled. The Update (UEV) event is generated by one of
the following events:
a. Counter overflow/underflow
b. Setting the UG bit
c. Update generation through the slave mode controller
Buffered registers are then loaded with their preload values.
• 1’b1: UEV disabled. The Update event is not generated, shadow
registers keep their value (ARR, PSC, CCRx).
continues on next page
7.5. Registers 65
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.3 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
URS 2 RW 0 Update request source
This bit is set and cleared by software to select the UEV event sources.
• 1’b0: Any of the following events generate an update interrupt or
DMA request if enabled. These events can be:
a. Counter overflow/underflow
b. Setting the UG bit
c. Update generation through the slave mode controller
• 1’b1: Only counter overflow/underflow generates an update interrupt
or DMA request if
enabled.
OPM 3 RW 0 One pulse mode
• 1’b0: Counter is not stopped at update event
• 1’b1: Counter stops counting at the next update event (clearing the
bit CEN)
DIR 4 RW 0 Direction
• 1’b0: Counter used as upcounter
• 1’b1: Counter used as downcounter
note: when CMS != 0, DIR is update by hardware,so you can’t write this
bit.but if you real write this bit ,this bit is overwriten.
CMS 6:5 RW 0 Center-aligned mode selection
• 2’b00: Edge-aligned mode. The counter counts up or down depend ing on the direction bit (DIR).
• 2’b01: Center-aligned mode 1. The counter counts up and down al ternatively. Output compare interrupt flags of channels configured in
output (CCxS=00 in TIMx_CCMRx register) are set only when the
counter is counting down.
• 2’b10: Center-aligned mode 2. The counter counts up and down al ternatively. Output compare interrupt flags of channels configured in
output (CCxS=00 in TIMx_CCMRx register) are set only when the
counter is counting up.
• 2’b11: Center-aligned mode 3. The counter counts up and down al ternatively. Output compare interrupt flags of channels configured in
output (CCxS=00 in TIMx_CCMRx register) are set both when the
counter is counting up or down.
ARPE 7 RW 0 Auto-reload preload enable
• 1’b0: TIMx_ARR register is not buffered
• 1’b1: TIMx_ARR register is buffered
CKD 9:8 RW 0 clock division
This bit-field indicates the division ratio between the timer clock (CK_INT)
frequency and the dead-time and sampling clock (tDTS)used by the dead time generators and the digital filters (ETR, TIx),
• 2’b00: tDTS=tCK_INT
• 2’b01: tDTS=2*tCK_INT
• 2’b10: tDTS=4*tCK_INT
• 2’b11: tDTS=tCK_INT
Reserved 31:10 / 0x0 /
7.5. Registers 66
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
7.5.1.2 TIM CONTROL REGISTER 2
Address offset: 0x4.
Table 7.4: Register Format of TIM_CR2
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
CCPC 0 RW 0 Capture/compare preloaded control
• 1’b0: CCxP,CCxNP, CCxE, CCxNE and OCxM bits are not
preloaded
• 1’b1: CCxP,CCxNP, CCxE, CCxNE and OCxM bits are preloaded,
after having been written, they are updated only when a commutation
event (COM) occurs (COMG bit set or rising edge detected on TRGI,
depending on the CCUS bit).
Reserved 1 / 0x0 /
CCUS 2 RW 0 Capture/compare control update selection
• 1’b0: When capture/compare control bits are preloaded (CCPC=1),
they are updated by setting the COMG bit only
• 1’b1: When capture/compare control bits are preloaded (CCPC=1),
they are updated by setting the COMG bit or when an rising edge
occurs on TRGI
CCDS 3 RW 0 Capture/compare DMA selection
• 1’b0: CCx DMA request sent when CCx event occurs
• 1’b1: CCx DMA requests sent when update event occurs
MMS 6:4 RW 0 Master mode selection
These bits allow to select the information to be sent in master mode to slave
timers for synchronization (TRGO). The combination is as follows:
• 3’b000: Reset - the UG bit from the TIMx_EGR register is used as
trigger output (TRGO). If the reset is generated by the trigger input
(slave mode controller configured in reset mode) then the signal on
TRGO is delayed compared to the actual reset.
• 3’b001: Enable - the Counter Enable signal CNT_EN is used as trig ger output (TRGO). It is useful to start several timers at the same time
or to control a window in which a slave timer is enable. The Counter
Enable signal is generated by a logic AND between CEN control
bit and the trigger input when configured in gated mode. When the
Counter Enable signal is controlled by the trigger input, there is a
delay on TRGO, except if the master/slave mode is selected (see the
MSM bit description in TIMx_SMCR register).
• 3’b010: Update - The update event is selected as trigger output
(TRGO). For instance a master timer can then be used as a prescaler
for a slave timer.
• 3’b011: Compare Pulse - The trigger output send a positive pulse
when the CC1IF flag is to be set (even if it was already high), as soon
as a capture or a compare match occurred.(TRGO).
• 3’b100: Compare - OC1REF signal is used as trigger output (TRGO)
• 3’b101: Compare - OC2REF signal is used as trigger output (TRGO)
• 3’b110: Compare - OC3REF signal is used as trigger output (TRGO)
• 3’b111: Compare - OC4REF signal is used as trigger output (TRGO)
TI1S 7 RW 0 TI1 selection
• 1’b0: The CH1 pin is connected to TI1 input
• 1’b1: The CH1,CH2 and CH3 pins are connected to the TI1 in put(XOR combination)
continues on next page

Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual

Value
Description
OIS1 8 RW 0 Output Idle state 1 (OC1 output)
• 1’b0: OC1=0 (after a dead-time if OC1N is implemented) when
MOE=0
• 1’b1: OC1=1 (after a dead-time if OC1N is implemented) when
MOE=0
OIS1N 9 RW 0 Output Idle state 1 (OC1N output)
• 1’b0: OC1N=0 after a dead-time when MOE=0
• 1’b1: OC1N=1 after a dead-time when MOE=0
OIS2 10 RW 0 Output Idle state 2 (OC2 output)
• 1’b0: OC1=0 (after a dead-time if OC1N is implemented) when
MOE=0
• 1’b1: OC1=1 (after a dead-time if OC1N is implemented) when
MOE=0
OIS2N 11 RW 0 Output Idle state 2 (OC2N output)
• 1’b0: OC1N=0 after a dead-time when MOE=0
• 1’b1: OC1N=1 after a dead-time when MOE=0
OIS3 12 RW 0 Output Idle state 3 (OC3 output)
• 1’b0: OC1=0 (after a dead-time if OC1N is implemented) when
MOE=0
• 1’b1: OC1=1 (after a dead-time if OC1N is implemented) when
MOE=0
OIS3N 13 RW 0 Output Idle state 3 (OC3N output)
• 1’b0: OC1N=0 after a dead-time when MOE=0
• 1’b1: OC1N=1 after a dead-time when MOE=0
OIS4 14 RW 0 Output Idle state 4 (OC4 output)
• 1’b0: OC1=0 (after a dead-time if OC1N is implemented) when
MOE=0
• 1’b1: OC1=1 (after a dead-time if OC1N is implemented) when
MOE=0
Reserved 31:15 / 0x0 /
7.5.1.3 TIM SLAVE MODE CONTORL REGISTER
Address offset: 0x8.
7.5. Registers 68
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.5: Register Format of TIM_SMCR
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
SMS 3:0 RW 0 Slave mode selection
When external signals are selected the active edge of the trigger signal
(TRGI) is linked to the polarity selected on the external input (see Input
Control register and Control Register description.
• 4’b0000: Slave mode disabled - if CEN = ‘1’ then the prescaler is
clocked directly by the internal clock.
• 4’b0001: Encoder mode 1 - Counter counts up/down on TI1FP1 edge
depending on TI2FP2 level.
• 4’b0010: Encoder mode 2 - Counter counts up/down on TI2FP2 edge
depending on TI1FP1 level.
• 4’b0011: Encoder mode 3 - Counter counts up/down on both TI1FP1
and TI2FP2 edges depending on the level of the other input.
• 4’b0100: Reset Mode - Rising edge of the selected trigger input
(TRGI) reinitializes the counter and generates an update of the regis ters.
• 4’b0101: Gated Mode - The counter clock is enabled when the trigger
input (TRGI) is high. The counter stops (but is not reset) as soon
as the trigger becomes low. Both start and stop of the counter are
controlled.
• 4’b0110: Trigger Mode - The counter starts at a rising edge of the
trigger TRGI (but it is not reset). Only the start of the counter is
controlled.
• 4’b0111: External Clock Mode 1 - Rising edges of the selected trig ger (TRGI) clock the counter.
• 4’b1000: Combined reset + trigger mode – Rising edge of the se lected trigger input(TRGI) reinitializes the counter, generates an up date of the registers and starts the counter.
Codes above 1000 : Reserved.
When CC1 channel configured as input: CC1NP/CC1P bits select the active
polarity of TI1FP1 and TI2FP1 for trigger or capture operations, please refer
the CC1P register.
TS 8:4 RW 0 Trigger selection
This bit-field selects the trigger input to be used to synchronize the counter.
• 5’b00000: Internal Trigger 0 (ITR0)
• 5’b00001: Internal Trigger 1 (ITR1)
• 5’b00010: Internal Trigger 2 (ITR2)
• 5’b00011: Internal Trigger 3 (ITR3)
• 5’b00100: TI1 Edge Detector (TI1F_ED)
• 5’b00101: Filtered Timer Input 1 (TI1FP1)
• 5’b00110: Filtered Timer Input 2 (TI2FP2)
• 5’b00111: External Trigger input (ETRF)
• 5’b01100: Internal Trigger 4 (ITR4)
• 5’b01101: Internal Trigger 5 (ITR5)
• 5’b01110: Internal Trigger 6 (ITR6)
• 5’b01111: Internal Trigger 7 (ITR7)
• 5’b10100: Internal Trigger 8 (ITR8)
• 5’b10101: Internal Trigger 9 (ITR9)
• 5’b10110: Internal Trigger 10 (ITR10)
• 5’b10111: Internal Trigger 11 (ITR11)
• 5’b11100: Internal Trigger 12 (ITR12)
• 5’b11101: Internal Trigger 13 (ITR13)
• 5’b11110: Internal Trigger 14 (ITR14)
• 5’b11111: Internal Trigger 15 (ITR15)
others reserved.
continues on next page
7.5. Registers 69
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.5 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
MSM 9 RW 0 Master/slave mode
• 1’b0: No action
• 1’b1: The effect of an event on the trigger input (TRGI) is delayed
to allow a perfect synchronization between the current timer and its
slaves (through TRGO). It is useful if we want to synchronize several
timers on a single external event.
ETF 13:10 RW 0 External trigger filter
This bit-field then defines the frequency used to sample ETRP signal and
the length of the digital filter applied to ETRP. The digital filter is made
of an event counter in which N consecutive events are needed to validate a
transition on the output:
• 4’b0000: No filter, sampling is done at fDTS
• 4’b0001: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=2
• 4’b0010: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=4
• 4’b0011: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=8
• 4’b0100: fSAMPLING=fDTS/2, N=6
• 4’b0101: fSAMPLING=fDTS/2, N=8
• 4’b0110: fSAMPLING=fDTS/4, N=6
• 4’b0111: fSAMPLING=fDTS/4, N=8
• 4’b1000: fSAMPLING=fDTS/8, N=6
• 4’b1001: fSAMPLING=fDTS/8, N=8
• 4’b1010: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=5
• 4’b1011: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=6
• 4’b1100: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=8
• 4’b1101: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=5
• 4’b1110: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=6
• 4’b1111: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=8
ETPS 15:14 RW 0 External trigger prescaler
External trigger signal ETRP frequency must be at most 1/4 of TIMxCLK
frequency. A prescaler can be enabled to reduce ETRP frequency. It is
useful when inputting fast external clocks.
• 2’b00: Prescaler OFF
• 2’b01: ETRP frequency divided by 2
• 2’b10: ETRP frequency divided by 4
• 2’b11: ETRP frequency divided by 8
ECE 16 RW 0 External clock enable
This bit enables External clock mode 2.
• 1’b0: External clock mode 2 disabled
• 1’b1: External clock mode 2 enabled. The counter is clocked by any
active edge on the ETRF signal.
Note:
1: Setting the ECE bit has the same effect as selecting external clock mode
1 with
TRGI connected to ETRF (SMS=111 and TS=00111).
2: It is possible to simultaneously use external clock mode 2 with the fol lowing slave modes: reset mode, gated mode and trigger mode. Neverthe less, TRGI must not be connected to ETRF in this case (TS bits must not
be 00111).
3: If external clock mode 1 and external clock mode 2 are enabled at the
same time, the external clock input is ETRF
continues on next page

Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual

Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
ETP 17 RW 0 External trigger polarity
This bit selects whether ETR or ETR is used for trigger operations
• 1’b0: ETR is non-inverted, active at high level or rising edge.
• 1’b1: ETR is inverted, active at low level or falling edge.

CC2DE 10 RW 0 Capture/Compare 2 DMA request enable

7.5.1.5 TIM STATUS REGISTER
Address offset: 0x10.
Table 7.7: Register Format of TIM_SR
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
UIF 0 RC 0 Update interrupt flag
This bit is set by hardware on an update event. It is cleared by software.
• 0: No update occurred.
• 1: Update interrupt pending. This bit is set by hardware when the
registers are updated:
• At overflow or underflow regarding the repetition counter value (up date if repetition counter = 0) and if the UDIS=0 in the TIMx_CR1
register.
• When CNT is reinitialized by software using the UG bit in
TIMx_EGR register, if URS=0 and UDIS=0 in the TIMx_CR1 reg ister.
• When CNT is reinitialized by a trigger event,if URS=0 and UDIS=0
in the TIMx_CR1 register.
note: this bit can read clear or write 0 to clear(RC / W0C)
continues on next page
7.5. Registers 72
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.7 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
CC1IF 1 W0C 0 Capture/Compare 1 interrupt flag
If channel CC1 is configured as output: This flag is set by hardware when
the counter matches the compare value, with some exception in center aligned mode (refer to the CMS bits in the TIMx_CR1 register description).
It is cleared by software.
• 0: No match.
• 1: The content of the counter TIMx_CNT matches the content of the
TIMx_CCR1 register.
When the contents of TIMx_CCR1 are greater than the contents of
TIMx_ARR, the CC1IF bit goes high on the counter overflow (in upcount ing and up/down-counting modes) or underflow (in downcounting mode)
If channel CC1 is configured as input: This bit is set by hardware on a
capture. It is cleared by software or by reading the TIMx_CCR1 register.
• 0: No input capture occurred
• 1: The counter value has been captured in TIMx_CCR1 register (An
edge has been detected on IC1 which matches the selected polarity)
CC2IF 2 W0C 0 Capture/Compare 2 interrupt flag
Refer to CC1IF description
CC3IF 3 W0C 0 Capture/Compare 3 interrupt flag
Refer to CC1IF description
CC4IF 4 W0C 0 Capture/Compare 4 interrupt flag
Refer to CC1IF description
COMIF 5 W0C 0 COM interrupt flag
This flag is set by hardware on COM event (when Capture/compare Control
bits - CCxE, CCxNE, OCxM - have been updated). It is cleared by software.
• 0: No COM event occurred.
• 1: COM interrupt pending.
TIF 6 W0C 0 Trigger interrupt flag
This flag is set by hardware on trigger event (active edge detected on TRGI
input when the slave mode controller is enabled in all modes but gated
mode. It is set when the counter starts or stops when gated mode is se lected. It is cleared by software.
• 0: No trigger event occurred.
• 1: Trigger interrupt pending.
BIF 7 W0C 0 Break interrupt flag
This flag is set by hardware as soon as the break input goes active. It can
be cleared by software if the break input is not active.
• 0: No break event occurred.
• 1: An active level has been detected on the break input. An interrupt
is generated if BIE=1 in the TIM_DIER register.
B2IF 8 W0C 0 Break 2 interrupt flag
This flag is set by hardware as soon as the break 2 input goes
active. It can be cleared by software if the break 2 input is not
active.
• 0: No break event occurred.
• 1: An active level has been detected on the break 2 input. An interrupt
is generated if BIE=1 in the TIM_DIER register.
continues on next page
7.5. Registers 73
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.7 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
CC1OF 9 W0C 0 Capture/Compare 1 overcapture flag
This flag is set by hardware only when the corresponding channel is con figured in input capture mode. It is cleared by software by writing it to
‘0’.
• 0: No overcapture has been detected.
• 1: The counter value has been captured in TIMx_CCR1 register while
CC1IF flag was already set
CC2OF 10 W0C 0 Capture/Compare 2 overcapture flag
Refer to CC1OF description
CC3OF 11 W0C 0 Capture/Compare 3 overcapture flag
Refer to CC1OF description
CC4OF 12 W0C 0 Capture/Compare 4 overcapture flag
Refer to CC1OF description
SBIF 13 W0C 0 System Break interrupt flag
This flag is set by hardware as soon as the system break input goes active.
It can be cleared by software if the system break input is not active.
This flag must be reset to re-start PWM operation.
• 0: NO break event occurred.
• 1: An active level has been detected on the system break input. An
interrupt is generated if BIE=1 in the TIM_DIER register.
Reserved 31:14 / 0x0 /
7.5.1.6 TIM EVENT GENERATION REGISTER
Address offset: 0x14.
Table 7.8: Register Format of TIM_EGR
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
UG 0 WO 0 Update generation
This bit can be set by software, it is automatically cleared by hardware.
• 0: No action
• 1: Reinitialize the counter and generates an update of the registers.
Note that the prescaler counter is cleared too (anyway the prescaler
ratio is not affected). The counter is cleared if the center-aligned
mode is selected or if DIR=0 (upcounting), else it takes the auto reload value (TIMx_ARR) if DIR=1 (downcounting).
CC1G 1 WO 0 Capture/Compare 1 generation
This bit is set by software in order to generate an event, it is automatically
cleared by hardware.
0: No action
1: A capture/compare event is generated on channel 1:
If channel CC1 is configured as output:
CC1IF flag is set, Corresponding interrupt or DMA request is sent if en abled.
If channel CC1 is configured as input:
The current value of the counter is captured in TIMx_CCR1 register. The
CC1IF flag is set, the corresponding interrupt or DMA request is sent if
enabled. The CC1OF flag is set if the CC1IF flag was already high.

7.5. Registers 74
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.8 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
CC2G 2 WO 0 Capture/Compare 2 generation
Refer to CC1G description
CC3G 3 WO 0 Capture/Compare 3 generation
Refer to CC1G description
CC4G 4 WO 0 Capture/Compare 4 generation
Refer to CC1G description
COMG 5 WO 0 Capture/Compare control update generation
This bit can be set by software, it is automatically cleared by hardware
• 0: No action
• 1: When CCPC bit is set, it allows to update CCxE, CCxNE and
OCxM bits
TG 6 WO 0 Trigger generation
This bit is set by software in order to generate an event, it is automatically
cleared by hardware.
• 0: No action
• 1: The TIF flag is set in TIMx_SR register. Related interrupt or DMA
transfer can occur if enabled.
BG 7 WO 0 Break generation
This bit is set by software in order to generate an event, it is automatically
cleared by hardware.
• 0: No action
• 1: A break event is generated. MOE bit is cleared and BIF flag is set.
Related interrupt can occur if enabled.
B2G 8 WO 0 Break generation
This bit is set by software in order to generate an event, it is automatically
cleared by hardware.
• 0: No action
• 1: A break 2 event is generated. MOE bit is cleared and B2IF flag is
set. Related interrupt can occur if enabled.
Reserved 31:9 / 0x0 /
7.5.1.7 TIM CAPTURE/COMPARE MODE REGISTER 1 OUPTPUT COMPARE MODE
The same register can be used for input capture mode (this section) or for output compare mode (next section). The
direction of a channel is defined by configuring the corresponding CCxS bits. All the other bits of this register have a
different function for input capture and for output compare modes. It is possible to combine both modes independently
(e.g. channel 1 in input capture mode and channel 2 in output compare mode).
Address offset: 0x18.
7.5. Registers 75
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.9: Register Format of TIM_CCMR1
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
CC1S 1:0 RW 0 Capture/Compare 1 selection
This bit-field defines the direction of the channel (input/output) as well as
the used input.
• 2’b00:CC1 channel is configured as output
• 2’b01: CC1 channel is configured as input, IC1 is mapped on TI1
• 2’b10: CC1 channel is configured as input, IC1 is mapped on TI2
• 2’b11: CC1 channel is configured as input, IC1 is mapped on TRC.
This mode is working only if an internal trigger input is selected
through TS bit (TIM_SMCR register)
OC1PE 2 RW 0 Output Compare 1 preload enable
• 1’b0: Preload register on TIM_CCR1 disabled. TIM_CCR1 can be
written at anytime, the new value is taken in account immediately.
• 1’b1:Preload register on TIM_CCR1 enabled. Read/Write operations
access the preload register. TIM_CCR1 preload value is loaded in the
active register at each update event.
continues on next page
7.5. Registers 76
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.9 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
OC1M 6:3 RW 0 Output Compare 1 mode
These bits define the behavior of the output reference signal OC1REF from
which OC1 and OC1N are derived. OC1REF is active high whereas OC1
and OC1N active level depends on CC1P and CC1NP bits.
• 4’b0000: Frozen - The comparison between the output compare reg ister TIM_CCR1 and the counter TIM_CNT has no effect on the out puts.(this mode is used to generate a timing base).
• 4’b0001: Set channel 1 to active level on match. OC1REF sig nal is forced high when the counter TIM_CNT matches the cap ture/compare register 1 (TIM_CCR1).
• 4’b0010: Set channel 1 to inactive level on match. OC1REF signal is
forced low when the counter TIM_CNT matches the capture/compare
register 1 (TIM_CCR1).
• 4’b0011: Toggle - OC1REF toggles when TIM_CNT=TIM_CCR1.
• 4’b0100: Force inactive level - OC1REF is forced low.
• 4’b0101: Force active level - OC1REF is forced high.
• 4’b0110: PWM mode 1 - In upcounting, channel 1 is active as long
as TIM_CNT<TIM_CCR1 else inactive. In downcounting, channel
1 is inactive (OC1REF=’0’) as long as TIM_CNT>TIM_CCR1 else
active (OC1REF=’1’).
• 4’b0111: PWM mode 2 - In upcounting, channel 1 is inactive as long
as TIM_CNT<TIM_CCR1 else active. In downcounting, channel 1
is active as long as TIM_CNT>TIM_CCR1 else inactive.
• 4’b1000: Retrigerrable OPM mode 1 - In up-counting mode, the
channel is active until a trigger event is detected (on TRGI signal).
Then, a comparison is performed as in PWM mode 1 and the channels
becomes active again at the next update. In down-counting mode, the
channel is inactive until a trigger event is detected (on TRGI signal).
Then, a comparison is performed as in PWM mode 1 and the channels
becomes inactive again at the next update.
• 4’b1001: Retrigerrable OPM mode 2 - In up-counting mode, the
channel is inactive until a trigger event is detected (on TRGI sig nal). Then, a comparison is performed as in PWM mode 2 and the
channels becomes inactive again at the next update.In down-counting
mode, the channel is active until a trigger event is detected (on TRGI
signal). Then, a comparison is performed as in PWM mode 1 and the
channels becomes active again at the next update.
• 4’b1010: Reserved,
• 4’b1011: Reserved,
• 4’b1100: Combined PWM mode 1 - OC1REF has the same behavior
as in PWM mode 1. OC1REFC is the logical OR between OC1REF
and OC2REF.
• 4’b1101: Combined PWM mode 2 - OC1REF has the same behavior
as in PWM mode 2. OC1REFC is the logical AND between OC1REF
and OC2REF.
• 4’b1110: Asymmetric PWM mode 1 - OC1REF has the same be havior as in PWM mode 1. OC1REFC outputs OC1REF when the
counter is counting up, OC2REF when it is counting down.
• 4’b1111: Asymmetric PWM mode 2 - OC1REF has the same be havior as in PWM mode 2. OC1REFC outputs OC1REF when the
counter is counting up, OC2REF when it is counting down.

Table 7.9 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
OC2CE 15 RW 0 Output Compare 2 clear enable
• 1’b0:OC1Ref is not affected by the ETRF input
• 1’b1:OC1Ref is cleared as soon as a High level is detected on ETRF
input
OC1FE 16 RW 1 Output Compare 1 fast enable
This bit is used to accelerate the effect of an event on the trigger in input on
the CC output.
• 0: CC1 behaves normally depending on counter and CCR1 values
even when the trigger is ON. The minimum delay to activate CC1
output when an edge occurs on the trigger input is 5 clock cycles.
• 1: An active edge on the trigger input acts like a compare match on
CC1 output. Then, OC is set to the compare level independently from
the result of the comparison. Delay to sample the trigger input and to
activate CC1 output is reduced to 3 clock cycles. OCFE acts only if
the channel is configured in PWM1 or PWM2 mode.
Reserved 17 / 0x0 /
IC1PSC 19:18 RW 0 Input capture 1 prescaler
This bit-field defines the ratio of the prescaler acting on CC1 input (IC1).
The prescaler is reset as soon as CC1E=’0’ (TIMx_CCER register).
• 2’b00: no prescaler, capture is done each time an edge is detected on
the capture input
• 2’b01: capture is done once every 2 events
• 2’b10: capture is done once every 4 events
• 2’b11: capture is done once every 8 events
IC1F 23:20 RW 0 Input capture 1 filter
This bit-field defines the frequency used to sample TI1 input and the length
of the digital filter applied to TI1. The digital filter is made of an event
counter in which N consecutive events are needed to validate a transition
on the output:
• 4’b0000: No filter, sampling is done at fDTS
• 4’b0001: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=2
• 4’b0010: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=4
• 4’b0011: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=8
• 4’b0100: fSAMPLING=fDTS/2, N=6
• 4’b0101: fSAMPLING=fDTS/2, N=8
• 4’b0110: fSAMPLING=fDTS/4, N=6
• 4’b0111: fSAMPLING=fDTS/4, N=8
• 4’b1000: fSAMPLING=fDTS/8, N=6
• 4’b1001: fSAMPLING=fDTS/8, N=8
• 4’b1010: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=5
• 4’b1011: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=6
• 4’b1100: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=8
• 4’b1101: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=5
• 4’b1110: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=6
• 4’b1111: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=8
OC2FE 24 RW 1 Output Compare 2 fast enable
Refer to OC1FE description
Reserved 25 / 0x0 /

7.5.1.8 TIM CAPTURE/COMPARE MODE REGISTER 2 OUTPUT COMPARE MODE
The same register can be used for input capture mode (this section) or for output compare mode (next section). The
direction of a channel is defined by configuring the corresponding CCxS bits. All the other bits of this register have a
different function for input capture and for output compare modes. It is possible to combine both modes independently
(e.g. channel 1 in input capture mode and channel 2 in output compare mode).
Address offset: 0x1C.

7.5.1.9 TIM CAPTURE/COMPARE ENABLE REGISTER
Address offset: 0x20.
Table 7.11: Register Format of TIM_CCER
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
CC1E 0 RW 0 Capture/Compare 1 output enable
CC1 channel configured as output:
• 1’b0: Off - OC1 is not active. OC1 level is then function of MOE,
OSSI, OSSR, OIS1, OIS1N and CC1NE bits.
• 1’b1: On - OC1 signal is output on the corresponding output pin
depending on MOE, OSSI, OSSR, OIS1, OIS1N and CC1NE bits.
CC1 channel configured as input: This bit determines if a capture of the
counter value can actually be done into the input capture/compare register
1 (TIMx_CCR1) or not.
0: Capture disabled.
1: Capture enabled.
On channels having a complementary output, this bit is preloaded. If the
CCPC bit is set in the TIMx_CR2 register then the CC1E active bit takes
the new value from the preloaded bit only when a Commutation event is
generated.
continues on next page
7.5. Registers 87
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.11 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
CC1P 1 RW 0 Capture/Compare 1 output polarity
CC1 channel configured as output:
• 1’b0: OC1 active high
• 1’b1: OC1 active low
CC1 channel configured as input: CC1NP/CC1P bits select the active po larity of TI1FP1 and TI2FP1 for trigger or capture operations.
00: non-inverted/rising edge. The circuit is sensitive to TIxFP1 rising
edge (capture or trigger operations in reset, external clock or trigger mode),
TIxFP1 is not inverted (trigger operation in gated mode or encoder mode).
01: inverted/falling edge. The circuit is sensitive to TIxFP1 rising edge
(capture or trigger operations in reset, external clock or trigger mode),
TIxFP1 is inverted (trigger operation in gated mode or encoder mode).
10: reserved, do not use this configuration.
11: non-inverted/both edges/ The circuit is sensitive to both TIxFP1 rising
and falling edges (capture or trigger operations in reset, external clock or
trigger mode), TIxFP1 is not inverted (trigger operation in gated mode).
This configuration must not be used in encoder mode.
Note: This bit is not writable as soon as LOCK level 2 or 3 has been pro grammed (LOCK bits in TIMx_BDTR register).
On channels having a complementary output, this bit is preloaded. If the
CCPC bit is set in the TIMx_CR2 register then the CC1P active bit takes
the new value from the preloaded bit only when a Commutation event is
generated.
Note: This bit should not keep changing when CCX channel configured as
input. for example, input is always inactive, but the CCXP or CCXNP is
changed from 0 to 1, the implementation will mistakenly realize that there
is a rising edge
CC1NE 2 RW 0 Capture/Compare 1 complementary output enable
• 1’b0: Off - OC1N is not active. OC1N level is then function of MOE,
OSSI, OSSR, OIS1, OIS1N and CC1E bits.
• 1’b1: On - OC1N signal is output on the corresponding output pin
depending on MOE, OSSI, OSSR, OIS1, OIS1N and CC1E bits.
On channels having a complementary output, this bit is preloaded. If the
CCPC bit is set in the TIMx_CR2 register then the CC1NE active bit takes
the new value from the preloaded bit only when a Commutation event is
generated.
CC1NP 3 RW 0 Capture/Compare 1 complementary output polarity
CC1 channel configured as output:
• 1’b0: OC1N active high.
• 1’b1: OC1N active low.
CC1 channel configured as input:
This bit is used in conjunction with CC1P to define the polarity of TI1FP1
and TI2FP1. Refer to CC1P description.
Note: This bit is not writable as soon as LOCK level 2 or 3 has been pro grammed (LOCK bits in TIMx_BDTR register) and CC1S=’00’ (channel
configured as output).
On channels having a complementary output, this bit is preloaded. If the
CCPC bit is set in the TIMx_CR2 register then the CC1NP active bit takes
the new value from the preloaded bit only when a Commutation event is
generated.

Address offset: 0x28.
Table 7.13: Register Format of TIM_PSC
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
PSC 31:0 RW 0 Prescaler value
The counter clock frequency (CK_CNT) is equal to fCK_PSC / (PSC[31:0]
+ 1).
PSC contains the value to be loaded in the active prescaler register at each
update event (including when the counter is cleared through UG bit of
TIMx_EGR register or through trigger controller when configured in ‘reset
mode’).

Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value

7.5.1.13 TIM REPETITION COUNTER REGISTER
Address offset: 0x30.
7.5. Registers 93
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.15: Register Format of TIM_RCR
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
REP 7:0 RW 0 Repetition counter value
These bits allow the user to set-up the update rate of the compare
registers (i.e. periodic transfers from preload to active registers) when
preload registers are enable, as well as the update interrupt generation
rate, if this interrupt is enable.
Each time the REP_CNT related downcounter reaches zero, an up date event is generated and it restarts counting from REP value. As
REP_CNT is reloaded with REP value only at the repetition update
event U_RC, any write to the TIMx_RCR register is not taken in ac count until the next repetition update event.
It means in PWM mode (REP+1) corresponds to:
• the number of PWM periods in edge-aligned mode
• the number of half PWM period in center-aligned mode.
GC4C 10:8 RW 0 GC4C[0]:group channle 4 and channel 1
0: No effect of OC4REF on OC1REF
1: OC1REF is logical AND of OC1REF and OC4REF
GC4C[1]:group channel 4 and channel 2.
0: No effect of OC4REF on OC2REF
1: OC2REF is logical AND of OC2REF and OC4REF
GC4C[2]:group channel 4 and channel 3.
0: No effect of OC4REF on OC3REF
1: OC3REF is logical AND of OC2REF and OC4REF
These bit can either have immediate effect or be preloaded and taken
into account after an update event (if preload feature is selected)
Note: they are also possible to apply this distortion on combined
PWM signals.
IC_PSC_REP_EN 14:11 RW 0 IC_PSC_REP_EN[0] : ic1psc_rep enable.
0 : IC1PSC use the ic1psc in TIM_CCMR1 .
1: : IC1PSC use the ic1psc_rep in TIM_IC_PSC_REP.
IC_PSC_REP_EN[1]: ic2psc_rep enable.
IC_PSC_REP_EN[2]: ic3psc_rep enable.
IC_PSC_REP_EN[3]: ic4psc_rep enable.
Reserved 31:15 / 0x0 /
7.5.1.14 TIM CAPTURE/COMPARE REGISTER 1
Address offset: 0x34.
7.5. Registers 94
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.16: Register Format of TIM_CCR1
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
CCR1 31:0 RW 0 Capture/Compare 1 value
If channel CC1 is configured as output: CCR1 is the value to be loaded in
the actual capture/compare 1 register (preload value).
It is loaded permanently if the preload feature is not selected in the
TIMx_CCMR1 register (bit OC1PE). Else the preload value is copied in
the active capture/compare 1 register when an update event occurs.
The active capture/compare register contains the value to be compared to
the counter TIMx_CNT and signaled on OC1 output.
If channel CC1 is configured as input: CR1 is the counter value transferred
by the last input capture 1 event (IC1). The TIMx_CCR1 register is read only and cannot be programmed.
7.5.1.15 TIM CAPTURE/COMPARE REGISTER 2

Re set
Value
Description

the actual capture/compare 2 register (preload value).
It is loaded permanently if the preload feature is not selected in the
TIMx_CCMR1 register (bit OC2PE). Else the preload value is copied in
the active capture/compare 2 register when an update event occurs.
The active capture/compare register contains the value to be compared to
the counter TIMx_CNT and signaled on OC2 output.
If channel CC2 is configured as input: CCR2 is the counter value transferred
by the last input capture 2 event (IC2). The TIMx_CCR2 register is read only and cannot be programmed.

7.5.1.18 TIM BREAK AND DEAD-TIME REGISTER
Address offset: 0x44.
7.5. Registers 96
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.20: Register Format of TIM_BDTR
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
DTG 7:0 RW 0 Dead-time generator setup
This bit-field defines the duration of the dead-time inserted between the
complementary outputs. DT correspond to this duration.
DTG[7:5]=0xx => DT=DTG[7:0]x tdtg with tdtg=tDTS.
DTG[7:5]=10x => DT=(64+DTG[5:0])xtdtg with Tdtg=2xtDTS.
DTG[7:5]=110 => DT=(32+DTG[4:0])xtdtg with Tdtg=8xtDTS.
DTG[7:5]=111 => DT=(32+DTG[4:0])xtdtg with Tdtg=16xtDTS.
Example if TDTS=125ns (8MHz), dead-time possible values are:
0 to 15875 ns by 125 ns steps,
16 us to 31750 ns by 250 ns steps,
32 us to 63us by 1 us steps,
64 us to 126 us by 2 us steps
LOCK 9:8 RW 0 Lock configuration
These bits offer a write protection against software errors.
• 2’b00: LOCK OFF - No bit is write protected.
• 2’b01: LOCK Level 1 = DTG bits in TIMx_BDTR register, OISx
and OISxN bits in TIMx_CR2 register and BKE/BKP/AOE bits in
TIMx_BDTR register can no longer be written.
• 2’b10: LOCK Level 2 = LOCK Level 1 + CC Polarity bits
(CCxP/CCxNP bits in TIMx_CCER register, as long as the related
channel is configured in output through the CCxS bits) as well as
OSSR and OSSI bits can no longer be written.
• 2’b11: LOCK Level 3 = LOCK Level 2 + CC Control bits (OCxM
and OCxPE bits in TIMx_CCMRx registers, as long as the related
channel is configured in output through the CCxS bits) can no longer
be written.
OSSI 10 RW 0 Off-state selection for Idle mode
This bit is used when MOE=0 due to a break event or by a software write,
on channels configured as outputs.
• 1’b0: When inactive, OC/OCN outputs are disabled (the timer re leases the output control which is taken over by the GPIO logic and
which imposes a Hi-Z state).
• 1’b1: When inactive, OC/OCN outputs are first forced with their in active level then forced to their idle level after the deadtime. The
timer maintains its control over the output.
OSSR 11 RW 0 Off-state selection for Run mode
This bit is used when MOE=1 on channels. OSSR is not implemented if
complementary output is implemented in the timer.
• 1’b0: OC/OCN outputs are disabled as soon as CCxE=0 or
CCxNE=0 (the timer releases the output control which is taken over
by the GPIO logic, which forces a Hi-Z state).
• 1’b1: OC/OCN outputs are enabled with their inactive level as soon
as CCxE=0 or CCxNE=0 (the output is still controlled by the timer).
BKE 12 RW 0 Break enable
This bit enables the complete break protection (including all sources con nected to bk_acth and BKIN sources).
• 1’b0: Break function disabled
• 1’b1: Break function enabled
continues on next page
7.5. Registers 97
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.20 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
BKP 13 RW 0 Break polarity
• 1’b0: Break input BRK is active low
• 1’b1: Break input BRK is active high
AOE 14 RW 0 Automatic output enable
• 1’b0: MOE can be set only by software
• 1’b1: MOE can be set by software or automatically at the next update
event (if none of the break inputs BRK and BRK2 is active)
MOE 15 RW 0 Main output enable
This bit is cleared asynchronously by hardware as soon as one of the break
inputs is active (BRK or BRK2). It is set by software or automatically de pending on the AOE bit. It is acting only on the channels which are config ured in output.
• 1’b0: In response to a break 2 event. OC and OCN outputs are dis abled
In response to a break event or if MOE is written to 0: OC and OCN outputs
are disabled or forced to idle state depending on the OSSI bit.
• 1’b1: OC and OCN outputs are enabled if their respective enable bits
are set (CCxE, CCxNE in TIMx_CCER register).
BKF 19:16 RW 0 Break filter
This bit-field then defines the frequency used to sample BRK signal and
the length of the digital filter applied to BRK. The digital filter is made of
an event counter in which N consecutive events are needed to validate a
transition on the output:
• 4’b0000: No filter, BRK acts asynchronously
• 4’b0001: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=2
• 4’b0010: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=4
• 4’b0011: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=8
• 4’b0100: fSAMPLING=fDTS/2, N=6
• 4’b0101: fSAMPLING=fDTS/2, N=8
• 4’b0110: fSAMPLING=fDTS/4, N=6
• 4’b0111: fSAMPLING=fDTS/4, N=8
• 4’b1000: fSAMPLING=fDTS/8, N=6
• 4’b1001: fSAMPLING=fDTS/8, N=8
• 4’b1010: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=5
• 4’b1011: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=6
• 4’b1100: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=8
• 4’b1101: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=5
• 4’b1110: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=6
• 4’b1111: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=8
Note: This bit cannot be modified when LOCK level 1 has been pro grammed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register).
continues on next page
7.5. Registers 98
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.20 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
BK2F 23:20 RW 0 Break 2 filter
This bit-field then defines the frequency used to sample BRK2
signal and the length of the digital filter applied to BRK2. The
digital filter is made of an event counter in which N consecutive
events are needed to validate a transition on the output:
• 4’b0000: No filter, BRK2 acts asynchronously
• 4’b0001: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=2
• 4’b0010: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=4
• 4’b0011: fSAMPLING=fCK_INT, N=8
• 4’b0100: fSAMPLING=fDTS/2, N=6
• 4’b0101: fSAMPLING=fDTS/2, N=8
• 4’b0110: fSAMPLING=fDTS/4, N=6
• 4’b0111: fSAMPLING=fDTS/4, N=8
• 4’b1000: fSAMPLING=fDTS/8, N=6
• 4’b1001: fSAMPLING=fDTS/8, N=8
• 4’b1010: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=5
• 4’b1011: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=6
• 4’b1100: fSAMPLING=fDTS/16, N=8
• 4’b1101: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=5
• 4’b1110: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=6
• 4’b1111: fSAMPLING=fDTS/32, N=8
Note: This bit cannot be modified when LOCK level 1 has been pro grammed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register).
BK2E 24 RW 0 Break 2 enable
This bit enables the complete break 2 protection (including all sources con nected to bk_acth and BKIN sources).
• 1’b0: Break2 function disabled
• 1’b1: Break2 function enabled
Note: The BKIN2 must only be used with OSSR=OSSI=1.
Note: This bit cannot be modified when LOCK level 1 has been pro grammed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register).
BK2P 25 RW 0 Break 2 polarity
• 1’b0: Break input BRK2 is active low
• 1’b1: Break input BRK2 is active high
Note: This bit cannot be modified when LOCK level 1 has been pro grammed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register).
Reserved 31:26 / 0x0 /
7.5.1.19 TIM DMA CONTROL REGISTER
Address offset: 0x48.
7.5. Registers 99
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.21: Register Format of TIM_DCR
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
DBA 4:0 RW 0 DMA base address
This 5-bits vector defines the base-address for DMA transfers (when
read/write access are done through the TIMx_DMAR address). DBA is
defined as an offset starting from the address of the TIMx_CR1 register.
Example:
• 5’b00000: TIMx_CR1
• 5’b00001: TIMx_CR2
• 5’b00010: TIMx_SMCR
Reserved 7:5 / 0x0 /
DBL 12:8 RW 0 DMA burst length
This 5-bit vector defines the length of DMA transfers (the timer recognizes
a burst transfer when a read or a write access is done to the TIMx_DMAR
address), i.e. the number of transfers. Transfers can be in half-words or in
bytes (see example below).
• 5’b00000: 1 transfer
• 5’b00001: 2 transfers
• 5’b00010: 3 transfers
Example: Let us consider the following transfer: DBL = 7 bytes & DBA =
TIMx_CR1.
• If DBL = 7 bytes and DBA = TIMx_CR1 represents the address of
the byte to be transferred, the address of the transfer should be given
by the following equation:
(TIMx_CR1 address) + DBA + (DMA index), where DMA index = DBL
In this example, 7 bytes are added to (TIMx_CR1 address) + DBA, which
gives us the address from/to which the data will be copied. In this case,
the transfer is done to 7 registers starting from the following address:
(TIMx_CR1 address) + DBA
According to the configuration of the DMA Data Size, several cases may
occur:
• If the DMA Data Size is configured in half-words, 16-bit data will be
transferred to each of the 7 registers.
• If the DMA Data Size is configured in bytes, the data will also be
transferred to 7 registers: the first register will contain the first MSB
byte, the second register, the first LSB byte and so on. So with the
transfer Timer, one also has to specify the size of data transferred by
DMA.
Reserved 15:13 / 0x0 /
MAP_IDX 20:16 RO 0 The index of DMAR mapping register
Example:
• 5’b00000: TIMx_CR1
• 5’b00001: TIMx_CR2
• 5’b00010: TIMx_SMCR
Reserved 31:21 / 0x0 /
7.5.1.20 TIM DMA ADDRESS FOR FULL TRANSFER
Address offset: 0x4C.
7.5. Registers 100
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 7.22: Register Format of TIM_DMAR
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
DMAB 31:0 RW 0 DMA register for burst accesses
A read or write operation to the DMAR register accesses the register lo cated at the address (TIMx_CR1 address) + (DBA + DMA index) x 4 where
TIMx_CR1 address is the address of the control register 1, DBA is the DMA
base address configured in TIMx_DCR register, DMA index is automati cally controlled by the DMA transfer, and ranges from 0 to DBL (DBL
configured in TIMx_DCR).
note: this register maps to other register which include from TIM_CR1 to
TIM_DBTR.mapping rules are related to TIM_DCR.
Note
note: this register maps to other register which include from TIM_CR1 to TIM_DBTR.mapping rules are related to
TIM_DCR.

Value

note: this bit generated according to the configuration, does
not affect the function.

note: this bit generated according to the configuration, does not affect the function.

Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
7.5.1.23 ALTERNATE FUNCTION OPTION REGISTER 1
Address offset: 0x58.
Table 7.25: Register Format of TIM_AF1
Field Bit Ac cess
Re set
Value
Description
BKINE 0 RW 0 BRK BKIN input enable
This bit enables the BKIN alternate function input for the timer’s BRK
input.BKIN input is ‘ORed’ with the other BRK sources
• 1’b0: BKIN input disabled
• 1’b1: BKIN input enabled
Note: This bit can not be modified as long as LOCK level 1 has been
programmed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register)
BKCMP1E 1 RW 0 BRK COMP1 enable
This bit enables the COMP1 for the timer’s BRK input. COMP1 output
is ‘ORed’ with the other BRK sources.
• 1’b0: COMP1 input disabled
• 1’b1: COMP1 input enabled
Note: This bit can not be modified as long as LOCK level 1 has been
programmed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register)
BKCMP2E 2 RW 0 BRK COMP2 enable
This bit enables the COMP2 for the timer’s BRK input. COMP2 output
is ‘ORed’ with the other BRK sources.
• 1’b0: COMP2 input disabled
• 1’b1: COMP2 input enabled
Note: This bit can not be modified as long as LOCK level 1 has been
programmed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register)
Reserved 7:3 / 0x0 /
BKDF1BK0E 8 RW 0 BRK dfsdm1_break[0] enable
This bit enables the dfsdm1_break[0] for the timer’s BRK input.
dfsdm1_break[0] output is ‘ORed’ with the other BRK sources.
• 1’b0: dfsdm1_break[0] input disabled
• 1’b1: dfsdm1_break[0] input enabled
Note: This bit can not be modified as long as LOCK level 1 has been
programmed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register)
BKINP 9 RW 0 BRK BKIN input polarity
This bit selects the BKIN alternate function input sensitivity. It must be
programmed together with the BKP polarity bit
• 1’b0: BKIN input is active high
• 1’b1: BKIN input is active low
Note: This bit can not be modified as long as LOCK level 1 has been
programmed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register)
BKCMP1P 10 RW 0 BRK COMP1 input polarity
This bit selects the COMP1 input sensitivity. It must be programmed
together with the BKP polarity bit
• 1’b0: COMP1 input is active high
• 1’b1: COMP1 input is active low
Note: This bit can not be modified as long as LOCK level 1 has been
programmed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register)
BKCMP2P 11 RW 0 BRK COMP2 input polarity
This bit selects the COMP2 input sensitivity. It must be programmed
together with the BKP polarity bit
• 1’b0: COMP2 input is active high
• 1’b1: COMP2 input is active low
Note: This bit can not be modified as long as LOCK level 1 has been
programmed(LOCK bits in TIM_BDTR register)

BASIC_TIMER(0/1/2/3/4/5/6/7)
This section applies to instantiation of u_basic_timer0, u_basic_timer1, u_basic_timer2, u_basic_timer3, u_basic_timer4,
u_basic_timer5, u_basic_timer6, u_basic_timer7
8.1 Introduction
The Nuclei BASIC TIMER consist of a 32-bit auto-reload counter driven by a programmable prescaler.
It is completely independent and may be used as generic timers for time-base generation.
8.2 Main Features
The main features of BASIC TIMER is expressed as below:
• 32-bit auto-reload upcounter.
• 16-bit programmable prescaler used to divide the counter clock frequency.
• Interrupt/DMA request generation by two update event sources:
– Counter overflow,
– Event generated by software.
The block diagram of the BASIC TIMER is shown in WWDG block diagram. (page 266).
Fig. 8.1: BASIC TIMER block diagram
110
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
8.3 Functional Description
8.3.1 Time-base Unit
The main block of the programmable timer is a 32-bit upcounter with its related auto-reload register. The counter clock
can be divided by a prescaler.
The counter, the auto-reload register and the prescaler register can be written or read by software. This is true even when
the counter is running.
The time-base unit includes:
• Counter Register (TIME_CNT),
• Prescaler Register (TIME_PSC),
• Auto-Reload Register (TIME_ARR).
The auto-reload register is preloaded. The preload register is accessed each time an attempt is made to write or read the
auto-reload register. The contents of the preload register are transferred into the shadow register permanently or at each
update event UEV, depending on the AUTO_BUFFER_EN bit in the TIME_CR1 register. The update event is sent when
the counter reaches the overflow value and if the UPD_EVT_DIS bit equals 0 in the TIME_CR1 register. It can also be
generated by software. The generation of the update event is described in detail for each configuration.
The counter is clocked by the prescaler output CK_CNT, which is enabled only when the counter enable bit (CEN) in the
TIME_CR1 register is set.
8.3.2 Prescaler Description
The prescaler can divide the counter clock frequency by any factor between 1 and 65536. It is based on a 16-bit counter
controlled through a 16-bit register (in the TIME_PSC register). It can be changed on the fly as the TIME_PSC control
register is buffered. The new prescaler ratio is taken into account at the next update event.
Counter timing diagram with prescaler division is 2 (page 111) gives an examples of the counter behavior when the
prescaler ratio is changed on the fly.
0 1 2
2
2
0 1 2 0 1 2 0 1 2 0 1 2 0 1 2 0 1 2 0
Viewer does not support full SVG 1.1
Fig. 8.2: Counter timing diagram with prescaler division is 2
8.3.3 Counting Mode
The counter counts from 0 to the auto-reload value (contents of the TIME_ARR register), then restarts from 0 and generates
a counter overflow event.
An update event can be generate at each counter overflow or by setting the UPD_EVT_SW bit in the TIME_EGR register
(by software).
The UEV event can be disabled by software by setting the UPD_EVT_DIS bit in the TIME_CR1 register. This avoids
updating the shadow registers while writing new values into the preload registers. In this way, no update event occurs until
the UPD_EVT_DIS bit has been written to 0, however, the counter and the prescaler counter both restart from 0 (but the
prescale rate does not change). In addition, if the UPD_EVT_SEL (update request selection) bit in the TIME_CR1 register
is set, setting the UG bit generates an update event UEV, but the EVT_STATUS bit is not set (so no interrupt or DMA
request is sent).
8.3. Functional Description 111
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
When an update event occurs, all the registers are updated and the update flag (EVT_STATUS bit in the TIME_SR register)
is set (depending on the UPD_EVT_SEL bit):
• The buffer of the prescaler is reloaded with the preload value (contents of the TIME_PSC register)
• The auto-reload shadow register is updated with the preload value (TIME_ARR)
The following figures show some examples of the counter behavior for different clock frequencies when TIME_ARR =
0x3.
0 1 2
2
0 1 2 0 1 2 0 1 2 0 1 2 0 1 2 0 1 2
2
0 32 1 0 1 2
0
Viewer does not support full SVG 1.1
Fig. 8.3: Counter timing diagram, internal clock divided by 2
8.3.4 Clock Source
The counter clock is provided by the Internal clock (icb_clk) source.
The CEN (in the TIME_CR1 register) and UPD_EVT_SW bits (in the TIME_EGR register) are actual control bits and
can be changed only by software (except for that UPD_EVT_SW remains cleared automatically). As soon as the CEN bit
is written to 1, the prescaler is clocked by the internal clock.
8.3.5 Debug Mode
When the sub-systerm enters the debug mode, the BASIC TIMER counter either continues to work normally or stops,
depending on the the status of interface (dbg_stop_i).

I2C(0/1)
This section applies to instantiation of u_i2c0, u_i2c1
9.1 Introduction
I2C (Inter-Integrated Circuit) bus Interface serves as an interface between the microcontroller and the serial I2C bus. It
provides multimaster capability, and controls all I2C bus-specific sequencing, protocol, arbitration and timing. It supports
the standard mode (Sm, up to 100 kHz), Fast mode (Fm, up to 400 kHz), Fast mode plus mode (Fm+, up to 1MHz) and
High-speed mode (Hs-mode, up to 3.4MHz).
Depending on specific device implementation DMA capability can be available for reduced CPU overload.
9.2 Main Features
The main features of I2C are the following:
• Parallel-bus/I2C protocol converter.
• Master and Slave capability: the same interface can act as Master or Slave.
• Two internal FIFOs for transmit and receive data:
– The width of FIFOs is 32 bit,
– The depth of FIFOs is 8.
• I2C Master features:
– Clock generation,
– Start and Stop generation.
• I2C Slave features:
– Programmable I2C address detection,
– Stop bit detection.
– Programmable two slave address registers and two mask registers.
• Generation and detection of 7-bit/10-bit addressing.
• Support up to two slave address responses(both with configurable mask fields)
• Support general call software reset.
• Support different communication speed:
– Standard Speed (up to 100 kHz),
– Fast Speed (up to 400 kHz),
117
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
– Fast Speed Plus (up to 1 MHz).
– High Speed (up to 3.4 MHz).
• Status flags:
– Transmitter/Receiver mode flag,
– End-of-Byte transmission flag,
– I2C busy flag.
• Clock Stretching Capability:
– TX data is not ready,
– RX data is not be fetched by CPU or DMA-core.
• Support DMA access with DMA controller for high speed data communication.
• Programmable SCL and SDA timing characters of I2C-Specification
– Hold time for (repeated) START condition (tHD;STA),
– Set-up time for Repeated START condition (tSU;STA),
– Data set-up time (tSU;DAT),
– Data hold time (tHD;DAT),
– High period of the SCL clock (tHIGH),
– Low period of the SCL clock (tLOW),
– Set-up time for STOP condition (tSU;STO),
– Bus time between a STOP and START condition (tBUF).
• Programmable low levels and high levels of SCL period.
• Programmable digital noise filters.
• SMBus specification rev 2.0 compatibility
– Hardware PEC (Packet Error Checking) generation and verification with ACK control
– Support Address resolution protocol (ARP)
– Support Host and slave device
– Support SMBus Alert
– Support Timeouts and IDLE condition detection
• PMBus rev 1.1 standard compatibility
This manual describes the full set of features implemented in I2C peripheral.I2C implement the full set of features as
shown in I2C Implementation (page 118) .
Table 9.1: I2C Implementation
I2C features I2C
Master-mode/Slave-mode Supported
7-bit addressing mode Supported
10-bit addressing mode Supported
Standard-mode(up to 100KHz) Supported
Fast-mode(up to 400KHz) Supported
Fast-mode Plus(up to 1MHz) Supported
High-speed mode(up to 3.4MHz) Supported
General call software reset Supported
Clock stretching capability Supported
DMA access with DMA-controller Supported
SMBus Supported
9.2. Main Features 118
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
9.3 Functional Description
In addition to receiving and transmitting data, this interface converts it from serial to parallel format and vice versa. The
interrupts are enabled or disabled by software. The interface is connected to the I2C bus by a data pin (SDA) and by a
clock pin (SCL). It can be connected with a standard (up to 100 kHz), fast (up to 400 kHz), fast plus (up to 1 MHz) or high
speed mode (up to 3.4 MHz) I2C bus.
9.3.1 Mode Selection
The interface can operate in one of the four following modes:
• Master transmitter
• Master receiver
• Slave transmitter
• Slave receiver
By default, it operates in master mode, Which can generate a START or STOP condition to I2C slave peripheral and
allow multimaster capability. The interface can switch from master to slave by setting the SETUP[11] register. When the
controller is working in the slave mode, allowing multislave capability.
9.3.2 Communication Flow
In Master mode, the I2C interface initiates a data transfer and generates the clock signal. A serial data transfer always
begins with a start condition and ends with a stop condition. Both start and stop conditions are generated in master mode
by software.
In Slave mode, the interface is capable of recognizing its own dual addresses (7 or 10-bit), and the General Call address.
Data and addresses are transferred as 8-bit bytes, MSB first. The first byte(s) following the start condition contain the
address (one in 7-bit mode, two in 10-bit mode). The address is always transmitted in Master mode.
A 9th clock pulse follows the 8 clock cycles of a byte transfer, during which the receiver must send an acknowledge bit to
the transmitter. Refer to I2C Bus Protocol (page 119).
Fig. 9.1: I2C Bus Protocol
Acknowledge may be enabled or disabled by software. The I2C interface addresses (dual addressing 7-bit/ 10-bit and/or
general call address) can be selected by software.
The block diagram of the I2C interface is shown in I2C Block Diagram (page 120).
9.3. Functional Description 119
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 9.2: I2C Block Diagram
9.3.3 I2C Initialization
Enabling and disabling the peripheral:
The I2C peripheral clock must be configured and enabled in the clock controller.
The digital filter must be configured before enabling the I2C via setting CFG_DNF_NUM in SETUP register.
Then the I2C can be enabled by setting the ENABLE bit in the SETUP register.
Note: The I2C peripheral can work with the configure register defaults. User don’t need to configure timings anymore.
Caution: Changing the timing configuration is not allowed when the I2C transmission is working.
Fig. 9.3: I2C Initialization Flowchart
9.3. Functional Description 120
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
9.3.4 Software Reset
A software reset can be performed by setting the SOFT_RESET bit in the SETUP register. In that case I2C lines SCL and
SDA are released. Internal states machines are reset and communication control bits, as well as status bits come back to
their reset value. The configuration registers are not impacted.
When soft-ware set the SETUP[7], then a soft-reset pulse will be generated and reset the I2C-Cotroller right now.
9.3.5 Master SCL and SDA Timing
Fig. 9.4: I2C Protocol Timing Generate
The I2C-bus timing characteristics is given in I2C Protocol Timing Generate (page 121). The timings must be configured
in order to guarantee a correct data hold and setup time, used in master and slave modes. This is done by programming
those timing registers:
• I2C_TIMING.PRESC[31:0].
• CFG_START_SETUP_TIME[31:0], CFG_START_HOLD_TIME[31:0].
• CFG_SDA_SETUP_TIME[31:0], CFG_SDA_HOLD_TIME[31:0].
• CFG_SCL_HIGH_PERIOD[31:0], CFG_SCL_LOW_PERIOD[31:0].
• CFG_STOP_SETUP_TIME[31:0], CFG_BUS_FREE_TIME[31:0].
The minimum HIGH and LOW periods of the SCL clock specified in I2C-bus sepcification determine the maximum bit
transfer rates of 100 kbit/s for Standard-mode devices, 400 kbit/s for Fast-mode devices, and 1000 kbit/s for Fast-mode
Plus. Devices must be able to follow transfers at their own maximum bit rates, either by being able to transmit or receive
at that speed.
The timings must be configured in order to guarantee a correct data hold and setup time, used in master and slave mode.
Symbol Parameter Timing register
tSU;STA Set-up time for a START condition CFG_START_SETUP_TIME[31:0]
tHD;STA Hold time for a START condition CFG_START_HOLD_TIME[31:0]
tSU;DAT Data set-up time CFG_SDA_SETUP_TIME[31:0]
tHD;DAT Data hold time CFG_SDA_HOLD_TIME[31:0]
tSU;STA Set-up time for a STOP condition CFG_STOP_SETUP_TIME[31:0]
tBUF Bus free time between a STOP and START condition CFG_BUS_FREE_TIME[31:0]
Note: The unit of timing register is I2C_TIMING.PRESC[31:0], which is prescaler of icb_clk.
9.3.6 I2C Master Mode
In Master mode, the I2C interface initiates a data transfer and generates the clock signal. A serial data transfer always begins
with a START condition and ends with a STOP condition. Master mode is selected as soon as the START condition is
generated on the bus with a START bit.
The following is the main required sequence in master CPU mode.
• Program the timing registers in order to generate correct I2C-timing.
• Configure I2C-Controller worked in Master mode by enable ROLE_MODE bit in SETUP[11] register.
9.3. Functional Description 121
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• Program ENABLE bit in SETUP[1] register to enable I2C-Controller.
• Set the START bit in the SETUP[2] register to generate a START condition.
• Configure the TXDATA register.
• Program the WRITE or READ bit in SETUP[5:4] register to make master worked in transimter mode or receiver
mode.
• When BYTE_ON_GOING bit in STATUS[4] is low, which indicate the byte is transmit done.
Master
initialization
Initialization settings
Enable interrupts and/or DMA
End
Configure timing registers to genetate I2C timing
Configure ROLE_MODE bit in SETUP[11]
Set START bit in SETUP[2]
Program ENABLE bit in SETUP[1]
Fig. 9.5: Master Initialization Flowchart
9.3.7 START and STOP Condition
All transactions begin with a START (S) and are terminated by a STOP (P), Which is shown in START and STOP Conditions
(page 123). A HIGH to LOW transition on the SDA line while SCL is HIGH defines a START condition. A LOW to HIGH
transition on the SDA line while SCL is HIGH defines a STOP condition.
9.3. Functional Description 122
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 9.6: START and STOP Conditions
Setting the START bit causes the interface to generate a Start condition on SDA and SCL line.
After the last byte is written to the TXDATA register, the STOP bit is set by software to generate a STOP condition.
START and STOP conditions are always generated by the master. The bus is considered to be busy after the START
condition. The I2C-Controller is considered to be free again a certain time after the STOP condition.
The I2C-Controller stays busy if a repeated START (Sr) is generated instead of a STOP condition. In this respect, the
START (S) and repeated START (Sr) conditions are functionally identical.
Note: In master mode, setting the START bit causes the interface to generate a ReStart condition at the end of the current
byte transfer.
Once the start condition is sent, the slave address will be transimitted from TXDATA register.
9.3.8 Slave Address Transmission
Then the slave address is sent to the SDA line via the internal shift register.
In 7-bit addressing mode, one address byte is sent.
As soon as the address byte has sent, The byte-on-going bit(STATUS[4] register) is clear by hardware. Then the master
waits for a read of the ACK bit in STATUS[2] register which indicate the slave is send hand shake on SDA line.
The master can decide to enter Transmitter or Receiver mode depending on the LSB of the slave address sent.
In 7-bit addressing mode:
• To enter Transmitter mode, a master sends the slave address with LSB reset.
• To enter Receiver mode, a master sends the slave address with LSB set.
In 10-bit addressing mode:
• To enter Transmitter mode, a master sends the header (11110xx0) and then the slave address, (where xx denotes the
two most significant bits of the address).
• To enter Receiver mode, a master sends the header (11110xx0) and then the slave address. Then it should send a
repeated Start condition followed by the header (11110xx1), (where xx denotes the two most significant bits of the
address).
The READ or WRITE bit in SETUP[5:4] register indicates whether the master is in Receiver or Transmitter mode.
9.3. Functional Description 123
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
9.3.9 Master Transmitter
Fig. 9.7: Master Transmiter Mode
Following the address transmission, the master sends bytes from TXDATA register to the SDA line via the internal shift
register.
When the acknowledge pulse is received, the ACK bit in STATUS register is set by hardware.
If the BYTE_ON_GOING bit in STATUS[4] register is clear and a new data-byte is written in the TXDATA register, then
master sends next bytes from TXDATA register to the SDA line by set the WRITE bit in SETUP[4] register. Which is
shown in Master Transmiter Mode (page 124).
9.3.10 Master Receiver
Fig. 9.8: Master Receiver Mode
Following the address transmission, the I2C interface enters Master Receiver mode. In this mode the interface receives
bytes from the SDA line into the RXDATA register via the internal shift register. And send acknowledge pulse after each
byte.
When the acknowledge pulse(the ACK bit is low) is send, which means Master send hand shake and the Slave will send
next data to the SDA line.
If the BYTE_ON_GOING bit in STATUS[4] register is clear and a data-byte is fetched by a read RXDATA register, then
the Master can send next read operate to the SDA line by set the READ bit in SETUP[5] register. Which is shown in
Master Receiver Mode (page 124).
Closing the communication:
The master sends a NACK for the last byte received from the slave. After receiving this NACK, the slave releases the
control of the SCL and SDA lines. Then the master can send a Stop/Restart condition.
9.3.11 I2C Slave Mode
In order to work in slave mode, the user must enable at least one slave address. Two slave addresses are available in order
to program the slave own addresses I2C_SLAVE_ADDRESS1 and I2C_SLAVE_ADDRESS2 registers.
As soon as a START condition is detected, the address is received from the SDA line and sent to the shift reg ister. Then it is compared with the address of the SLAVE_ADDRESS1 register when SLAVE_ADDRESS1_EN
9.3. Functional Description 124
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
enabled, or SLAVE_ADDRESS2 register when SLAVE_ADDRESS2_EN enabled, or SLAVE_ADDRESS1 regis ter and SLAVE_ADDRESS2 register when both SALVE_ADDRESS1_EN and SLAVE_ADDRESS2_EN enabled,
or the General call address(0x0000_0000). The SLAVE_ADDRESS1 register and SLAVE_ADDRESS2 register
can be masked by SLAVE_ADDRESS1_MASK register and SLAVE_ADDRESS2_MASK register. As soon as the
each bit of SLAVE_ADDRESS1_MASK register and SLAVE_ADDRESS2_MASK register is not equal to 0, the
SALVE_ADDRESS1 register and SLAVE_ADDRESS2 register is valid.
Header or address not matched: the interface ignores it and waits for another Start condition.
Header matched (10-bit mode only): the interface generates an acknowledge pulse and waits for the 8-bit slave address.
Address matched: the interface generates in sequence:
1.The interface generates an acknowledge pulse
2.The software has to read the RXDATA register to check which slave address has been acknowledged.
In 10-bit mode, after receiving the address sequence the slave is always in Receiver mode. It will enter Transmitter mode
on receiving a repeated Start condition followed by the header sequence with matching address bits and the least significant
bit set (11110xx1).
Caution: In slave mode, the SCL period Tscl and I2C kernel clock period Ti2c_core_clk must respect the following
conditions: Tscl > 10 * Ti2c_core_clk.
9.3. Functional Description 125
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Configure I2C_SLAVE_ADDRESS
Configure I2C_10BIT_ADDR_EN
Master
initialization
Initialization settings
Enable interrupts and/or DMA
End
Configure timing registers to genetate I2C timing
Configure ROLE_MODE bit in SETUP[11]
Set START bit in SETUP[2]
Program ENABLE bit in SETUP[1]
Fig. 9.9: Slave Initialization Flowchart
9.3. Functional Description 126
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
9.3.12 Slave Transmitter
Fig. 9.10: Slave Transmiter Mode
Following the address reception and after clearing ADDR, the slave sends bytes from the TXDATA register to the SDA
line via the internal shift register. The slave stretches SCL low until TXDATA filled with the data to be sent. Which is
shown in Slave Transmiter Mode (page 127).
When the acknowledge pulse is received:
• The I2C_SLAVE_TXE bit of STATUS[11] register is set by hardware and an interrupt is generated if the INT_IE[3]
bit is set, which indicate software should fill a data-byte into TXDATA register.
If a data-byte is written in the TXDATA register, then slave sends next bytes from TXDATA register to the SDA line.
9.3.13 Slave Receiver
Fig. 9.11: Slave Receive Mode
Following the address reception and after clearing ADDR, the slave receives bytes from the SDA line into the RXDATA
register via the internal shift register. Which is shown in Slave Receive Mode (page 127).
After each byte the interface generates in sequence:
• An acknowledge pulse if the ACK bit is set.
• The I2C_SLAVE_RXF bit is set by hardware and an interrupt is generated if the INT_IE[3] bit is set.
If the data in the RXDATA register is not read before the end of the next data reception, the I2C_SLAVE_RXF bit is set
and the interface waits until software fetch the received data by a read from the RXDATA register, stretching SCL low.
9.3.14 Closing Slave Communication
After the last data byte is transferred a STOP condition is generated by the master.
When SDA remains HIGH during this ninth clock pulse (NACK, SETUP[6]=1), this is defined as the Not Acknowledge
signal.
9.3. Functional Description 127
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
The master can then generate either a STOP condition to abort the transfer, or a repeat START condtion to start a new
transter.
There are four conditions that lead to the generate of a NACK:
1. No receiver is present on the bus with the transmitted address so there is no device to respond with an acknowledge.
2. During the transfer, the receiver gets data or commands that it does not understand.
3. During the transfer, the receiver cannot receive any more data bytes.
4. A master-receiver must signal the end of the transfer to the slave transmitter.
When the master send STOP condition on the bus, The BUSY bit(STATUS[0]) is cleared, which indicate the SCL and
SDA line has entered idle.
9.3.15 General Call Address
The general call address is for addressing every device connected to the I2C-Bus at the same time. However, if a device
does not need any of the data supplied within the general call structure, it can ignore this address by not issuing an ac knowledgment. If a device does require data from a general call address, it acknowledges this address and behave as a
slave-receiver.
When the I2C-Controller is working in the slave mode, it can response to General-Call-Reset as a Soft-Reset to itself.
Fig. 9.12: General Call Address Format
When bit B is a ‘zero’, the second byte has the following definition:
• 0000_0110 (06h): General Call Reset
On receiving this 2-byte sequence, The I2C-Controller will generate a soft-reset. The generate-call-reset will reset the
main logic of I2C-Controller to the idle state and release the SCL and SDA line (pull-up). The I2C-Controller take to
ensure that is not pulling down the SDA or SCL line after applying the supply voltage, since these low levels would block
the bus.
9.3.16 Continuous Communication Using DMA
The I2C is capable of continuing communication using the DMA. The DMA requests for Rx buffer and Tx buffer are
generated independently.
Note: You should refer to sub-systerm specs for availability of the DMA controller. If DMA is not available in the sub systerm, you should use the I2C as explained in Section 6.3, which is CPU mode for transmitter or receiver.
Corresponding channel
needs to be enabled first
dma transfer request
dma controller ack after
transfer complete
pa_mem deassert
request after dma acked
dma controller deassert ack after
pa_mem request deassert
new dma transfer request
only after ack deassert
Fig. 9.13: I2C DMA Transfer Flow Diagram
9.3. Functional Description 128
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Transmission using DMA
DMA mode can be enabled for transmission by setting DMA_EN bit in the I2C_SETUP[0] register. Data is loaded from
a SRAM area configured using the DMA peripheral (refer to the DMA specification) to the I2C_TXFIFO whenever the
FIFO is empty, DMA-Core will access transmit-data through soc bus.
Only the data are transferred with DMA.
• In master mode: the initialization, the slave address, direction, number of bytes and START bit are programmed by
software (the transmitted slave address cannot be transferred with DMA). When all data are transferred using DMA,
the DMA must be initialized before setting the START bit.
• In slave mode: when all data are transferred using DMA, the DMA must be initialized before the address match
event, or in interrupt subroutine, before clearing ADDR.
Reception using DMA
DMA mode can be enabled for reception by setting the DMA EN bit in I2C_SETUP[0] register. Data is loaded from the
I2C_RXFIFO to a SRAM area configured using the DMA peripheral (refer to the DMA specification) whenever a data
byte is received, DMA-Core will access receive-data through soc bus.
• In master mode, the initialization, the slave address, direction, number of bytes and START bit are programmed by
software. When all data are transferred using DMA, the DMA must be initialized before setting the START bit.
• In slave mode, when all data are transferred using DMA, the DMA must be initialized before the address match
event, or in the interrupt subroutine, before clearing the ADDR flag.
9.3.17 SMBus features
The System Management Bus (SMBus) is a two-wire interface through which various devices can communicate with each
other and with the rest of the system. It is based on I2C principles of operation. SMBus provides a control bus for system
and power management related tasks.
The SMBus refers to three types of devices
• Slave device
• Master device
• Host device
The I2C can be configured as master or slave device, and also as a host.
SMBus command protocol
The device supports eleven command protocols. A device may use any or all of the eleven command protocols to com municate. The protocols are Quick Command, Send Byte, Receive Byte, Write Byte, Write Word, Read Byte, Read Word,
Process Call, Block Read, Block Write and Block Write-Block Read Process Call. These protocols should be implemented
by the user software.
Address resolution protocol (ARP)
SMBus slave address conflicts can be resolved by dynamically assigning a new unique address to each slave device. In
order to provide a mechanism to isolate each device for the purpose of address assignment each device must implement
a unique device identifier(UDID). This 128-bit number is implemented by software. This peripheral supports the Ad dress Resolution Protocol (ARP). The SMBus Device Default Address (0b1100 001) is enabled by setting SMDEN bit in
I2C_ADVANCED_SETUP register. The ARP commands should be implemented by the user software.
Arbitration is also performed in slave mode for ARP support.
Slave byte conrtol
The SMBus device allows ACK control in salve mode. The slave byte control mode be enabled by setting SBCEN bit in
I2C_ADVANCED_SETUP register.
Host Notify protocol
The SMBus device supports the Host Notify protocol by setting the SMHEN bit in the I2C_ADVANCED_SETUP register.
In this case the host acknowledges the SMBus Host Address (0b0001 000), and the SMBus device acts as a master and the
host as a slave.
9.3. Functional Description 129
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
SMBus alert
A slave-only device can signal the host through SMBALERT# that it wants to talk. The host processes the interrupt and
simultaneously accesses all SMBALERT# devices through the Alert Response Address (0b0001 100). Only the device(s)
which pulled SMBALERT# low will acknowledge the Alert Response Address.
When configured as a slave device(SMHEN=0), the SMBALERT# pin is pulled low by setting the ALERTEN bit in the
I2C_ADVANCED_SETUP register. The Alert Response Address is enabled at the same time.
When configured as a host (SMHEN=1), the ALERT_STUS flag is set in the I2C_ADVANCED_STATUS register when a
falling edge is detected on the SMBALERT# pin and ALERTEN=1. An interrupt is generated if the ERR_INE_IE bit is set
in the INT_IE register. When ALERTEN=0, the SMBALERT line is considered high even if the external SMBALERT#
pin is low.
Packet error checking (PEC)
Packet error checking is implemented by appending a Packet Error Code (PEC) at the end of each message transfer.
The packet error checking is calculated by using CRC-8 polynomial on all the message bytes (except ACK, NACK, START,
STOP nor Repeated START bits), and must be calculated in the order of the bits as received.
The SMBus device supports the PEC by setting the PECEN bit in the I2C_ADVANCED_SETUP register. The PECIRQ
bit in I2C_ADVANCED_SETUP register needs to be enabled when device needs to send or receive PEC.
The PECBYTE field in the I2C_ADVANCED_STATUS register is used to store the PEC results.
Time out
SMBus defines a minimum bus clock frequency FSMB of 10 KHz. Maximum clock frequency for SMBus is defined at
100 KHz.
SMBus specifies TLOW: SEXT as the cumulative clock low extend time for a slave device. SMBus specifies TLOW:
MEXT as the cumulative clock low extend time for a master device.
SMBus defines a clock low time-out, TTIMEOUT of 35 ms.
