IDU
This section applies to instantiation of u_idu
10.1 Introduction
The Nuclei IDU (Interrupt Distribution Unit) and the processor core interface are closely coupled, which enables low
latency interrupt processing and efficient processing of late arriving interrupts.
All interrupts including the interrupt, event, none-mask-interrupt(nmi), which all come from sub-system. And IDU can
provides Inter Core Interrupt(ICI) and Semaphores Mechanism.
Fig. 10.1: IDU Block Diagram
144
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Note
The signal followed by * indicates that the signal is avaliable depending on whether it is asynchronous with the IDU.
10.2 Main Features
The main features of IDU are the following:
• Support upto 32 Inter-Core-Interrupts (ICI) distribute. The ICI-INT in here is a level.
• If number of Core is smaller than 24, Support transmit Inter-Core-Interrupts (ICI) informations from source Core
to destination Core.
• Support upto 128 SoC-EVENTs(from SoC) distribute. The SoC-EVENT in here should be a pulse.
• Support upto 4096 SoC-IRQs(from SoC) distribute. The SoC-IRQ in here should be a level.
• Support upto 128 SoC-NMIs (No-Mask-Interrupt) distribute. The SoC-NMI in here should be a level.
• Support upto 32 Semaphores Registers Mechanism.
• Implementation of System Control Registers.
• Support Lock Mode Mechanism for the specific SoC-NMI, which can be used for CPU-Security Mode.
• Support the SoC-IRQs can be treat as Soc-Events Mechanism.
• Low-latency exception and interrupt handing.
10.3 Functional Description
10.3.1 External Interrupt Distribution
The IDU acts as an interrupt distributor in the sub-system, it supports:
• Broadcast all external interrupts to a specific core or all target cores.
• Let the specific core to claim the specific interrupt, which is responsed by that specifics core only and the other core
cannot calim this interrupt.
10.3.2 Interrupt Distribution Broadcast Mode
If user wants to use IDU to broadcast the external interrupt to some/all target cores, it just needs to turn on the related
interrupt INTn_INDICATOR register’s responding bits. The each INTn_INDICATOR register’s responding bits indicate
the each core ID number of all target cores.
ex: In multi-core mode, INTn_INDICATOR[i] indicates that the interrupt number N is enabled and sent to the core number
i.
10.3.3 Interrupt Distribution First Come First Claim Mode
If user wants to use IDU to broadcast the external interrupt to some/all target cores, then let the first coming core to claim
the interrupt, beside the INTn_INDICATOR register, it also needs INTn_MASK register to cooperate.
ex: In multi-core mode,if user wants INT1 responsed by CORE1 only,the register INT1_INDICATOR[1] and
INT1_MASK[1] should be write “1”.
The key points are the followings:
• Write the right value to INTn_Indicator register.
10.2. Main Features 145
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• When the external interrupt comes,it will be broadcast to all targeted cores that enable corresponding bit of
INTn_Indicator,in the interrupt N’s handler of all cores, firstly write its Core Id (not mhartid) to INTn_MASK,
then read the INTn_MASK value, if the value matches the core id, it means it claim the interrupt successfully, then
continues to handle the interrupt normally. If not, it means other core has claimed the interrupt, so quit the interrupt
handler.
• When a core claims the interrupt successfully and handle the interrupt, before executing mret, it should write the
reset value (all 1) to INTn_MASK.
Note
In single-core mode,all INTn_INDICATOR and INTn_MASK registers are enabled , therefore the interrupt will be
distributed.
10.3.4 Inter Core Interrupt
Inter Core Interrupt (ICI) means that one core can send interrupt to another core in a multi-core system. IDU implements
a good Inter Core Interrupt mechanism with two kind registers as follows:
• SEND_CORE_ID register: The Core ID which sends this Inter Core Interrupt
• RECEIVE_CORE_ID register: The Core ID which receives this Inter Core Interrupt
If Core n (Core ID=n) wants to send the ICI to Core m (Core ID =m), then the flow is simple:
• Core n writes the value n SEND_CORE_ID (ICI_SHADOW_REG[31:16]) and writes the value m to RE CEIVE_CORE_ID (ICI_SHADOW_REG[15:0]).
• Then the n COREm_INT_STATUS register is updated and IDU trigger ICI to Core m automatically.
• When Core m receives the ICI and enter the ICI Interrupt Service Routine (ISR), then it can query the
Corem_INI_STATUS register and will get that the current ICI is triggered by Core n. And the Core m also need to
write 1 to clear the bit of the source ICI sending CoreID before the ISR returns.
• What’s more, if core_num<=24 in your system, idu support transmit ICI interrupt information (8-bit)
by (ICI_SHADOW_REG[31:24]). The objective (Core m) can get this informations by read its own
COREm_INT_STATUS registers[31:24].
Note
In the ISR of ICI, it should clear the corresponding bit/bits of its own COREn_INT_STATUS. If the ISR has finished
the job of Core_n’s ICI, then clear Core n; if it has finished Core_n and Core_ms, then clear both the bits etc.
In single-core mode, the inter-core interrupt is to send and receive for itself. When soc_irq_num<=32, the bit range of this
register is soc_irq_num. But soc_irq_num>32, there are n 32-bit registers(n=soc_irq_num/32).
10.3.5 IDU Semaphore
IDU also provides up to 32 semaphores to let uses to configure and use. And Semaphore is very useful for multi-core
system.
The related register is SEMAPHOREn.
The key points of software to use semaphores are the followings:
• All Cores in the system agree on using SEMAPHOREn register to protect a critical resource (an UART device for
example).
• If Core n wants to access the critical resource, it should try to own the SEMAPHOREn register by writing its Core
ID to this register, then read the SEMAPHOREn value and compare with the value with its Core ID, if it matches,
then it owns the semaphore successfully and can access the critical resource; if it not matches, obviously it does not
own the semaphore and can’t access the critical resource.
10.3. Functional Description 146
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• When the Core n owns the register SEMAPHOREn and finishes the job related the critical resource, then it should
release the register by write all 1 to it.
10.3.6 Event Distribution Broadcast Mode
The IDU acts as an event distributor in the sub-system, it supports:
• Broadcast all external event to a specific core or all target cores.
• Treat any external interrupt as external event.
If user wants to use IDU to broadcast the external event to some/all target cores, it just needs to turn on the related interrupt
COREn_EVENT_SEL register’s responding bits. The each COREn_EVENT_SEL register’s responding bits indicate the
each external event source.
ex: In some dual-core systems, if user set CORE0_EVENT_SEL[5] which indicate core0 will receive external event5.
If user wants to treat any external interrupt as event, set corresponding bit of INT_AS_EVENT_EN register.
ex: Write INT_AS_EVENT_EN[5] to “1”, which means the external interrupt5 is treated as event
10.3.7 NMI Distribution Broadcast Mode
If user wants to use IDU to broadcast the external nmi(no mask interrupt) to some/all target cores, it just needs to turn
on the related interrupt COREn_NMI_SEL register’s responding bits. The each COREn_NMI_SEL register’s responding
bits indicate the each external event source.
ex: In some dual-core systems, if user set CORE1_EVENT_SEL[7] which indicate core1 will receive external event7.
Lock Mode Mechanism
When soft-ware write 0x1234_5678 and 0x90CD_EFAB sequentially, all SoC-NMI distribution register is be locked and
cannot be changed until reset IDU module.

LGPIO Interrupt Requests (page 158) presents the IDU interrupt requests.

hartn_event The external event to hart n

LGPIO(0/1/2/3)
This section applies to instantiation of u_lgpio0, u_lgpio1, u_lgpio2, u_lgpio3
11.1 Introduction
The Nuclei LGPIO (Logic general-purpose I/Os) describes the operation of the Logic General Purpose Input/Output Con troller(LGPIO). The LGPIO controller is a peripheral device mapped in the internal memory map. Each port bit of the
General Purpose I/O(GPIO) Ports, can be individually configured by software in several modes(direction, pull up-enable,
drive value and so on), as well as selecting between various sources of the controls for these signals(HW I/O Function).
11.2 Main Features
The main features of LGPIO are the following:
• Output states: push-pull or open-drain + pull-up/down.
• Output data from output data register (GPIOx_OVAL) or peripheral (alternate function output).
• Input states: floating, pull-up/down.
• Input data to input data register (GPIOx_IVAL) or peripheral (alternate function input).
• Bit operate agilely for each LGPIO:
– Bit set (GPIOx_BIT_SET register),
– Bit reset (GPIOx_BIT_RESET register),
– Bit toggle (GPIOx_BIT_TOGGLE register).
• Locking mechanism (GPIOx_MASK) provided to freeze the I/O port oval.
• Highly flexible pin multiplexing allows the use of I/O pins as LGPIOs or as one of several peripheral functions.
• Support noise filters function and the length of noise canbe configured by soft-ware.
155
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
11.3 Functional Description
Fig. 11.1: LGPIO Block Diagram
Subject to the specific hardware characteristics of each I/O port listed in the datasheet, each port bit of the general-purpose
I/O (GPIO) ports can be individually configured by software in several modes:
• Input floating.
• Input pull-up.
• Input pull-down.
• Output open-drain with pull-up or pull-down capability.
• Output push-pull with pull-up or pull-down capability.
• Alternate function push-pull with pull-up or pull-down capability.
• Alternate function open-drain with pull-up or pull-down capability.
Each I/O port bit is freely programmable, however the I/O port registers have to be accessed as 32-bit words.
11.3.1 Interrupts
A single interrupt bit can be generated for each LGPIO bit. The interrupt can be driven by rising or falling edges, or by
level values, and each can be enabled individually.
Inputs are synchronized before being sampled by the interrupt logic, so the input pulse width must be long enough to be
detected by the synchronization logic.
To enable an interrupt, set the corresponding bit in the RISE_IE and/or FALL_IE and/or HIGH_IE and/or LOW_IE to 1.
If the correpsonding bit in RISE_IP or FALL_IP or HIGH_IP or LOW_IP is set, an interrupt pin will be raised.
All ports have external interrupt capability. To use external interrupt lines, the port must be configured in input mode.
11.3. Functional Description 156
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 11.2: Structure of A Single LGPIO Pin with Control Registers
Note
This structure is repeated for each pin.
11.3.2 I/O Port Control Registers
• GPIOx_IE: Used to enable the I/O input mode.
• GPIOx_MODE0,GPIOx_MODE1: Used to select the output type (push-pull or open-drain).
• GPIOx_PULL_MODE0,GPIOx_PULL_MODE1: Used to select the pull-up/pull-down whatever the I/O direction.
11.3.3 I/O Port Data Registers
• GPIOx_IVAL: Stores into the input data which input through the I/O, a read-only register.
• GPIOx_OVAL: Stores the data to be output, it is read/write accessible.
11.3.4 I/O Data Bitwise Handling
• GPIOx_SET/GPIOx_RESET: Allows the application to set and reset each individual bit in the output data register.
• GPIOx_TOGGLE: Allows the application to toggle each individual bit in the output data register.
• GPIOx_MASK: Allows the application to mask each individual bit in the output data register.
11.3.5 I/O Alternate Function Input/Output
There registers are provided to select one of the alternate function inputs/outputs available for each I/O. With these registers,
the user can connect an alternate function to some other pins as required by the application.
This means that a number of possible peripheral functions are multiplexed on each LGPIO using the
GPIOx_AFIO_SEL0,GPIOx_AFIO_SEL1 and GPIOx_AFIO_SEL2 alternate function registers. The application
11.3. Functional Description 157
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
can thus select any one of the possible functions for each I/O. The AF selection signal being common to the alternate
function input and alternate function output, a single channel is selected for the alternate function input/output of a given
I/O.
11.3.6 Drive Strength
When configured as output, each pin has a SW-controllable Drive Strength:
{GPIOx_DS3,GPIOx_DS2,GPIOx_DS1,GPIOx_DS0}.
11.3.7 Output Inversion
When configured as an output (either SW or IOF controlled), the SW-writable out xor register (GPIOx_OUT_XOR) is
combined with the output to invert it.
11.3.8 Noise Filters
In LGPIO, the user can configure the noise filters, if needed. Each PAD can turn on/off filter function independently (by
GPIO_GRP*_DIGITAL_FILTER_EN register).
The length of noise can be setted by GPIO_DIGITAL_NOISE_FILTER_LENGTH register and unit is LGPIO’s clk.
The signal’s length which is smaller than GPIO_DIGITAL_NOISE_FILTER_LENGTH will be treated as noise,
The signal’s length which is equality or bigger than GPIO_DIGITAL_NOISE_FILTER_LENGTH will be treated as normal
function signal.
The delay for digital funciton is GPIO_DIGITAL_NOISE_FILTER_LENGTH+1.
11.4 Interrupts
LGPIO Interrupt Requests (page 158) presents the LGPIO interrupt requests.
Table 11.1: LGPIO Interrupt Requests
Interrupt event Event flag Enable control bit
Rising edge from pad of LGPIO RISE_IP RISE_IE
Falling edge from pad of LGPIO FALL_IP FALL_IE
High level from pad of LGPIO HIGH_IP HIGH_IE
Low level from pad of LGPIO LOW_IP LOW_IE
Note
(1) Each LGPIO bit can sample four types of external interrupt event from PAD: rise edge, fall edge, high level or
low level.
(2) But LGPIO only generates high level interrupt type to CPU.

12
RTC(0/1/2)
This section applies to instantiation of u_rtc0, u_rtc1, u_rtc2
12.1 Introduction
The Nuclei RTC (Real-time Clock) provides an automatic wakeup to manage all low-power modes.
The real-time clock is an independent timer. The RTC provides a set of continuously running counters which can be used,
with suitable software, to provide a clock-calendar function. The counter values can be written to set the current time/date
of the system.
The RTC includes also a periodic programmable wakeup flag with interrupt capability.
12.2 Main Features
The main features of RTC are the following:
• Programmable prescaler: division factor is up to 2^20.
• 64-bit programmable counter for long-term measurement.
• Two separate clocks:
– ICB clock for ICB interface,
– RTC clock for RTC Core.
• Two separate reset types:
– The ICB interface is reset by icb_rstn,
– The RTC Core (Prescaler, Alarm, Counter and Divider) is reset by rtc_rstn.
• Three dedicated maskable interrupt lines:
– Alarm interrupt, for generating a software programmable alarm interrupt,
– Seconds interrupt, for generating a periodic interrupt signal with a programmable period length,
– Overflow interrupt, to detect when the internal programmable counter rolls over to zero.
• Digital calibration circuit(periodic counter correction) obtained in a calibration window of several seconds.
167
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
12.3 Functional Description
The RTC consists of two main units (see RTC block diagram (page 168)). The first one (ICB Interface) is used to interface
with the ICB bus. This unit also contains a set of 32-bit registers accessible from the ICB bus in read or write mode.
The other unit (RTC Core) consists of a chain of programmable counters made of two main blocks. The first block is the
RTC prescaler block, which generates the RTC time base TR_CLK (TR_CLK is generated by RTC_CLK divider) that can
be programmed to have a period of up to 1 second. It includes a 20-bit programmable divider (RTC Prescaler). Every
TR_CLK period, the RTC generates an interrupt (Second Interrupt) if it is enabled in the RTC_CRH register. The second
block is a 64-bit programmable counter that can be initialized to the current system time. The system time is incremented
at the TR_CLK rate and compared with a programmable date (stored in the RTC_ALR register) in order to generate an
alarm interrupt, if enabled in the RTC_CRH control register.
Fig. 12.1: RTC block diagram
Note
• The interface of SECOND_IRQ/OVERFLOW_IRQ/ALARM_IRQ is icb_clk domain.
• The interface of SECOND_EVENT/OVERFLOW_EVENT/ALARM_EVENT is rtc_clk domain.
12.3.1 Configuring RTC Registers
To write in the RTC_PRL, RTC_CNT, RTC_ALR, RTC_CALR registers, the peripheral must enter Configuration Mode.
This is done by setting the CNF bit in the RTC_CRL register.
Configuration procedure:
1. Set the CNF bit to enter configuration mode.
2. Write to one or more RTC registers.
3. Clear the CNF bit to exit configuration mode.
12.3. Functional Description 168
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
12.3.2 RTC Flag Assertion
The RTC Second flag (SECF) is asserted on each RTC Core clock cycle before the update of the RTC Counter.
The RTC_Alarm and RTC Alarm flag (ALRF) are asserted on the last RTC Core clock cycle before the counter reaches
the RTC Alarm value stored in the Alarm register increased by one (RTC_ALR + 1).
Fig. 12.2: RTC second and alarm waveform example with RTC_PR(RTC_PRL)=0x3, RTC_ALARM=0x4
The RTC Overflow flag (OWF) is asserted on the last RTC Core clock cycle before the counter reaches
0xFFFF_FFFF_FFFF_FFFF.
Fig. 12.3: RTC Overflow waveform example with RTC_PR(RTC_PRL)=0x3
12.3.3 RTC Smooth Digital Calibration
The RTC frequency can be digitally calibrated and corrected of the frequency is performed using series of small adjustments
(adding or subtracting individual RTC_CLK pulses). These adjustments are fairly well distributed so that the RTC is well
calibrated even when observed over short durations of time.
The smooth digital calibration is performed during a cycle of about 2^20 RTC_CLK pulses, or 32 (default is 32, which
also support 16 or 8) seconds when the input frequency is 32768 Hz. This cycle is maintained by a 20-bit counter
(calr_win_cnt[19:0]), clocked by RTC_CLK.
The smooth calibration register (RTC_CALR) specifies the number of RTC_CLK clock cycles to be masked or added
during the specific cycle period (default is 32 seconds):
• RTC_CALR[15]: Speicifies the calibration direction (adding or subtracting individual RTC_CLK pulses).
• RTC_CALR[8:0]: Specifies the number of RTCCLK pulses to be adjusted during the 32 seconds cycle.
• RTC_CALR[12]: Speicifies calibration cycle period is 16 seconds.
• RTC_CALR[13]: Speicifies calibration cycle period is 8 seconds.

12.5 Registers

Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
SECF 0 W0C 0x0 SECF: Second flag.
This bit is set by hardware when the 32-bit pro grammable prescaler overflows, thus incrementing the
RTC counter. Hence this flag provides a periodic sig nal with a period corresponding to the resolution pro grammed for the RTC counter (usually one second). An
interrupt is generated if SECIE=1 in the RTC_CRH reg ister. It can be cleared only by software. Writing “1” has
no effect.
-1’b0: Second flag condition not met.
-1’b1: Second flag condition met.
ALRF 1 W0C 0x0 ALRF: Alarm flag.
This bit is set by hardware when the 64-bit pro grammable counter reaches the threshold set in the
RTC_ALRL/RTC_ALRH register. An interrupt is gen erated if ALRIE=1 in the RTC_CRH register. It can be
cleared only by software. Writing “1” has no effect.
-1’b0: Alarm not detected.
-1’b1: Alarm detected.
OWF 2 W0C 0x0 OWF: Overflow flag.
This bit is set by hardware when the 64-bit pro grammable counter overflows. An interrupt is generated
if OWIE=1 in the RTC_CRH register. It can be cleared
only by software. Writing “1” has no effect.
-1’b0: Overflow not detected.
-1’b1: 64-bit programmable counter overflow occurred.

13
QSPI(1/2/3)
This section applies to instantiation of u_qspi1, u_qspi2, u_qspi3
13.1 Introduction
The Nuclei SPI (Serial Peripheral Interface) is a specialized communication interface targeting single, dual or quad SPI
Flash memories. It can operate in any of the three following modes:
• Register mode: SPI sends and receives by configuring the corresponding registers.
• DMA mode: SPI DMA interface uses a stream pre-processing protocol to ease the construction of SPI transfers
combining commands and data stream.
• XIP memory-mapped mode: the external Flash memory is mapped to the device address space and is seen by the
system as if it was an internal memory.
13.2 Main Features
The main features of SPI are the following:
• The SPI controller support master/slave operation over the single-lane, dual-lane,quad-lane and half duplex single lane protocols.
• Programmable clock polarity and phase (CPOL/CPHA).
• Configurable MSB First or LSB First.
• Master/Slave mode configurable frequency (FPCLK/2 max).
• SPI bus busy status flag.
• The baseline controller provides a FIFO-based interface for performing programmed I/O. Software initiates a transfer
by enqueuing a frame in the transmit FIFO; when the transfer completes, the slave response is placed in the receive
FIFO.
• Buffer or FIFO size for RX and TX configurable at RTL level.
• SDR and DDR support.
• Configurable SCK sample points for DDR mode.
176
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual

Fig. 13.1: NUQSPI block diagram
13.3.2 NUSPI Signal Interface Protocol Modes
13.3.2.1 Half-duplex SPI Mode
Legacy SPI mode allows just a single bit to be sent/received serially. In this mode, data is sent to the Flash memory over
the SO signal (whose I/O shared with IO0). Data received from the Flash memory arrives via SI (whose I/O shared with
IO0).use this single bit mode by setting the HSDMODE fields SPI_CR.HSDMODE to 1. In each phase which is configured
in single mode:
• IO0 (SO/SI) is inout mode
• IO1 is in output mode and forced to 1
• IO2 is in output mode and forced to 0 (to deactivate the write protect function)
• IO3 is in output mode and forced to 1 (to deactivate the hold function)
13.3.2.2 Single SPI Mode
Legacy SPI mode allows just a single bit to be sent/received serially.
In this mode data is sent to the Flash memory over the SO signal (whose I/ O shared with IO0).
Data received from the Flash memory arrives via SI (whose IO0/ IO1/ IO2/ IO3 are all are at high/ I / O shared with IO1).
The different phases can each be configured separately to use this single bit mode by setting the CMD_PROTO/
ADDR_PROTO/ MODE_PROTO/ DATA_PROTO fields (in FFMT/FFMT1) to 00.
In each phase which is configured in single mode:
• IO0 (SO) is in output mode
• IO1 (SI) is in input mode (high impedance)
• IO2 is in output mode and forced to 0 (to deactivate the write protect function)
13.3. Functional Description 177
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• IO3 is in output mode and forced to 1 (to deactivate the hold function)
13.3.2.3 Dual SPI Mode
In dual SPI mode two bits are sent/received simultaneously over the IO0/ IO1 signals.
The different phases can each be configured separately to use dual SPI mode by setting the CMD_PROTO/
ADDR_PROTO/ MODE_PROTO/ DATA_PROTO fields (in FFMT/ FFMT1) to 01.
In each phase which is configured in dual mode:
• IO0/IO1 are at high-impedance (input) during the data phase for read operations, and outputs in all other cases
• IO2 is in output mode and forced to 0
• IO3 is in output mode and forced to 1
13.3.2.4 Quad SPI Mode
In quad SPI mode four bits are sent/received simultaneously over the IO0/ IO1/ IO2/ IO3 signals.
The different phases can each be configured separately to use quad SPI mode by setting CMD_PROTO/ ADDR_PROTO/
MODE_PROTO/ DATA_PROTO fields (in FFMT/ FFMT1) to 10.
In each phase which is configured in quad mode IO0/ IO1/ IO2/ IO3 are all are at high impedance (input) during the data
phase for read operations and outputs in all other cases.
13.3.2.5 SDR Mode
Normal register mode can be configured by SPI_CR register to set the SDR mode By default, the DDR_EN bit (SPI_CR[2])
is 0 and the NUQSPI operates in single data rate (SDR) mode.
In SDR mode, when the NUQSPI is driving the IO0/SO, IO1, IO2, IO3 signals, these signals transition only with the
falling edge of CLK.
When receiving data in SDR mode, the NUQSPI assumes that the Flash memories also send the data using CLK falling
edge. the signals are sampled using the following (rising) edge of CLK.
13.3.2.6 DDR Mode
Normal register mode can be configured by SPI_CR register to set the SDR mode By default, the DDR_EN bit (SPI_CR[2])
is 1 and the NUQSPI operates in single data rate (DDR) mode.
In DDR mode, when the NUQSPI is driving the IO0/SO, IO1, IO2, IO3 signals in the address/alternate-byte/data phases,
a bit is sent on each of the falling and rising edges of CLK.
Instruction Address Mode Dummy Data
Viewer does not support full SVG 1.1
Fig. 13.2: An example of a ddr read command in quad mode
13.3. Functional Description 178
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
13.3.2.7 Slave And Master Mode
The SPI block supports master and slave mode. In master mode, SCK signals are set to output. In slave mode, SCK signals
are set to input. In the master mode, the SPI can support data lengths of 4-32. In the slave mode, the SPI only supports
data lengths of 8, 16 and 32 . In slave mode only support SDR mode.
13.3.3 NUSPI Communication Formats
During SPI communication, receive and transmit operations are performed simultaneously. The serial clock (SCK) syn chronizes the shifting and sampling of the information on the data lines. The communication format depends on the clock
phase, the clock polarity and the data frame format. To be able to communicate together, the master and slave devices must
follow the same communication format and be synchronized correctly.
Four possible timing relationships may be chosen by software, using the CPOL and CPHA bits in the SPI_SCKMODE
register. The CPOL (clock polarity) bit controls the idle state value of the clock when no data are being transferred. This
bit affects both master and slave modes. If CPOL is reset, the SCK pin has a low-level idle state. If CPOL is set, the SCK
pin has a high-level idle state.
If the CPHA bit is set, the second edge on the SCK pin captures the first data bit transacted (falling edge if the CPOL bit
is reset, rising edge if the CPOL bit is set). Data are latched on each occurrence of this clock transition type. If the CPHA
bit is reset, the first edge on the SCK pin captures the first data bit transacted (falling edge if the CPOL bit is set, rising
edge if the CPOL bit is reset). Data are latched on each occurrence of this clock transition type. The combination of the
CPOL (clock polarity) and CPHA (clock phase) bits selects the data capture clock edge.
CPHA=0
CPHA=1
Viewer does not support full SVG 1.1
Fig. 13.3: Data clock timing diagram
13.3.4 Configuration Of NUQSPI
The configuration procedure is almost the same for the master and the slave. For specific mode setups, follow the dedicated
chapters. When a standard communication has to be initialized, perform these steps:
The following is a common SPI configuration process
1. Write the proper GPIO registers: Configure GPIO for MOSI, MISO and SCK pins.
2. Write to the SPI_SCKDIV , SPI_FMT , SPI_FCTRL , SPI_CR and SPI_SCKMODE registers to set up proper values
of all not reserved bits and bit fields included there with next exceptions: First, determine the SPI mode to use. If it is
the normal mode master mode, then SPI_CR.MSTR=1 , SPI_FCTRL.FLASH_EN=0 . Configure SPI’s clock divi sion coefficient SPI_SCkDIV.DIV = 1, phase polarity SPI_SCKMODE.PHA=0 , SPI_SCKMODE.POL=0. Config ure the SPI protocol type FMT.PROTO = 0 (single line protocol), configure the SPI data size end type FMT.ENDINA
13.3. Functional Description 179
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
= 0 (large end), configure the data transceiver direction FMT.DIR = 1 (transmission direction), and configure the
SPI data length FMT.LEN = 8 (one byte).
3. The write SPI_TXDATA register fills txfifo with the data to be sent.
4. Finally, judge whether the SPI has been sent. If there is only one piece of data, you only need to judge STATUS.BUSY
= 0.If you need to wait for all the data in txfifo to be sent, you can judge STATUS.SPI_DONE=0.

13.6. Full Signal Interface 192
14
QSPI_XIP
This section applies to instantiation of u_qspi_xip0
14.1 Introduction
The Nuclei SPI (Serial Peripheral Interface) is a specialized communication interface targeting single, dual or quad SPI
Flash memories. It can operate in any of the three following modes:
• Register mode: SPI sends and receives by configuring the corresponding registers.
• DMA mode: SPI DMA interface uses a stream pre-processing protocol to ease the construction of SPI transfers
combining commands and data stream.
• XIP memory-mapped mode: the external Flash memory is mapped to the device address space and is seen by the
system as if it was an internal memory.
14.2 Main Features
The main features of SPI are the following:
• The SPI controller support master/slave operation over the single-lane, dual-lane,quad-lane and half duplex single lane protocols.
• Programmable clock polarity and phase (CPOL/CPHA).
• Configurable MSB First or LSB First.
• Master/Slave mode configurable frequency (FPCLK/2 max).
• SPI bus busy status flag.
• The baseline controller provides a FIFO-based interface for performing programmed I/O. Software initiates a transfer
by enqueuing a frame in the transmit FIFO; when the transfer completes, the slave response is placed in the receive
FIFO.
• The dedicated SPI controller implements a SPI flash read/write sequencer. The SPI controller is reset to a state
which allows memory-mapped reads.
• Buffer or FIFO size for RX and TX configurable at RTL level.
• SDR and DDR support.
• Configurable SCK sample points for DDR mode.
193
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual

14.3.2 NUQSPI Command Sequence
The NUQSPI communicates with the Flash memory using commands. Each command can include 5 phases: instruction,
address, mode byte, dummy, data. Any of these phases can be configured to be skipped, but at least one of the instruction,
address, alternate byte, or data phase must be present. nCS falls before the start of each command and rises again after
each command finishes.
Instruction Address Mode Dummy Data
Viewer does not support full SVG 1.1
Fig. 14.2: An example of a read command in quad mode
14.3. Functional Description 194
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Instruction Address Data
Viewer does not support full SVG 1.1
Fig. 14.3: An example of a write command in quad mode
14.3.2.1 Instruction Phase
During this phase, an 8-bit instruction, configured in READ INSTRUCTION field of FFMT[23:16] register, configured
in WRITE INSTRUCTION field of FFMT1[7:0] register, is sent to the Flash memory, specifying the type of operation to
be performed.Though most Flash memories can receive instructions only one bit at a time from the IO0/SO signal (single
SPI mode), the instruction phase can optionally send 2 bits at a time (over IO0/IO1 in dual SPI mode) or 4 bits at a time
(over IO0/IO1/IO2/IO3 in quad SPI mode). This can be configured using the CMD_PROTO field of FFMT[9:8] register.
When FFMT.CMD_EN = 0, the instruction phase is skipped, and the command sequence starts with the address phase.
14.3.2.2 Address Phase
In the address phase, 1-4 bytes are sent to the Flash memory to indicate the address of the operation. The number of address
bytes to be sent is configured in the addr_len field of FFMT[3:1] register. The address phase can send 1 bit at a time (over
SO in single SPI mode), 2 bits at a time (over IO0/IO1 in dual SPI mode), or 4 bits at a time (over IO0/IO1/IO2/IO3 in
quad SPI mode). This can be configured using the ADDR_PROTO field of FFMT[11:10] register. When ADDR_LEN =
00, the address phase is skipped, and the command sequence proceeds directly to the next phase, if any.
14.3.2.3 Mode Phase
In the alternate-bytes phase, 1-4 bytes are sent to the Flash memory, generally to control the mode of operation. The
number of alternate bytes to be sent is configured in the FFMT1.mode_cnt field of FFMT1[31:28] register. The bytes to
be sent are specified in the FFMT1.mode_code register. The alternate-bytes phase can send 1 bit at a time (over SO in
single SPI mode), 2 bits at a time (over IO0/IO1 in dual SPI mode), or 4 bits at a time (over IO0/IO1/IO2/IO3 in quad SPI
mode). This can be configured using the FFMT1.MODE_PROTO field of FFMT1[19:18] register.
When FFMT1.MODE_CNT= 0, the alternate-bytes phase is skipped, and the command sequence proceeds directly to the
next phase.
14.3.2.4 Dummy-cycle Phase
In the dummy-cycles phase, 1-31 cycles are given without any data being sent or received, in order to allow the Flash
memory the time to prepare for the data phase when higher clock frequencies are used. The number of cycles given during
this phase is specified in the FFMT.PAD_CNT field of FFMT[7:4] register. When PAD_CNT is zero, the dummy-cycles
phase is skipped, and the command sequence proceeds directly to the data phase, if present.
14.3.2.5 Data Phase
The data phase can send/receive 1 bit at a time (over SO/SI in single SPI mode), 2 bits at a time (over IO0/IO1 in
dual SPI mode), or 4 bits at a time (over IO0/IO1/IO2/IO3 in quad SPI mode). This can be configured using the
FFMT.DATA_PROTO field of FFMT[13:12] register.
14.3. Functional Description 195
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
14.3.3 NUSPI Signal Interface Protocol Modes
14.3.3.1 Half-duplex SPI Mode
Legacy SPI mode allows just a single bit to be sent/received serially. In this mode, data is sent to the Flash memory over
the SO signal (whose I/O shared with IO0). Data received from the Flash memory arrives via SI (whose I/O shared with
IO0).use this single bit mode by setting the HSDMODE fields SPI_CR.HSDMODE to 1. In each phase which is configured
in single mode:
• IO0 (SO/SI) is inout mode
• IO1 is in output mode and forced to 1
• IO2 is in output mode and forced to 0 (to deactivate the write protect function)
• IO3 is in output mode and forced to 1 (to deactivate the hold function)
14.3.3.2 Single SPI Mode
Legacy SPI mode allows just a single bit to be sent/received serially.
In this mode data is sent to the Flash memory over the SO signal (whose I/ O shared with IO0).
Data received from the Flash memory arrives via SI (whose IO0/ IO1/ IO2/ IO3 are all are at high/ I / O shared with IO1).
The different phases can each be configured separately to use this single bit mode by setting the CMD_PROTO/
ADDR_PROTO/ MODE_PROTO/ DATA_PROTO fields (in FFMT/FFMT1) to 00.
In each phase which is configured in single mode:
• IO0 (SO) is in output mode
• IO1 (SI) is in input mode (high impedance)
• IO2 is in output mode and forced to 0 (to deactivate the write protect function)
• IO3 is in output mode and forced to 1 (to deactivate the hold function)
14.3.3.3 Dual SPI Mode
In dual SPI mode two bits are sent/received simultaneously over the IO0/ IO1 signals.
The different phases can each be configured separately to use dual SPI mode by setting the CMD_PROTO/
ADDR_PROTO/ MODE_PROTO/ DATA_PROTO fields (in FFMT/ FFMT1) to 01.
In each phase which is configured in dual mode:
• IO0/IO1 are at high-impedance (input) during the data phase for read operations, and outputs in all other cases
• IO2 is in output mode and forced to 0
• IO3 is in output mode and forced to 1
14.3.3.4 Quad SPI Mode
In quad SPI mode four bits are sent/received simultaneously over the IO0/ IO1/ IO2/ IO3 signals.
The different phases can each be configured separately to use quad SPI mode by setting CMD_PROTO/ ADDR_PROTO/
MODE_PROTO/ DATA_PROTO fields (in FFMT/ FFMT1) to 10.
In each phase which is configured in quad mode IO0/ IO1/ IO2/ IO3 are all are at high impedance (input) during the data
phase for read operations and outputs in all other cases.
14.3. Functional Description 196
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
14.3.3.5 SDR Mode
Normal register mode can be configured by SPI_CR register to set the SDR mode By default, the DDR_EN bit (SPI_CR[2])
is 0 and the NUQSPI operates in single data rate (SDR) mode.
XIP mode can set the SDR mode by configuring the FFMT1.DDR_EN register. The different phases can each be configured
separately to use SDR mode by setting t he DDR_EN[3:0] fields (in FFMT1) to cmd/ addr/ mode/ data.
In SDR mode, when the NUQSPI is driving the IO0/SO, IO1, IO2, IO3 signals, these signals transition only with the
falling edge of CLK.
When receiving data in SDR mode, the NUQSPI assumes that the Flash memories also send the data using CLK falling
edge. the signals are sampled using the following (rising) edge of CLK.
14.3.3.6 DDR Mode
Normal register mode can be configured by SPI_CR register to set the SDR mode By default, the DDR_EN bit (SPI_CR[2])
is 1 and the NUQSPI operates in single data rate (DDR) mode.
XIP mode can set the DDR mode by configuring the FFMT1.DDR_EN register. The different phases can each be configured
separately to use DDR mode by setting the DDR_EN[3:0] fields (in FFMT1) to cmd/addr/mode/data.
In DDR mode, when the NUQSPI is driving the IO0/SO, IO1, IO2, IO3 signals in the address/alternate-byte/data phases,
a bit is sent on each of the falling and rising edges of CLK.
Instruction Address Mode Dummy Data
Viewer does not support full SVG 1.1
Fig. 14.4: An example of a ddr read command in quad mode
14.3.3.7 Slave And Master Mode
The SPI block supports master and slave mode. In master mode, SCK signals are set to output. In slave mode, SCK signals
are set to input. In the master mode, the SPI can support data lengths of 4-32. In the slave mode, the SPI only supports
data lengths of 8, 16 and 32 . In slave mode only support SDR mode.
14.3.4 NUSPI Communication Formats
During SPI communication, receive and transmit operations are performed simultaneously. The serial clock (SCK) syn chronizes the shifting and sampling of the information on the data lines. The communication format depends on the clock
phase, the clock polarity and the data frame format. To be able to communicate together, the master and slave devices must
follow the same communication format and be synchronized correctly.
Four possible timing relationships may be chosen by software, using the CPOL and CPHA bits in the SPI_SCKMODE
register. The CPOL (clock polarity) bit controls the idle state value of the clock when no data are being transferred. This
bit affects both master and slave modes. If CPOL is reset, the SCK pin has a low-level idle state. If CPOL is set, the SCK
pin has a high-level idle state.
If the CPHA bit is set, the second edge on the SCK pin captures the first data bit transacted (falling edge if the CPOL bit
is reset, rising edge if the CPOL bit is set). Data are latched on each occurrence of this clock transition type. If the CPHA
bit is reset, the first edge on the SCK pin captures the first data bit transacted (falling edge if the CPOL bit is set, rising
14.3. Functional Description 197
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
edge if the CPOL bit is reset). Data are latched on each occurrence of this clock transition type. The combination of the
CPOL (clock polarity) and CPHA (clock phase) bits selects the data capture clock edge.
CPHA=0
CPHA=1
Viewer does not support full SVG 1.1
Fig. 14.5: Data clock timing diagram
14.3.5 Configuration Of NUQSPI
The configuration procedure is almost the same for the master and the slave. For specific mode setups, follow the dedicated
chapters. When a standard communication has to be initialized, perform these steps:
The following is a common SPI configuration process
1. Write the proper GPIO registers: Configure GPIO for MOSI, MISO and SCK pins.
2. Write to the SPI_SCKDIV , SPI_FMT , SPI_FCTRL , SPI_CR and SPI_SCKMODE registers to set up proper values
of all not reserved bits and bit fields included there with next exceptions: First, determine the SPI mode to use. If it is
the normal mode master mode, then SPI_CR.MSTR=1 , SPI_FCTRL.FLASH_EN=0 . Configure SPI’s clock divi sion coefficient SPI_SCkDIV.DIV = 1, phase polarity SPI_SCKMODE.PHA=0 , SPI_SCKMODE.POL=0. Config ure the SPI protocol type FMT.PROTO = 0 (single line protocol), configure the SPI data size end type FMT.ENDINA
= 0 (large end), configure the data transceiver direction FMT.DIR = 1 (transmission direction), and configure the
SPI data length FMT.LEN = 8 (one byte).
3. The write SPI_TXDATA register fills txfifo with the data to be sent.
4. Finally, judge whether the SPI has been sent. If there is only one piece of data, you only need to judge STATUS.BUSY
= 0.If you need to wait for all the data in txfifo to be sent, you can judge STATUS.SPI_DONE=0.
The following is the configuration process of SPI XIP mode
1. Write the proper GPIO registers: Configure GPIO for MOSI, MISO and SCK pins.
2. Write to the SPI_SCKDIV , SPI_FFMT ,SPI_FFMT1, SPI_FCTRL , SPI_CR and SPI_SCKMODE registers to
set up proper values of all not reserved bits and bit fields included there with next exceptions: First, switch
the SPI controller to XIP mode, then SPI_CR.MSTR=1 , SPI_FCTRL.FLASH_EN=1. Configure SPI’s clock
division coefficient SPI_SCkDIV.DIV = 1, phase polarity SPI_SCKMODE.PHA=0 , SPI_SCKMODE.POL=0.
Configure SPI false command, address, dummy and other information, Configure SPI false command, ad dress, dummy and other information. For the configuration of read data, you need to open FFMT.CMD_EN,
and then configure the address length FFMT.ADD_LEN, configure the length FFMT.PAD_CNT of the read
dummy stage, configure the data protocol used in each stage as the command FFMT.CMD_PROTO, address
FFMT.ADDR_PROTO, FFMT.DATA_PROTO, and finally configure the read command FFMT.CMD_CODE read
dummy data FFMT.PAD_CODE
14.3. Functional Description 198
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
3. Configure flash write command FFMT1.WCMD_CODE. If dummy is required for write operation,
FFMT1.WAPD_CNT should be configured
4. Finally, you can access flash through ICB bus.

0x40 SPI_FMT Frame format(master/slave)(normal mode non-xip)

14.5.1.10 SPI_BOUNDARY_CFG
Address offset: 0x24.
Table 14.12: Register Format of SPI_BOUNDARY_CFG
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
spi_boundry_cfg 31:0 RW 0x3ff The boundary address configuration is a boundary address
(The boundary address must be a power of 2 (such as 16,
32, 64, etc.).) minus 1 . This register is modified under
the control of the spi_boundary_en register.
Usage scenario: In SPI XIP Burst mode, when the burst
length is very long, as a flash auto increment mode, some
flash auto increment sizes are determined by a bound ary. Therefore, the software needs to inform us of this
boundary size so that the hardware can resend addresses
at the boundary, which will not cause data overflow.SPI
XIP splits a contiguous address into multiple small burst
operations.

Address offset: 0x40.
Table 14.18: Register Format of SPI_FMT
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
spi_proto 1:0 RW 0x0 SPI protocol:(normal mode non-xip)
• 3’b000: Single
• 3’b001: Dual
• 3’b010: Quad
• 3’b011: Octol
• 3’b100: X16
• 3’b111: HDS (Half-duplex SPI is 3 wires Mode
(SS, SCK, SIO bidirectional data))
Note: For Octol mode or X16 mode .if the hardware does
not support 8-wire or X16 mode , the value is not writable,
and the register that writes the value remains the original
value .8-line lines in slave mode do not support byte mode
spi_endian 2 RW 0x0 SPI endianness bit:(normal mode non-xip)
• 1’b0: Transmit bit most-significant bit (MSB) first
• 1’b1: Transmit bit least-significant bit (LSB) first
spi_dir 3 RW 0x0 SPI I/O direction. This is reset to 1 for flash-enabled SPI
controllers, 0 otherwise.(normal mode non-xip)
• 1’b0: Rx: For dual and quad protocols, the DQ pins
are tri-stated. For the single protocol, the DQ0 pin
is driven with the transmit data as normal.
• 1’b1: Tx: The receive FIFO is not populated.
spi_proto_hi 4 RW 0x0 This bit field is used to extend the SPI_FMT.spi_proto reg ister to the high bits belonging to SPI_FMT.spi_proto
spi_endian_byte 5 RW 0x0 SPI endianness for byte:(normal mode non-xip)
• 1’b0: Transmit byte most-significant bit (MSB) first
• 1’b1: Transmit byte least-significant bit (LSB) first
Reserved 15:6 / 0x0 /
spi_len 21:16 RW 0x8 Number of bits per frame(normal mode non-xip)
• 6’b001000: 8 bits per Frame
• 6’b010000: 16 bits per Frame
• 6’b100000: 32 bits per Frame
Note: master mode: 4-32, slave mode: 8 , 16 ,32
Reserved 31:22 / 0x0 /

14.5.1.21 SPI_FCTRL
Address offset: 0x60.
Table 14.23: Register Format of SPI_FCTRL
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
spi_flash_en 0 RW 0x1 SPI XIP Mode Select
• 1’b0: disable
• 1’b1: enable
spi_flash_wmask_en 1 RW 0x0 SPI XIP Data mask Select.If the function is turned on,
our bus write operation will look at the wmask signal of
the bus, and turning on this function will affect the XIP
write performance, and if the function is turned off, the
XIP write will ignore the bus wmask signal
• 1’b0: disable
• 1’b1: enable
continues on next page
14.5. Registers 206
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 14.23 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
spi_flash_wen 2 RW 0x0 SPI XIP mode write enable
• 1’b0: disable
• 1’b1: enable
spi_flash_burst_en 3 RW 0x0 SPI XIP burst enable
• 1’b0: disable
• 1’b1: enable
spi_flash_wrap_en 4 RW 0x0 SPI XIP wrap enable:
• 1’b0: disable if slave don’t support warp
• 1’b1: enable if slave support wrap
Note: 1.Both burst-en and wrap-en are disabled: When
these two options are not enabled, regardless of whether
the CPU issues a burst or single read request, the QSPI XIP (Execute In Place) controller will break down these
requests into individual read requests to access the exter nal memory (such as flash or psram). This means that even
if the CPU attempts to improve data transfer efficiency
through burst mode, the actual operation is still performed
as individual accesses, potentially reducing overall perfor mance.
2.Both burst-en and wrap-en are enabled: With this setup,
if the CPU issues a burst request that fits the wrap mode
(i.e., cyclic reading of data within a 64-byte boundary),
the QSPI-XIP controller will automatically resend the ad dress to the external memory based on the CPU’s burst
wrap behavior. Notably, in this case, there is no need for
additional configuration of the wrap characteristics of the
external memory (flash/psram), as they work by default in
incremental (incr) mode, which meets the requirements.
3.Only burst-en is enabled with matching external memory
characteristics: If the burst wrap type issued by the CPU
matches the burst wrap characteristics supported by the ex ternal memory (flash/psram), then only burst-en needs to
be activated without enabling wrap-en. Additionally, it is
necessary to pre-activate the wrap features of the external
memory. This approach can provide higher performance
compared to the second scenario because it allows more
efficient data transfers, reducing unnecessary address re sends, thereby increasing the data transfer rate.
spi_boundary_en 5 RW 0x0 SPI XIP boundary config enable
• 1’b0: disable
• 1’b1: enable
Reserved 31:6 / 0x0 /

uDMA
This section applies to instantiation of u_udma0
15.1 Introduction
The Nuclei uDMA (Micro Direct Memory Access) controller serves as a hardware approach for direct data transfers
between peripherals/memory and memory without CPU intervention, targeting to off-load CPU from transferring big data
chunks where required.
15.2 Main Features
The main features of uDMA are the following:
• Support 1 mem2mem channels with software configurable priority.
• Support 28 peripheral channels for pa-mem transfers.
• Support memory access with 1-16 beat(s) incrementing burst type.
• Support configurable data width 8, 16, 32, 64 bits for memory access (depending on data bus width).
• Support fixed or increasing address generation algorithm.
• Support mem2mem continuous or repeat configurable-N times transfer mode.
• Support independent source and destination base address setting, byte, half-word or word aligned.
• Support arbitrary transfer block size within (32M-1) bytes.
• Support interrupt generation, masking and clearing.
Note
The specific number of channels depends on the configuration of RTL.
15.3 Functional Description
Two master internal bus interfaces (Wr Bus and Rd Bus) are available on DMA top for accessing memories.
DMA configuration programming is through another dedicated slave Reg Bus.
Mem2mem transfers be achieved by programming the corresponding configuration and control bits inside DMA controller.
With proper configuration, different types of interrupts (full-transfer done, half-transfer done and transfer error) can be
generated when the transfer status is reached.
216
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Linked list mode can be easily enabled through configuring the dedicated Linked-List Address (LLA) register. A repeat type of transfer mode is supported on each node of the entrire linked-list transfer process. However continuous transfer
mode is not allowed to work along with linked list transfer mode, see more detailed information on the LLA register
description.
Apart from regular mem2mem transfers, another type of data transfer involving peripheral is also supported. In this mode,
source or destination has peripheral attribute (pa-mem). The corresponding DMA channel has to be properly configured
first, e.g. the transfer size, source address, destination address and data size. Once the channel is enabled, DMA acknowl edges each request initiated by peripheral during which one data transfer (byte, half-word, word) takes place.
To avoid that one type of DMA transfer (mem2mem or pa-mem) keeps taking up the physical bus interface, round-robin
arbitration scheme is adopted when the two types of transfers are running in parallel.
The uDMA application block diagram is shown in uDMA Application Block Diagram (page 217).

15.4 Interrupts
The controller provides 2 groups of interrupts for each type of uDMA data transfer. The interrupt sources can also be
enabled or disabled separately through the DMA_CHX_IRQ_EN and DMA_PA_CHX_IRQ_EN register.
Setting the appropriate register bits to enable/disable the corresponding interrupt. Different interrupts are generated on the
following events:
• interrupt for channelx access error for data transfer
• interrupt for channelx half transfer for one TSIZE
• interrupt for channelx full transfer for one TSIZE
• interrupt for channelx access error for loading link configuration registers
• interrupt for channelx full transfer for whole linked list
• PA type interrupt for channelx access error
• PA type interrupt for channelx half transfer
• PA type interrupt for channelx full transfer
uDMA Interrupt Requests (page 218) presents the uDMA interrupt requests.

Field Bit Access Reset
Value

15.5.1.3 MEM2MEM TRANSFER TYPE CONFIG REGISTER (DMA_CHi_CFG_MCTRL)
Address offset: 0x0010 + 28 * i, where “i” is image component from 0 to 0
Address offset: 0x10.
Table 15.5: Register Format of DMA_CHi_CFG_MCTRL
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
TRANS_EN 0 RW 0x0 DMA transfer enable, asserting the bit will start a
mem2mem DMA transfer, it can be cleared by soft ware and the transfer will stop after current burst is
finished.
It can also be cleared by hardware when a complete
transfer is finished or a transfer error response occurs
in which case current transfer will be aborted.
• 1’b0: DMA transfer disable
• 1’b1: DMA transfer enable
continues on next page
15.5. Registers 222
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 15.5 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
TRANS_STAT 1 RO 0x0 Transfer type status
This bit accurately reflects the real channel status. Pro gram bit 0 trans_en cannot guarantee the channel to be
active since other higher priority channel could
still have ongoing transfer and this channel can only be
queued for further processing. Software can only read
an active ‘1’ when this channel is actually doing the
transfer.
• 1’b0: cannot guarantee the channel to be active
• 1’b1: this channel is actually doing the transfer
OUTS_CTRL_EN 2 RW 0x0 Transfer outstanding control
• 1’b0: DMA transfer outstanding number is lim ited to half of the internal DATA FIFO depth to
ensure correct functionality
• 1’b1: DMA transfer outstanding number can be
reached up to 64 bus transactions
Note that setting this bit 1 makes DMA data trans fer engine ignore the external read/write bus inter dependencies. This may cause potential deadlocks
where a read operation cannot be initiated while a
write transaction cannot be completed due to insuffi cient data from read channel. This bit is recommended
to be used for testing purposes.
Reserved 4:3 / 0x0 /
BST_BND 5 RW 0x0 Burst boundary control
This bit defines DMA burst transfer boundary crossing
options
• 1’b0: a DMA burst transfer cannot cross 1k
boundary address
• 1’b1: a DMA burst transfer can cross 1k bound ary address but not 4k
TRANS_MODE 7:6 RW 0x0 Transfer mode selection
• 2’b00: Single mode transfer
• 2’b01: Continuous mode transfer
• 2’b10: Transfer repeat mode, transfer continues
for N(defined in Transfer Repeat Number Regis ter) times with the same transfer configuration
• 2’b11: reserved
With continuous mode, a new transfer is auto-started
with the same transfer configuration upon completion
of current transfer.
And this auto-transfer keeps going until software pro grams the enable bit to “0” to disable the channel, the
transfer would then stop when current transfer is fin ished.
During repeat mode, the source/destination start ad dress of a new transfer follows the last accessed address
of last iteration of data transfers. However an offset can
be achieved by adding/subtracting the value defined in
the MARU register for obtaining flexible starting ad dress.
continues on next page
15.5. Registers 223
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 15.5 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
PRIORITY 9:8 RW 0x0 Priority for mem2mem transfers mem-mem transfer
priority in case multiple m2m channels are enabled
• 2’b00: Low
• 2’b01: Med
• 2’b10: High
• 2’b11: Ultra-high
Reserved 11:10 / 0x0 /
MDNA 12 RW 0x0 Next address generation algorithm for transmitting
data to destination memory
• 1’b0: Increasing address mode
• 1’b1: Fixed address
In case fixed address is configured, start destination ad dress is forced to be aligned
MSNA 13 RW 0x0 Next address generation algorithm for fetching data
from source memory

In case fixed address is configured, start source address
is forced to be aligned
Reserved 15:14 / 0x0 /
MDWIDTH 18:16 RW 0x0 Transfer width for transferring data to destination

• 3’b001: 16bit
• 3’b010: 32bit
• Others: reserved
Reserved 20:19 / 0x0 /
MSWIDTH 23:21 RW 0x0 Transfer width for fetching from source
• 3’b000: 8bit
• 3’b001: 16bit
• 3’b010: 32bit
• Others: reserved
MDBURST 27:24 RW 0x0 Number of transfers in a burst for transmitting data to
destination memory
• 4’b0000: : 1 transfer
• 4’b0001: 2 transfers
• 4’b0010: 3 transfers
• 4’b0011: 4 transfers
• 4’b0100: 5 transfers
• 4’b0101: 6 transfers
• 4’b0110: 7 transfers
• . . .

Field Bit Access Reset
Value

15.5.1.4 TRANSFER REPEAT TIME REGISTER (DMA_CHi_CFG_MRPT)
Address offset: 0x0014 + 28 * i, where “i” is image component from 0 to 0
Address offset: 0x14.
Table 15.6: Register Format of DMA_CHi_CFG_MRPT
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
TRANS_RPT 11:0 RW 0x0 DMA_CFG_MCTRL.trans_mode is configured as ‘b10
transfer repeat mode, this field defines the repeat loop
number. Configuring 0 is NOT allowed before a dma
transfer is initiated
RPT_IRQ_EN 12 RW 0X0 IRQ enable for last iteration of data transfer during repeat
mode
• 1’b0: full/half transfer IRQ is triggered for each
iteration of a repeated transfer
• 1’b1: only the last iteration of data transfer can
trigger a full/half transfer IRQ
Reserved 15:13 / 0x0 /
RPT_SAUM 16 RW 0x0 Source address update mode in repeat transfer mode
• 1’b0: source address for next iteration of transfer is
obtained by the addition of current source address
register and value of RSAU
• 1’b1: source address for next iteration of transfer
is obtained by subtracting current source address
register by the value of RSAU
continues on next page
15.5. Registers 225
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 15.6 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
RPT_DAUM 17 RW 0x0 Destination address update mode in repeat transfer mode
• 1’b0: destination address for next iteration of
transfer is obtained by the addition of current des tination address register and value of RDAU
• 1’b1: destination address for next iteration of
transfer is obtained by subtracting current destina tion address register by the value of RDAU
Reserved 31:18 / 0x0 /
15.5.1.5 TRANSFER SIZE REGISTER (DMA_CHi_CFG_MSIZE)
Address offset: 0x0018 + 28 * i, where “i” is image component from 0 to 0
Address offset: 0x18.
Table 15.7: Register Format of DMA_CHi_CFG_MSIZE
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
TSIZE 24:0 RW 0x0 Number of bytes for one DMA transfer.
‘0’ is NOT allowed for starting a transfer and may result in un expected result by doing so during DMA transmission. These
bits indicate the remaining bytes to be transferred.
It will be auto-reloaded to its original value for
a new transfer when current transfer is finished if
DMA_CFG_MCTRL.trans_mode is configured to ‘b01
or ‘b10, i.e.
continuous or repeat mode. A DMA transfer error may freeze
the register to the value of last successful transfer, however it
will start over and auto record the correct
remaining data number for the next new transfer.
Reserved 31:25 / 0x0 /
15.5.1.6 REPEAT ADDRESS UPDATE VALUE REGISTER (DMA_Chi_CFG_MRAU)
Address offset: 0x0024 + 28 * i, where “i” is image component from 0 to 0
Address offset: 0x24.
Table 15.8: Register Format of DMA_Chi_CFG_MRAU
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
RSAU 15:0 RW 0x0 Source address update value from last iteration of repeated
transfer during repeat mode
RDAU 31:16 RW 0x0 Destination address update value from last iteration of re peated transfer during repeat mode
15.5.1.7 LINK LIST ADDRESS REGISTER (DMA_Chi_CFG_MLLA)
Address offset: 0x0028 + 28 * i, where “i” is image component from 0 to 0
Address offset: 0x28.
15.5. Registers 226
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 15.9: Register Format of DMA_Chi_CFG_MLLA
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
LLA 31:0 RW 0x0 At the end of a (repeated) m2m transfer, the channel con figuration registers (MSRCADDR, MDSTADDR, MCTRL,
MRPT, MSIZE, MRAU and MLLR itself) are loaded from
the memory location with the address of this register value.
Note that the loaded configuration register contents must be
located at consecutive address space unlike the addresses of
MSIZE and MRAU with 8-bytes gap in case DMA bus width
is lower(or equal to) 32bits.
The configuration registers are not updated if the value of this
LLA register is “0, and the end of transfer is reached.
Note that when SW triggers a continuous mode of data trans fer with LLA being a non-zero value(link-list enabled), the
linked-list mode is ignored after the continuous data transfer
is terminated by SW programming the channel enable bit and
the whole channel transfer is completed.
Similarly, an ongoing link-list transfer will be stopped when
a continuous transfer mode is detected through loading the
LLA associated configuration registers.
The value of this register must be word-aligned for correct
configuration register loading, or else incorrect data transfer
may be resulted in.

TRANS_EN 0 RW 0x0 DMA transfer enable, asserting the bit will enable a
PA mem2mem DMA transfer, it can be cleared by

It can also be cleared by hardware when a complete
transfer is finished or a transfer error response occurs

• 1’b1: DMA transfer enable

MWIDTH 18:16 RW 0x0 Transfer width, source and destination transfers use

Address offset: 0x040C + 24 * i, where “i” is image component from 0 to 27

15.5. Registers 228
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 15.13: Register Format of DMA_PA_CH0_CFG_MSIZE
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
TSIZE 24:0 RW 0x0 Number of bytes for one DMA transfer.
‘0’ is NOT allowed for starting a transfer and may result in un expected result by doing so during DMA transmission. These
bits indicate the remaining bytes to be transferred.
It will be auto-reloaded to its original value for
a new transfer when current transfer is finished if
DMA_PA_Chx_CFG_MCTRL.trans_mode is configured as
continuous mode.
A DMA transfer error may freeze the register to the value
of last successful transfer, however it will start over and auto
record the correct remaining data number for
the next new transfer.
Reserved 31:25 / 0x0 /
Note
TSIZE field can only be integral multiple of the value in mwidth field of pamem controller register

USART(0/1/2/3/4)
This section applies to instantiation of u_usart0, u_usart1, u_usart2, u_usart3, u_usart4
16.1 Introduction
The USART (Universal Synchronous Asynchronous Receiver Transmitter) offers a flexible means of full-duplex data ex change with external equipment requiring an industry standard NRZ asynchronous serial data format. It also offers more
accuracy of baud rates using a fractional baud rate generator.
The USART supports both synchronous one-way and Half-duplex Single-wire communications, as well as Smartcard
protocol and modem operations (CTS/RTS) are also supported.
High speed data communication is possible by using the DMA Channel with DMA Controller.
16.2 Main Features
The main features of USART are the following:
• Full duplex, asynchronous communications.
• Two internal FIFOs for transmit and receive data:
– The width of FIFOs is 9 bit,
– The depth of FIFOs is 16.
• A common programmable transmit and receive baud rate:
– intergral baud rate generator systems,
– fractional baud rate generator systems.
• Auto baud rate detection
• Programmable data word length (5/6/7/8/9 bits).
• Configurable stop bits (0.5/1/1.5/2 stop bits) in TX and RX.
• Parity control:
– Transmits parity bit,
– Checks parity of received data byte.
• Single wire half duplex communication.
• Synchronous master/slave mode and clock output/input for synchronous communications.
• Support DMA access with DMA controller for high speed data communication.
• Separate enable bits for Transmitter and Receiver:
236
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
– The TX and RX can worked in DMA or CPU mode separately.
• Support Hardware flow control for modem.
• Programmable data order with MSB-first or LSB-first shifting.
• Separate signal polarity control for transmission and reception.
• Swappable Tx/Rx pin configuration.
• Support RS-485 transceiver.
• Smartcard mode:
– Supports the T=0 and T=1 asynchronous protocols for smartcards as defined in the ISO/IEC 7816-3 standard.
– 0.5 and 1.5 stop bits for Smartcard operation.
• Communication control/error detection flags.
• Interrupt sources with flags:
– support RX IDLE time out interrupt,
– support RX_FIFO/TX_FIFO water-mark interrupt.
This manual describes the full set of features implemented in USART peripheral.
USART implement the full set of features as shown in USART Implementation (page 237) .
Table 16.1: USART Implementation

16.3 Functional Description
The interface is externally connected to another device by three pins. Any USART bidirectional communication requires
a minimum of two pins: Receive Data In (RX) and Transmit Data Out (TX).
RX: Receive Data Input is the serial data input. The majority voting sampling techniques are used for data recovery by
discriminating between valid incoming data and noise.
TX: Transmit Data Output. When the transmitter is disabled, the output pin returns to its I/O port configuration. When
the transmitter is enabled and no data needs to be transmitted, the TX pin is High. In single-wire mode, this I/O is used to
transmit and receive the data.
Through these pins, serial data is transmitted and received in normal USART mode as frames comprising:
• An Idle Line prior to transmission or reception.
• A start bit.
• A data word (5/6/7/8/9 bits) least significant bit first.
• 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2 Stop bits indicating that the frame is complete.
16.3. Functional Description 237
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• This interface uses a fractional baud rate generator - with a 13-bit mantissa and 3-bit fraction.
• A status register (USART_CTS_FLAG).
• Data register (USART_TXDATA/USART_RXDATA).
• A baud rate register (USART_DIV) - 18-bit mantissa and 3-bit fraction.
The following pin is required in synchronous master/slave mode:
• CK: This pin acts as Clock output in Synchronous master.In Synchronous Master mode,this pin outputs the transmit ter data clock for synchronous transmission corresponding to SPI master mode (no clock pulses on start bit and stop
bit, and a software option to send a clock pulse on the last data bit). In parallel data can be received synchronously
on RX. This can be used to control peripherals that have shift registers (e.g. LCD drivers). The clock phase and
polarity are software programmable.
The following pins are required in Hardware flow control mode:
• CTS: Clear To Send.When driven high,this signal blocks the data transmission at the end of current transfer.
• RTS: Request To Send.When it is low,this signal indicates that the USART is ready to receive data.
The block diagram of the USART is shown in WWDG block diagram. (page 266).
Fig. 16.1: USART block diagram.
16.3.1 Clock Domain
The USART block diagram given in WWDG block diagram. (page 266) shows single clock domains.
16.3.2 Transmitter
The transmitter can send data words of either 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9 bits,depending on the USART_SETUP[6:4] register. When
the transmit enable bit (USART_TXCTRL[0]) is set, the data in the transmit shift register is output on the TX pin and the
corresponding clock pulses are output on the CK pin.
Character transmission
During an USART transmission, data shifts out the least significant bit first on the TX pin. In this mode, the TXFIFO
buffered USART_TXDATA register as a FIFO entry and the transmit state machine shift those entry out of FIFO one by
one.
Every character is preceded by a start bit which corresponds to a low logic level for one bit period. The character is
terminated by a configurable number of stop bits.
The following stop bits are supported by USART: 0.5, 1, 1.5 and 2 stop bits.
configurable stop bits
The number of stop bits to be transmitted with every character can be programmed in USART_TXCTRL[2:1].
16.3. Functional Description 238
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• 1 stop bit: This is the default value of number of stop bits.
• 2 stop bits: This will be supported by normal USART and Single-wire modes.
An idle frame transmission will include the stop bits.
16.3.3 Receiver
The USART can receive data words of either 5 or 6 or 7 or 8 or 9 bits depending on the 4~6 bits in the USART_SETUP
register.
Start bit detection
The start bit detection sequence is the same when oversampling by 16 or by 8.
In the USART, the start bit is detected when a specific bits of samples is recognized. The start bit is when a falling edge is
detected, and the second bit after that is 0, and at least two bits of the seventh, eighth, ninth bits are 0. This sequence is: 1
1 1 0 X 0 X X X 0 0 0 X X X X X X.
Fig. 16.2: Start bit detection
If the specific bits are not complete, the start bit detection aborts and the receiver returns to the idle state(no flag is set),where
it waits for a falling edge.
The start bit is confirmed as below:
1.the first sampling on the 3rd bit at 0.
2.the second sampling on the 7th, 8th and 9th bits also finds the 2 bits at 0.
Character reception:
During an USART reception,data are shifted out least significant bit first(default configuration)through the RX pin.
During an USART reception, data shifts in least significant bit first through the RX pin. In this mode, the RXFIFO buffered
receive data as FIFO entry and the ICB bus can fetch those data in sequence by access USART_RXDATA register.
Data sampling
Data-sampling techniques are used for data recovery by discriminating between valid incoming data and noise. When the
real baud divider is 15, the sampling on the 7th, 8th, 9th bits will determine this bit frame is 0 or 1. Which is shown in
Data sampling (page 240).
When the real baud rate divider is even num, the sample position (USART_SETUP[18:17]) can be setted for each data
frame.
16.3. Functional Description 239
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 16.3: Data sampling
Table 16.2: Noise detection from sampled data
Sampled value Received bit value Data validity
000 0 Valid
001 0 Valid
010 0 Valid
011 1 Valid
100 0 Valid
101 1 Valid
110 1 Valid
111 1 Valid
Stop bit detection
The number of stop bits to be received can be configured through the control bits of USART_TXCTRL[2:1], if there is
low level in the middle of stop_bit frame, the RX_FRAME_ERROR(USART_CTS_FLAG[8]) flag will be setted which is
inhibited in the uart protocal.
16.3.4 Baud Rate Generation
The baud rate for the receiver and transmitter (RX and TX) are both set to the same value as programmed in the Mantissa
and Fraction values of USART_DIV register.
Tx/Rx baud = fclk/(USART_DIV+1)
fclk: input clock (icb_clk) to the peripheral.
If the fractional enable bit (USART_SETUP[19]) is turn on, the real div is USART_DIV[20:0], the 0~2bit is fractional
part, the 20~3bit is mantissa part. When the fractional enable bit is turn off, the real div is USART_DIV[18:0], which are
all mantissa part.
16.3.5 Auto baud rate detection
The USART can detect and automatically set the ABR_EN bit in the UART_ADVANCED_SETUP register based on the
reception of character.
The auto baud rate detection mode can be set through ABR_MOD[1:0] field in the UART_ADVANCED_SETUP register.
There are four modes based on different character patterns.
• The start bit detection mode: Character starting with a bit at “1”.
• The negedge detection mode: Character starting with a 10xxxxxx bit pattern.
• The 0x7f detection mode: Character starting with 0x7f character frame in LSB first mode or a 0xfe in MSB first
mode.
• The 0x55 detection mode: Character starting with 0x55 character frame.
The auto baud rate detection completion is indicated by setting ABRF flag bit in UART_ADVANCED_STATUS register.
If auto baund rate has error, the ABRE flag bit be set.
16.3. Functional Description 240
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
16.3.6 Parity Control
Parity control (generation of parity bit in transmission and parity checking in reception) can be enabled by setting the parity
enable bit in the USART_SETUP register. Depending on the frame length defined by the length bit and parity selection
bit(even parity or odd parity).
Even parity
The parity bit is calculated to obtain an even number of “1s” inside the frame of the 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9 LSB bits(depending on
the frame length) and the parity bit.
Odd parity
The parity bit is calculated to obtain an odd number of “1s” inside the frame of the 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9 LSB bits(depending on
the frame length) and the parity bit.
16.3.7 USART Synchronous Mode
Master mode
The synchronous mode is selected by writing the CLKEN bit in the USART_SETUP register to 1. The USART allows
the user to control a bidirectional synchronous serial communications in master mode. The CK pin is the output of the
USART transmitter clock. No clock pulses are sent to the CK pin during start bit and stop bit. Depending on the state
of the LBCL bit in the USART_SETUP register clock pulses will or will not be generated during the last valid data bit
(address mark). The CPOL bit in the USART_SETUP register allows the user to select the clock polarity, and the CPHA
bit in the USART_SETUP register allows the user to select the phase of the external clock. During idle, the external CK
clock is not activated. In synchronous mode the USART transmitter works exactly like in asynchronous mode. But as
CK is synchronized with TX (according to CPOL and CPHA), the data on TX is synchronous. In this mode the USART
receiver works in a different manner compared to the asynchronous mode. If RXEN=1, the data is sampled on CK (rising
or falling edge, depending on CPOL and CPHA).
The USART supports master mode only: it cannot receive or send data related to an input clock (CK is always an output).
Fig. 16.4: USART example of synchronous trasmission
16.3. Functional Description 241
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 16.5: USART data clock timing diagram (8bit)
Fig. 16.6: USART data clock timing diagram (9bit)
Slave mode
The synchronous slave mode is selected by programming the SLV_EN bit in the UART_SPI_SLAVE (0x34) register to
‘1’.
In this mode, the USART can be used to control bidirectional synchronous serial communications in slave mode. The
SCLK pin is the input of the USART in slave mode.
Note: When the peripheral is used in SPI slave mode, the frequency of peripheral clock source (icb_clk) must be greater
than 3 times the CK input frequency.
The CPOL bit and the CPHA bit in the USART_CR2 register are used to select the clock polarity and the phase of the
external clock, respectively (as figure show below).
16.3. Functional Description 242
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 16.7: USART data clock timing diagram in synchronous slave mode (8 data bits mode)
An underrun error flag is available in slave transmission mode. This flag is set when the first clock pulse for data trans mission appears while the software has not yet loaded any value to USART_TXFIFO.
The slave supports the hardware and software NSS management.
Slave Select (NSS) pin management
The hardware or software slave select management can be set through the DIS_NSS bit in the UART_SPI_SLAVE (0x34)
register:
• Software NSS management (DIS_NSS = 1)
The SPI slave is always selected and NSS input pin is ignored.
• Hardware NSS management (DIS_NSS = 0)
The SPI slave selection depends on NSS input pin. The slave is selected when NSS is low and deselected when NSS is
high.
Note: When USART is woking in spi slave mode, The CTS input pin is reused as NSS pin.
SPI Slave underrun error
An underrun error may occur if the moment the data is written to the USART_TXFIFO is too close to the first SCLK
transmission edge.
To avoid this underrun error, the USART_TXFIFO’s data entry should be prepared when the first SCLK edge is coming.
16.3.8 Single-wire Half-duplex Communication
The USART can be configured to follow a single-wire half-duplex protocol. In single-wire half-duplex mode, the TX and
RX pins are connected internally. The selection between half and full-duplex communication is made with a control bit
HDSEL in USART_SETUP.
As soon as HDSEL is written to ‘1’:
• The TX and RX lines are internally connected,
• The RX pin is no longer used,
16.3. Functional Description 243
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• The TX pin is always released when no data is transmitted. Thus, it acts as a standard I/O in idle or in reception.
It means that the I/O must be configured so that TX is configured as alternate function open-drain with an external
pull-up.
Apart from this, the communication protocol is similar to normal USART mode.Any conflict on the line must be managed
by the software (for instance by using a centralized arbiter). In particular, the transmission is never blocked by hardware
and continues as soon as data are written in the data register while the TXEN bit is set.
16.3.9 Continuous Communication Using DMA
The USART is capable of performing continuous communications using the DMA. The DMA requests for Rx buffer and
TX buffer are generated independently.

Fig. 16.8: USART dma transfer flow diagram
Transmission using DMA
DMA mode can be enabled for transmission by setting DMA EN bit in the USART_SETUP[15] register. Data are
loaded from a SRAM area configured using the DMA peripheral (refer to the corresponding DMA section) to the US ART_TXFIFO whenever the FIFO is empty, DMA-Core will access transmit-data through ICB bus.
Reception using DMA
DMA mode can be enabled for reception by setting the DMA EN bit in USART_SETUP[15] register. Data are loaded from
the USART_RXFIFO to a SRAM area configured using the DMA peripheral (refer to the corresponding DMA section)
whenever a data byte is received, DMA-Core will access receive-data through ICB bus.
TX and RX can work in CPU mode or DMA mode independently
Transmission and Reception can be worked in CPU or DMA independently by setting the SEPARATE EN bit in US ART_SETUP[2] register. In this mode, the transmission is enabled by DMA EN bit in USART_SETUP[15] register, the
reception is enabled by RX DMA EN bit in USART_SETUP[20].
Note
You should refer to sub-system specs for availability of the DMA controller. If DMA is not available in the sub-system,
you should use the USART as explained in Section 6.1 or 6.2, which is CPU mode for transmitter or receiver.
16.3.10 Hardware Flow Control
It is possible to control the serial data flow between 2 devices by using the CTS input and the RTS output. Hardware flow
control between two USARTs (page 245) shows how to connect 2 devices in this mode:

RTS and CTS flow control can be enabled independently by writing the RTSE and CTSE bits to ‘1’ in the USART_SETUP
register.
RTS flow control
If the RTS flow control is enabled (RTSE=1), then RTS is asserted (tied low) as long as the USART receiver is ready to
receive new data. When the receive register is full, RTS is deasserted, indicating that the transmission is expected to stop
at the end of the current frame. RTS flow control (page 245) shows an example of communication with RTS flow control
enabled.

Fig. 16.10: RTS flow control
CTS flow control
If the CTS flow control is enabled (CTSE=1), then the transmitter checks the CTS input before transmitting the next frame.
If CTS is asserted (tied low), then the next data is transmitted (assuming that a data is to be transmitted, in other words, if
TXE=0), else the transmission does not occur. When CTS is deasserted during a transmission, the current transmission is
completed before the transmitter stops.
When CTSE=1, the status bit (CTS_RISE_FLAG and CTS_FALL_FLAG) is automatically set by hardware as soon as the
CTS input toggles. It indicates when the receiver becomes ready or not ready for communication. An interrupt is generated
if the interrupt enable bit in the USART_INT_EN register is set. The figure below shows an example of communication
with CTS flow control enabled.
