16.3.11 RS485
The USART controllers support RS485 standard. This standard uses differential signals to transmit data, so it can com municate over longer distances at higher bit rates than RS232.
Fig. 16.12: Driver Control Diagram in RS485 Mode
Driver Control
As shown in figure above, in a two-wire multidrop network, an external RS485 transceiver is needed for differential to
single-ended conversion. A RS485 transceiver contains a driver and a receiver.
To reduce cost of software, in our design DE is controlled by hardware. DE is connected to RTS of USART.
Turnaround Delay
When a UART controller is switched to transmitter mode, the RS485 protocol requires a turnaround delay of at least one
cycle after the stop bit.
The transmitter supports turnaround active one cycles before start bit or delay of one cycles added after the stop bit.
When DE_ASSERT_EARLY_EN register (0x1C[22]) is set, The transmitter’s DE will active one cycles added before the
start bit.
When DE_DEASSERT_EARLY_EN register (0x1C[23]) is set, The transmitter’s DE will delay one cycles added after
the stop bit.
16.3. Functional Description 246
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
16.3.12 USART Smartcard mode
Smartcard mode is selected by setting the SMARTCARD_EN bit in the USART_SETUP register (0x1C).
The SMARTCARD_EN bit can also be set to provide a clock to the Smartcard.
The Smartcard interface is designed to support asynchronous Smartcard protocol as defined in the ISO 7816-3 standard.
Both T=0 (character mode) and T=1 (block mode) are supported.
The USART should be configured as:
• 8 bit plus parity.
• 1.5 stop bits when transmitting and receiving data. It is also possible to choose 0.5 stop bit for reception.
In T=0 (character) mode, the parity error is indicated at the end of each character during the guard time period.
Fig. 16.13: ISO 7816-3 asynchronous protocol
This figure shows examples of what can be seen on the data line with and without parity error.
When connected to a Smartcard, the TX output of the USART drives a bidirectional line that is also driven by the Smartcard.
The TX pin must be configured as open drain.
Smartcard mode implements a single wire half duplex communication protocol.
• In transmission, if the Smartcard detects a parity error, it signals this condition to the USART by driving the line low
(NACK). This NACK signal (pulling transmit line low for 1 baud clock) causes a framing error on the transmitter
side (configured with 1.5 stop bits). The USART can handle automatic re-sending of data according to the protocol.
The number of retries is programmed in the AUTO_RETRY_CNT bitfield in the SMARTCARD_SETUP (0x60)
register. If the USART continues receiving the NACK after the programmed number of retries, it stops transmitting
and signals the error as a framing error (TX_FRAME_ERR_FLAG bitfield, UART_STATUS register).
• Smartcard auto-retry in transmission: A delay of 2.5 baud periods is inserted between the NACK detection by the
USART and the start bit of the repeated character. If the software wants to repeat it again, it must insure the minimum
2 baud periods required by the standard.
• In transmission, the USART inserts the Guard Time (as programmed in the SMARTCARD_TIMING register) be tween two successive characters. As the Guard Time is measured after the stop bit of the previous character.
• If a framing error is detected on the transmitter end (due to a NACK from the receiver), the NACK is not detected as
a start bit by the receive block of the transmitter. According to the ISO protocol, the duration of the received NACK
can be 1 or 2 baud clock periods.
• On the receiver side, if a parity error is detected and a NACK is transmitted the receiver does not detect the NACK
as a start bit.
• In T=1 (block) mode, the parity error transmission can be deactivated by clearing the NACK bit in the SMART CARD_SETUP register.
Smartcard clk
The USART can provide a clock to the Smartcard through the SCLK output. In Smartcard mode, SCLK is not associated
to the communication but is simply derived from the internal peripheral input clock through a 8-bit prescaler. The division
ratio is configured in the SMARTCARD_TIMING register. SCLK frequency can be programmed from 0x0 to 0xff.
Direct and inverse convention
16.3. Functional Description 247
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
The Smartcard protocol defines two conventions: direct and inverse.
The direct convention is defined as: LSB first, logical bit value of 1 corresponds to a H state of the line and parity is even.
In order to use this convention, the following control bits must be programmed: MSBFIRST=0, DATAINV=0 (default
values).
The inverse convention is defined as: MSB first, logical bit value 1 corresponds to an L state on the signal line and parity
is even. In order to use this convention, the following control bits must be programmed: MSBFIRST=1, DATAINV=1.
Note: When logical data values are inverted (0=H, 1=L), the parity bit is also inverted in the same way.
In order to recognize the card convention, the card sends the initial character, TS, as the first character of the ATR (Answer
To Reset) frame. The two possible patterns for the TS are: LHHL LLL LLH and LHHL HHH LLH.
• (H) LHHL LLL LLH sets up the inverse convention: state L encodes value 1 and moment 2 conveys the most
significant bit (MSB first). When decoded by inverse convention, the conveyed byte is equal to ‘3F’.
• (H) LHHL HHH LLH sets up the direct convention: state H encodes value 1 and moment 2 conveys the least
significant bit (LSB first). When decoded by direct convention, the conveyed byte is equal to ‘3B’.
Character parity is correct when there is an even number of bits set to 1 in the nine moments 2 to 10.
As the USART does not know which convention is used by the card, it needs to be able to recognize either pattern and
act accordingly. The pattern recognition is not done in hardware, but through a software sequence. Moreover, supposing
that the USART is configured in direct convention (default) and the card answers with the inverse convention, TS = LHHL
LLL LLH => the USART received character is 03 and the parity is odd.
Method 1
The USART is programmed in standard Smartcard mode/direct convention. In this case, the TS pattern reception generates
a parity error interrupt and error signal to the card.
• The parity error interrupt informs the software that the card did not answer correctly in direct convention. Software
then reprograms the USART for inverse convention.
• In response to the error signal, the card retries the same TS character, and it is correctly received this time, by the
reprogrammed USART.
Alternatively, in answer to the parity error interrupt, the software may decide to reprogram the USART and to also generate
a new reset command to the card, then wait again for the TS.
Method 2
The USART is programmed in 9-bit/no-parity mode, no bit inversion. In this mode it receives any of the two TS patterns
as:
• (H) LHHL LLL LLH = 0x103 -> inverse convention to be chosen
• (H) LHHL HHH LLH = 0x13B -> direct convention to be chosen
The software checks the received character against these two patterns and, if any of them match, then programs the USART
accordingly for the next character reception.
If none of the two is recognized, a card reset may be generated in order to restart the negotiation.
16.3.13 USART LIN(local interconnection network mode)
The LIN mode is selected by setting the LIN_EN bit in UART_ADVANCED_SETUP register (0x7c).
LIN transmission
The LIN mode needs to configure 8-bit word length and set LIN_EN bit to enter LIN mode.Setting the LIN_SBKF bit in
UART_ADVANCED_STATUS register (0x80) sends 13-bit 0 as a break character. Then two bits of value 1 are sent to
enable the next start detection.
LIN reception
When LIN mode is enabled, the break detection circuit is activated. A break can be detected whenever it occurs, during
idle state or during a frame.
When the receiver is enabled, the circuit looks at the RX input for a start signal. The method for detecting start bits is the
same when searching break characters or data. If 10 (when the LIN_LBDL = 0 in UART_ADVANCED_SETUP register)
16.3. Functional Description 248
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
or 11 (when the LIN_LBDL = 1 in UART_ADVANCED_SETUP register) consecutive bits are detected as 0, and followed
by a delimiter character, the LIN_LBDF bit flag is set in UART_ADVANCED_STATUS register. If LIN_LBDIE_IRQ_EN
bit =1 (in UART_INT_EN register), an interrupt is generated. Before validating the break, the delimiter is checked for as
it signifies that the RX line has returned to high level.
If a 1 is sampled before the 10 or 11 have occurred, the break detection circuit cnacels the current detection and searched
for a start bit again.
If the LIN mode is disabled (LIN_EN=0), the receiver continues working as normal USART, without taking into account
the break detection.
When the receiver detects a break character, the FRAME_ERR_FLAG bit in UART_STATUS register be set at the begin ning of the delimiter.
The USART treats the break character as data 0 and inputs it into the rx FIFO.
16.4 Interrupts
USART Interrupt Requests (page 249) presents the USART interrupt requests.
Table 16.3: USART Interrupt Requests
Interrupt event Event flag Enable control bit Description
RX FIFO watermark rxip rxie Interrupt occurs when RX FIFO depth
exceeds the watermark level.
TX FIFO watermark txip txie Interrupt occurs when TX FIFO depth
less than the watermark level.
Parity checking error rx_error_parity parity error en Interrupt occurs when data parity error is
happened in receive mode.
RX FIFO overflow rx_error_overflow overflow error en Interrupt occurs when RX FIFO is full
and data is still being transferred.
Cts rise flag Cts rise ctsie rise en Interrupt occurs when the CTS signal
rises.
Cts fall flag Cts fall ctsie fall en Interrupt occurs when the CTS signal
falls.
RX frame error RX frame error rx frame error en Interrupt occurs when the RX line is low
level in stop bit phase.
RX stop bit error RX stop bit error rx stop bit error en Interrupt occurs when the stop bit receiv ing data detects an error.
Rx idle timeout rx idle tout status rx idle tout irq en Interrupt occurs when the receive FIFO
in not empty, and the time of RX IDLE
is bigger than RX_IDLE_TOUT_NUM
register.
Rx watermark time out
rx watermark tout rx watermark tout irq en Interrupt occurs when the time of RX
watermark interrupt is bigger than
RX_WM_TOUT_NUM register.
TX end of transfer tx eot status tx eot irq en Interrupt occurs when the data transfer is
complete.
RX end of transfer rx eot status rx eot irq en Interrupt occurs when the data reception
is complete.
The interrupt mapping is shown in USART interrupt mapping diagram (page 250).

Address offset: 0x14.
Table 16.10: Register Format of UART_STATUS
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
TXIP 0 RO 0x0 Transmit watermark interrupt pending
RXIP 1 RO 0x0 Receive watermark interrupt pending
TX_BUSY 2 RO 0x0 TX busy status flag:
-0: No TX transfer on-going
-1: TX transfer on-going
RX_BUSY 3 RO 0x0 RX busy status flag:
-0: No RX transfer on-going
-1: RX transfer on-going
RX_ERROR_OVER_FLOW 4 W1C 0x0 RX overflow error status flag(by writing 1 clr 0):
-0: No error
-1: RX overflow error occurred
RX_ERROR_PARITY 5 W1C 0x0 RX parity error status flag(by writing 1 clr 0):
-0: No error
-1: RX parity error occurred
CTS_RISE_FLAG 6 W1C 0x0 CTS rise flag:
-0: No rise change(from 0 to 1) occurred on the CTS
status line
-1: A rise change(from 0 to 1) occurred on the CTS
status line
CTS_FALL_FLAG 7 W1C 0x0 CTS fall flag:
-1’b0: No fall change(from 1 to 0) occurred on the
CTS status line
-1’b1: A fall change(from 1 to 0) occurred on the
CTS status line
STOP_BIT_ERR_FLAG 8 W1C 0x0 If rx line is low level in stop_bit mode, there will be
a stop_bit error status and write 1 to clear this status.
Reserved 11:9 / 0x0 /
RX_IDLE_TOUT_STATUS 12 W1C 0x0 The receive timeout interrupt is asserted when the
receive fifo in not empty, and no more data is receive
during a RX_IDLE_TOUT_NUM.
RX_WM_TOUT_STATUS 13 W1C 0x0 The rx watermark timeout interrupt is asserted when
the time of rx watermark interrupt is asseted than
RX_WM_TOUT_NUM register
TX_FIFO_FULL 14 RO 0x0 Transmit FIFO full, the status bit is cleared when US ART had fetched tx-data from transmit-fifo.
RX_FIFO_EMPTY 15 RO 0x1 Receive FIFO empty, the status bit is cleared when
USART had receive first rx-data successfully.
TX_EOT 16 W1C 0x0 TX transfer done status
RX_EOT 17 W1C 0x0 RX transfer done status
SPI_SLV_UNDER_RUN 18 W1C 0x0 In slave transmission mode, this flag is set when the
first clock pulse for data transmission
appears while the software has not yet loaded any
value into TX_FIFO.
SPI_SLV_OVER_RUN 19 W1C 0x0 In slave receive mode, this flag is set when the first
clock pulse for data transmission
appears while RX_FIFO is full already.
CTS_PIN 20 RO 0x0 This bit is set/reset by hardware. It is the status of
the CTS input pin.
-0: CTS pin is low.
-1: CTS pin is high.
TX_FIFO_EMPTY 21 RO 0x1 Transmit FIFO empty, the status bit is cleared when
soft-ware had sent first tx-data into transmit-fifo.

Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description

FRAME_ERR_FLAG 23 W1C 0x0 When transmitting data in Smartcard mode, this bit
is set when the maximum number of transmit at tempts is reached without success (the card NACKs

-0: No Framing error is detected/No break field

16.5.1.7 UART_DIV
Address offset: 0x18.
Table 16.11: Register Format of UART_DIV
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
BAUD_DIV 20:0 RW 0x0 Baud_rate = fusart_clk/(BAUD_DIV+1).
If fraction baud generator is disable, the
BAUD_DIV[18:0] is real divider for software.
If fraction baud generator is enable, the BAUD_DIV[20:3]
is mantissa part of real divider, the BAUD_DIV[2:0] is
fractional part,
which can be setted as below:
-3’b000: 0
-3’b001: 0.125
-3’b010: 0.25
-3’b011: 0.375
-3’b100: 0.5
-3’b101: 0.625
-3’b110: 0.75
-3’b111: 0.875
Reserved 31:21 / 0x0 /
Note
The BAUD_DIV[20:0] should not be setted as 0 or 1 whether the fraction baud generator is enable or not. Here are
two main reasons as below:
1. The counter in USART will be underflow when the BAUD_DIV[21:0] register is setted as 0.
2. There is noise detection logic for sampled data, so the RX length should be bigger than 2 USART’s clk and the
BAUD_DIV[21:0] register should not be setted as 1.

Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
16.5.1.8 UART_SETUP
Address offset: 0x1C.
Table 16.12: Register Format of UART_SETUP
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
PARITY_EN 0 RW 0x0 Parity bit generation and check configuration
bitfield:
-1’b0: Disabled parity bit generation and
check
-1’b1: Enabled parity bit generation and check
PARITY_SEL 1 RW 0x0 Parity selection
-1’b0: even parity
-1’b1: odd parity
SEPARATE_EN 2 RW 0x0 Enable this bit can make tx and rx worked in
cpu or dma mode independently
-1’b0: Both tx and rx is controled by the

-1’b1: The rx control by RX_DMA_EN bit
USART_SETUP[20] in register, and tx control
by DMA_EN in USART_SETUP[15] Register
Reserved 3 / 0x0 /
BIT_LENGTH 6:4 RW 0x3 Character length bitfield:
-3’b000 : 5 bits
-3’b001 : 6 bits
-3’b010 : 7 bits
-3’b011 : 8 bits
-3’b100 : 9 bits
CLK_EN 7 RW 0x0 Clock enable: this bit allows the user to enable
the CK pin
-1’b0: CK pin disabled
-1’b1: CK pin enabled
Only when TX mode and clk_en =1 ,tx_clk_o
can output.
CPOL 8 RW 0x0 Clock polarity: this bit allows the user to select
the polarity of the clock output on the CK pin
in synchronous mode.
It works in conjunction with CPHA bit to pro duce the desired clock/data
-1’b0: Steady LOW: Steady low value on CK
pin outside transmission window.
-1’b1: Steady HIGH: Steady high value on CK
pin outside transmission window.
CPHA 9 RW 0x0 Clock phase: this bit allows the user to select
the phase of the clock output on the CK pin in
synchronous mode.
It works in conjunction with
CPOL bit to produce the desired
clock/data relationship
-1’b0: First transition: The first clock transi tion is the first data caputure edge.
-1’b1: Second transition:The scond clock tran sition is the first data caputre edge

Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 16.12 – continued from previous page
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
LBCL 10 RW 0x0 Last bit clock pulse: this bit allows the user to
select whether the clock pulse assocaited with
the
Last data bit transmitted(MSB) has to be out put on the CK pin in synchronous mode.
-1’b0: Not output: The clock pulse of the last
data bit is not output to the CK pin
-1’b1: Output: The clock pulse of the last data
bit is output to the CK pin
HDSEL 11 RW 0x0 HDSEL: simplex model selection
-1’b0: Disable: simplex model is not selected
-1’b1: Enable: simplex model is selected
SMARTCARD_EN 12 RW 0x0 Smartcard mode enable
This bit is used for enabling Smartcard mode.
-1’b0: Smartcard Mode disabled
-1’b1: Smartcard Mode enabled
RTSE 13 RW 0x0 RTS enable:
-1’b0: RTS hardware flow control disabled
-1’b1: RTS mode enable, data is only re quested when there is space in the RX FIFO,
The transmission of data is expected to cease
after the current character has been transmit ted.
The RTS output is asserted(tie to 0)when a data
can be received.
CTSE 14 RW 0x0 CTS enable:
-1’b0: CTS hardware flow control disable
-1’b1: CTS mode enable, data is only trans mitted when the CTS input is asserted(tied to
0).
If the CTS input is deasserted while a data is
being transmitted, the the transmission is com plete before stopping.
If a data is written into the RX FIFO while
CTS is deasserted, the transmission is post poned until CTS is asserted.
DMA_EN 15 RW 0x0 DMA_EN:select mode
-1’b0: cpu mode, tx data come from icb or
other bus
-1’b1: dma mode, tx data come frome dma
Note: this bit control rx channel is based
on SEPARATE_EN register, when SEPA RATE_EN is enable, this bit is used for tx channle only
VOTE_MAJORITY_EN 16 RW 0x1 Enable the 2/3 majority logic in data frame
-1’b0: Turn OFF 2/3 majority logic
-1’b1: Turn ON 2/3 majority logic
SAMPLE_POSITION 18:17 RW 0x2 When real div is even, this sampling position
in data frame can be selected:
-2’b00: Dither: One dataframe is upward, the
other one is downward at the two adjacent
frames
-2’b01: Downward
-2’b10: Upward

Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
FRACTION_BAUD 19 RW 0x0 Fractional baud rate

-1’b1: turn on fraction baud generator
RX_DMA_EN 20 RW 0x0 Rx channel dma mode enable, when SEPA RATE_EN bit is enable, this bit is assert or de asserted by soft-ware;

-1’b0: cpu mode

-1’b1: enable RS-485 mode
DE_ASSERTION_EARLY_EN 22 RW 0x0 Enable RS-485’s DE signal assert before

-1’b1: DE will assert before START condition

DE_DEASSERTION_DELAY_EN 23 RW 0x0 Enable RS-485’s DE signal dessert after STOP

-1’b0: DE will assert when STOP condition
happened
-1’b1: DE will assert after STOP condition one
bit-time later
DE_INV 24 RW 0x0 Enable RS-485’s DE signal polarity
-1’b0: DE pin signal works using the standard
logic levels (VDD =1/idle, Gnd=0/mark)
-1’b1: DE pin signal values are inverted.
((VDD =0/mark, Gnd=1/idle).
MSBFIRST 25 RW 0x0 Most significant bit first. This bit is set and
cleared by software.
-1’b0: data is transmitted/received with data
bit 0 first, following the start bit.
-1’b1: data is transmitted/received with the
MSB (bit 7/8) first, following the start bit.
DATAINV 26 RW 0x0 Binary data inversion. This bit is set and
cleared by software.
-1’b0: Logical data from the data register are
send/received in positive/direct logic. (1=H,
0=L)
-1’b1: Logical data from the data register are
send/received in negative/inverse logic. (1=L,
0=H). The
parity bit is also inverted.
TXINV 27 RW 0x0 TX pin active level inversion. This bit is set
and cleared by software.
-1’b0: TX pin signal works using the standard
logic levels (VDD =1/idle, Gnd=0/mark)
-1’b1: TX pin signal values are inverted.
((VDD =0/mark, Gnd=1/idle).

Field Bit Ac cess

Value
Description
AUTO_RETRY_CNT 2:0 RW 0x0 Smartcard auto-retry count
This bitfield specifies the number of retries for
transmission and reception in Smartcard mode.
In transmission mode, it specifies the number of
automatic retransmission retries,
before generating a transmission error
(TX_FRAME_ERR_FLAG, status[23]).

WWDG(0/1)
This section applies to instantiation of u_wwdg0, u_wwdg1
17.1 Introduction
The Nuclei WWDG (Window Watchdog) is used to detect the occurrence of a software fault, usually generated by external
interference or by unforeseen logical conditions, which causes the application program to abandon its normal sequence.
The watchdog circuit generates an MCU reset on expiry of a programmed time period, unless the program refreshes
the contents of the downcounter before the T[21:6] bit becomes cleared. An MCU reset is also generated if the 22-bit
downcounter value (in the control register) is refreshed before the downcounter has reached the window register value.
This implies that the counter must be refreshed in a limited window.
The block diagram of the WWDG is shown in WWDG block diagram. (page 266).
Fig. 17.1: WWDG block diagram.
17.2 Main Features
The main features of WWDG are the following:
• Programmable free-running downcounter.
• Conditional reset:
– Reset (if watchdog activated) when the downcounter value becomes less than 0x40.
– Reset (if watchdog activated) if the downcounter is reloaded outside the window.
266
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• Early wakeup interrupt (EWI): triggered (if enabled and the watchdog activated) when the downcounter is equal to
0x40.
• Counter freeze in JTAG debug mode.
17.3 Functional Description
If the watchdog is activated (the WDGA bit is set in the WWDG_CR register) and when the 22-bit downcounter (T[21:0]
bits) rolls over from 0x40 to 0x3F (T[21:6] becomes cleared), it initiates a reset. If the software reloads the counter while
the counter is greater than the value stored in the window register, then a reset is generated.
The application program must write in the WWDG_CR register at regular intervals during normal operation to prevent an
MCU reset. This operation must occur only when the counter value is lower than the window register value.
The WWDG_CFR register cannot be changed when WWDG is runing, If the application want change WWDG_CFR
register value, The procedure should be happened after WWDG module is reseted by icb_rstn, which maybe generated by
sub-system’s Reset-Control-Unit or WWDG’reset. The value to be stored in the WWDG_CFR register must be greater
than 0x40.
17.3.1 Enabling The Watchdog
The watchdog is always disabled after a reset. It is enabled by setting the WDGA bit in the WWDG_CR register, then it
cannot be disabled again except by a reset.
17.3.2 Controlling The Downcounter
When the watchdog is enabled, the T[21:0] bit must be set to prevent generating an immediate reset. The T[21:0] bits
contain the number of increments which represents the time delay before the watchdog produces a reset. The timing varies
between a minimum and a maximum value due to the unknown status of the prescaler when writing to the WWDG_CR
register (see Figure WWDG timing diagram (page 268)). The Configuration register (WWDG_CFR) contains the high limit
of the window: To prevent a reset, the downcounter must be reloaded when its value is lower than the window register
value and greater than 0x3F. Figure WWDG timing diagram (page 268) describes the window watchdog process.
17.3.3 Advanced Watchdog Interrupt Feature
The Early Wakeup Interrupt (EWI) can be used if specific safety operations or data logging must be performed before the
actual reset is generated. The EWI interrupt is enabled by setting the EWI bit in the WWDG_CFR register. When the
downcounter reaches the value 0x40, an EWI interrupt is generated and the corresponding interrupt service routine (ISR)
can be used to trigger specific actions (such as communications or data logging), before resetting the device.
In some applications, the EWI interrupt can be used to manage a software system check and/or system recovery/graceful
degradation, without generating a WWDG reset. In this case, the corresponding interrupt service routine (ISR) should
reload the WWDG counter to avoid the WWDG reset, then trigger the required actions.
The EWI interrupt is cleared by writing ‘0’ to the EWIF bit in the WWDG_SR register.
17.3.4 How To Program The Watchdog Timeout
You can use the formula in Figure WWDG timing diagram (page 268) to calculate the WWDG timeout.
Warning
When writing to the WWDG_CR register, always make T[21:6] is not 0x0 to avoid generating an immediate reset.
17.3. Functional Description 267
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 17.2: WWDG timing diagram
The formula to calculate the timeout value is given by:
twwdg = twwdg_clk * 4096 * 2^WDGTB[2:0] * (T[21:0]+1)
where:
twwdg: WWDG timeout
twwdg_clk: The clk of WWDG and measured in ms
4096: value corresponding to internal divider
As an example, let us assume ICB_CLK frequency is equal to 24 MHz, WDGTB[2:0] is set to 3 and T[21:0] is set to 63:
tWWDG = (1/24000) * 4096 * 2^3 * (63 + 1) = 87.38ms
17.3.5 Debug Mode
When the sub-system enters the debug mode, the WWDG counter either continues to work normally or stops, depending
on the status of interface (dbg_wwdg_stop).
17.4 Interrupts
WWDG Interrupt Requests (page 268) presents the wwdg interrupt requests.
Table 17.1: WWDG Interrupt Requests
Interrupt event Enable control bit
EWIF EWI
17.4. Interrupts 268

XKAN(0/1)
This section applies to instantiation of u_xkan0, u_xkan1
18.1 Introduction
The XKAN module is a communication controller implementing the CAN protocol specification version 2.0 part A, B and
CAN with Flexible Data-Rate protocol specification version 1.0.
There is a 8Kbyte ram in XKAN subsystem, the ram implements filters for accept CAN base format (2.0 A) and extended
format (2.0 B), receive buffers and transmit buffers. The number of this all function components can be configured by
XKAN register.
The Nuclei XKAN subsystem is shown in XKAN Block Diagram (page 271):.
Fig. 18.1: XKAN Block Diagram
Note: The size of Message RAM is 2048*32
271
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
18.2 Main Features
The main features of XKAN are the following:
• Supports CAN protocol version 2.0 A, B and CAN with Flexible Data-Rate protocol specification version 1.0
– Standard data frames
– Extended data frames
– Remote frames
– Zero to sixty-four bytes data length
– Programmable bit rate
• Compatible with ISO 11898-1 standard
• CAN error logging
• Improved acceptance filtering
• Two configurable receive FIFOs
• Up to 64 dedicated receive buffers, each receive buffer canbe configurable to store 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 32, 48 or 64
bytes data length
• Up to 32 dedicated transmit buffers, each transmit buffer canbe configurable to store 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 32, 48 or 64
bytes data length
• Configurable transmit FIFO
• Transmission abort capability
• Programmable loop-back test mode
• Time stamp based on 16-bit free-running timer
• Transceiver Delay Compensation feature when transmitting CAN with Flexible Data-Rate messages at faster data
rates
• Maskable module interrupts
• Two clock domains:
– ICB_CLK: XKAN subsystem ICB interface clock input
– XKAN_CLK: XKAN subsystem kernel clock input
• Power-down support
18.3 Functional Description
The XKAN module is a CAN protocol engine with a very flexible ram-buffers system for transmitting and receiving
CAN/CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frames. The mailbox system consists of a set of message buffers that store configuration
and control data, time stamp, message ID and data.
For Classical CAN frames(CAN2.0 A/B) and CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frames, simultaneous transmit and reception
through ram-buffers is supported. For mailbox reception, a matching algorithm makes it possible to store received frames
into ram-buffers that have the same ID programmed in the ID field. A masking scheme makes it possible to match the ID
programmed on the ram-buffers with a range of IDs on received CAN frames. For transmission, an arbitration algorithm
decides the prioritization of ram-buffers to be transmitted based on the CAN message ID. When receive Classical CAN
frames or CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frames, the ram-buffers are sized to adequately store the quantity of data bytes
selected in the filter fields. The quantity of CAN or CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frame ram-buffers is described in
Reception process. See also in Message RAM configuraton.
18.2. Main Features 272
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
18.3.1 XKAN general description
In today’s CAN with Flexible Data-Rate applications, the number of nodes in a network is increasing and often several
networks are linked together via gateways. Typically the number of messages in the system (and thus to be handled by each
node) has significantly increased. In addition to the application messages, Network Management and Diagnostic messages
have been introduced.
• An enhanced filtering mechanism is required to handle each type of message.
Furthermore, application tasks require more CPU time, therefore real-time constraints caused by message reception have
to be reduced.
• A transmit and receive buffer scheme allows the CPU to be dedicated to application tasks for a long time period
without losing messages.
The standard HLP (Higher Layer Protocol) based on standard CAN with Flexible Data-Rate drivers requires an efficient
interface to the CAN with Flexible Data-Rate controller.
Fig. 18.2: XKAN network topology
18.3.2 XKAN work mode
The XKAN module has the following work modes:
Function mode
In function mode, the XKAN operates receiving and/or transmitting message frames, errors are managed normally, and
all CAN protocol functions are enabled.
Test mode
In test mode, the XKAN should be used for production tests or self test only. The software control for XKAN_TX pin
interferes with all CAN protocol functions. It is not recommended to use test modes for application.
18.3.3 CAN Protocol Related Features
This section describes the CAN protocol related features.
18.3.3.1 CAN with Flexible Data-Rate Frames
The ISO 11898-1 standard specifies the Classical Frame format compliant to ISO 11898-1(2003) and introduces the CAN
Flexible Data Rate Frame format. The Classical CAN frame format allows bit rates up to 1 Mbit/s and payloads up to 8
byte per frame. The Flexible Data Rate frame format allows bit rates higher than 1 Mbit/s and payloads longer than 8 byte
per frame. The XKAN CAN receive and transmit CAN with Flexible Data-Rate messages interleaved with Classical CAN
messages.
There are three additional control bits in the CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frame:
• The Extended Data Length (EDL) bit enables a longer data payload with different data length coding.
18.3. Functional Description 273
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• The Bit Rate Switch (BRS) bit decides whether the bit rate is switched inside a CAN with Flexible Data-Rate format
frame.
• The Error State Indicator (ESI) flag is transmitted dominant by error active nodes, and recessive by error passive
nodes.
There are no remote frames in the CAN with Flexible Data-Rate format. A message configured to transmit a remote frame
is always sent out in the Classical CAN format. When RTR bit is recessive in a transmit or receive with Flexible Data-Rate
frame, XKAN also support send and receive this illegal frame. The RTR bit must be considered in Classical CAN frames
only.
CAN with Flexible Data-Rate messages may be formatted as long frames, in which the data field exceeds 8 bytes and may
range from 12 up to 64 bytes. CAN with Flexible Data-Rate messages can also be configured to support bit rate switching,
where the control field, the data field, and the CRC field of a CAN frame are transmitted with a higher bit rate than the
beginning and the end of the frame. Messages in Classical CAN format are limited to transport a maximum payload of 8
bytes at nominal rate. The below Figure Bit Fields in CAN Message Formats (page 274): illustrates the message formats
for Classical and with Flexible Data-Rate frames with either standard or extended ID.
Fig. 18.3: Bit Fields in CAN Message Formats
The ability to receive and transmit CAN with Flexible Data-Rate messages is enabled by the CFG_MCR.FD_EN bit. Either
a recessive R0 bit in CAN frames with 11-bit identifiers or a recessive R1 bit in CAN frames with 29-bit identifiers are
decoded as an EDL bit (not a reserved one). A CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frame is recognized by a recessive EDL bit,
while a Classical CAN frame is recognized by a dominant EDL bit. The BRS bit specifies whether this frame switches the
bit rate in its data phase. A long frame is decoded according to the DLC field value.
CAN with Flexible Data-Rate messages can be transmitted with two different bit rates. The first part of a CAN with Flexible
Data-Rate frame, from the Start Of Frame (SOF) bit until the Bit Rate Switch (BRS) bit, also called the arbitration phase,
18.3. Functional Description 274
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
is transmitted with the nominal bit rate based on a set of nominal CAN bit timing configuration values. The second part,
from the BRS bit until the CRC Delimiter bit, also named the data phase, is transmitted with the data bit rate defined by a
second set of CAN data bit timing configuration values. Finally, from the CRC Delimiter until the Intermission bits, the
transmission resumes to nominal bit rate. In CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frames with bit rate switching, the bit timing
is changed inside the frame at the sample point of the BRS bit if this bit is recessive. Before the BRS bit, in the CAN with
Flexible Data-Rate arbitration phase, the nominal CAN bit timing is used as defined by the CFG_NBTR register. Upon
detecting a recessive BRS bit, the CAN data bit timing is used as defined by the CFG_DBTR register.
CFG_MCR.BRS_EN enables the transmission of all frames with bit rate switching if the BRS bit in the selected TX BUFFER is set. CFG_MCR.BRS_EN also enable the receive of all frames with bit rate switching if the BRS bit in the
receive can frame is set. The CFG_MCR.FD_EN or CFG_MCR.BRS bits can be written any time but takes effect only for
the next message transmitted or received.
The nominal bit timing is resumed at either the sample point of the CRC Delimiter bit or when an error is detected,
whichever occurs first. The below Figure Bit Rate Switching Mechanism For CAN with Flexible Data-Rate Messages
(page 275): describes the mechanism for entering and leaving the data phase when BRS bit is recessive.
Fig. 18.4: Bit Rate Switching Mechanism For CAN with Flexible Data-Rate Messages
18.3.3.2 CRC Caculate process
There are different CRC polynomials for different CAN frame formats:
• The first polynomial, CRC_15, is used for all frames in Classical CAN format.
• The second, CRC_17, is used for frames in CAN with Flexible Data-Rate format with a data field up to sixteen bytes
long.
• The third, CRC_21, is used for frames in CAN with Flexible Data-Rate format with a data field longer than sixteen
bytes.
Each polynomial results in a Hamming distance of 6. At the start of the frame, all three CRC polynomials are calculated
concurrently. The CRC sequence to be transmitted is selected by the values of the EDL bit and the DLC bit field. When
receiving a message, the XKAN decodes EDL and DLC to select the proper CRC polynomial to check for a CRC error.
In CAN with Flexible Data-Rate format frames, stuff bits are included in the bit stream for CRC calculation. In Classical
CAN format frames, stuff bits are not included. In CAN with Flexible Data-Rate format frames, the CAN bit stuffing
method is changed for the CRC sequence so that the stuff bits are inserted at fixed positions. When the XKAN is transmitting
a CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frame, a fixed stuff bit is inserted just before the first bit of the CRC sequence, even if the
last bits of the preceding field do not fulfill the CAN stuff condition. Additional stuff bits are inserted after each fourth bit
of the CRC sequence. The value of any fixed stuff bit is the inverse value of its preceding bit. When the XKAN is receiving
18.3. Functional Description 275
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
a CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frame, it discards the fixed stuff bits from the bit stream for the CRC check. A Stuff Error
is detected if the fixed stuff bit has the same value as its preceding bit.
The XKAN detects errors in CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frames the same way as in Classical CAN frames. The error
counters REC[7:0] and TEC[7:0] in the CFG_ESR register accumulate the counts of Rx and Tx errors, respectively, for
both Flexible Data-Rate and non-Flexible Data-Rate frames indiscriminately.
When the XKAN is in the data phase, either transmitting or receiving a CAN with Flexible Data-Rate message, and detects
an error, it immediately switches back to the arbitration phase and to the nominal rate to start an Error Flag.
18.3.3.3 Loss Arbitratioin Process
XKAN will enter the bus integration state after detecting the arbitration lost and shall leave the bus integration state when
the idle conditon is detected.
The bus integration is status of a node waiting on an idle condition after a protocol exception event.
The idle condition is detection of a sequence of eleven consecutive sampled recessive bits.
18.3.3.4 Overload Frames
The XKAN does transmit overload frames when the following conditions are detected on the CAN bus:
• Detection of a dominant bit in the first/second bit of Intermission.
• Detection of a dominant bit at the 7th bit (last) of End of Frame field (Rx frames).
• Detection of a dominant bit at the 8th bit (last) of Error Frame Delimiter or Overload Frame Delimiter.
18.3.3.5 Interframe Spaces
The Interframe Space acts as a separator between frames. Data frames and remote frames must be separated from preceding
frames by an Interframe Space, regardless of the preceding frame¡¯s type (data frame, remote frame, error frame, overload
frame). However, error frames and overload frames do not need to be separated from preceding frames.
When XKAN is work as receiver, the end of frame is followed by interframe.
When XKAN is work as transmitter, after sending end of frame, the interframe will be send.
18.3.3.6 Transceiver Delay Compensation
The CAN with Flexible Data-Rate protocol allows the transmission and reception of data at a higher bit rate than the
nominal rate used in the arbitration phase when the message’s BRS bit is set. This feature enables the use of rates up to 8
Mbps.
During the data phase of a CAN with Flexible Data-Rate frame, the transmitter detects a bit error if it cannot receive its own
latest transmitted bit at the sample point of that bit. When bit rate switching is enabled (BRS bit is asserted), the length
of the CAN bit time in the data phase can become shorter than the transceiver’s loop delay, thus impeding the correct
comparison between the transmitted bit and the received bit within the current CAN bit time interval.
The XKAN supports an optional Transceiver Delay Compensation (TDC) mechanism that defines a secondary sample
point where the transmitted bit is correctly compared with the received bit in order to check for bit errors.
The TDC mechanism is enabled by the CFG_MCR.TDC_EN bit and is effective only during the data phase of CAN with
Flexible Data-Rate frames having the BRS bit set. It has no effect either on non-Flexible Data-Rate frames, or on with
Flexible Data-Rate frames transmitted at normal bit rate. The TDC is active from the sample point of the BRS bit until the
sample point of the CRC Delimiter bit, provided the respective message under transmission has the BRS bit set. When it
is active, a comparison is done between the real received bit and the delayed transmitted bit, where the delay is calculated
based on the measured transceiver loop delay.
18.3. Functional Description 276
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 18.5: Transceiver Loop Delay Measurement
For every transmitted flexible data-rate frame having the BRS bit set, the delay measurement is triggered by the transition
from the recessive EDL bit to the dominant R0 bit (as shown in Figure Transceiver Loop Delay Measurement (page 277)).
The loop delay is measured in XKAN kernel clock periods (XKANCLK), from the transmitted EDL-R0 edge to the
received EDL-R0 edge. The position of the secondary sample point is defined by the measured loop delay time added
to an offset value specified in CFG_TRANS_DELAY_COMP register. The CFG_PSR.TRANS_DELAY_COMP bit field
stores the result of this calculation.
18.3.3.7 Error Signalling
Whenever a bit error, stuff error, form error, or ACK error is detected by XKAN, an error flag shall be started by the
respective node at the next bit. When an error is detected in the data phase of a flexible data-rate frame, the node shall
switch from the data bit time back into the nominal bit time of the arbitration phase before it starts the error flag.
18.3.3.8 Error Type
Bus Errors in XKAN are categorized into one of the following types:
Bit Error
A Bit Error occurs when a node transmits a bit value (i.e., dominant or recessive) but the opposite bit is detected (e.g., a
dominant bit is transmitted but a recessive is detected). However, if the transmitted bit is recessive and is located in the
Arbitration Field or ACK Slot or Passive Error Flag, then detecting a dominant bit will not be considered a Bit Error.
Stuff Error
A stuff error is detected when 6 consecutive bits of the same value are detected (thus violating the bit-stuffing encoding
rules).
CRC Error
A receiver of a data or remote frame will calculate a CRC based on the bits it has received. A CRC error occurs when the
CRC calculated by the receiver does not match the CRC sequence in the received data or remote Frame.
Format Error
A Format Error is detected when a fixed-form bit field of a message contains an illegal bit. For example, the r1 and r0
fields must be dominant.
ACK Error
An ACK Error occurs when a transmitter does not detect a dominant bit at the ACK Slot.
18.3. Functional Description 277
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
18.3.3.9 Restricted operation
XKAN implementations may provide the restricted operation mode, where they shall be able to receive DFs and RFs and
shall give ACK to valid frames, but it shall not send EFs or OFs. In case of an error condition or overload condition, it
shall not send dominant bits; instead it shall treat this as a protocol exception event and shall enter the bus integration state,
In this state, XKAN will be exit by detected 11 consecutive recessive bits. The error counters shall not be incremented or
decremented while the CAN node is in restricted operation mode. The XKAN is set in the restricted-operation mode via
the restricted_operation bit in cfg_mcr register.
In restricted-operation mode, the XKAN is able:
• to transmit or receive data frames
• to transmit or receive remote frames
• to give acknowledge to valid frames in receive mode
This mode does not support:
• error frames sending
• overload frames sending
18.3.4 Main function description
18.3.4.1 XKAN Message RAM
The message RAM has a width of 32 bits. The XKAN module can be configured to allocate up to 2048 words in the message
RAM. It is not necessary to configure each of the sections listed in XKAN Message RAM configuration (page 278), nor is
there any restriction with respect to the sequence of the sections.
Fig. 18.6: XKAN Message RAM configuration
The XKAN and Soft-ware access the message RAM in 32-bit words align, not single bytes. The start address of all function
regions in message RAM is 32-bit word align.
Note: The XKAN does not check for erroneous configuration of the message RAM. Especially the configuration of the start
addresses of the different sections and the number of elements of each section has to be done carefully to avoid falsification
or loss of data.
18.3. Functional Description 278
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
18.3.4.2 Transmission process
The XKAN Tx function handles transmission requests for the dedicated Tx buffers. It controls the transfer of transmit
messages to the CAN core, the put and get Indices. Up to 32 Tx buffers can be set up for message transmission. Depending
on the configuration of the element size (CFG_TXDSIZE), between two and sixteen 32-bit words (CFG_TXDSIZE = 0 . . .
7) are used for storage of a CAN message data field.
Table 18.1: Possible configurations for frame transmission
CFG_MCR[8] CFG_MCR[9] TX buffer refer ence bit
TX buffer refer ence bit
CAN frame type
FD_EN BRS_EN EDL bit BRS bit
0 x Ignored Ignored Classic CAN
1 0 0 Ignored Classic CAN
1 0 1 Ignored CAN with Flexible Data-Rate without
bitrate switching
1 1 0 Ignored Classic CAN
1 1 1 0 CAN with Flexible Data-Rate without
bitrate switching
1 1 1 1 CAN with Flexible Data-Rate with bi trate switching
The Tx function starts a Tx scan to check for the highest priority pending Tx request (Tx buffer with lowest message ID)
when the Tx buffer request pending register CFG_TXBAR is updated, or when a transmission has been started.
18.3.4.2.1 Dedicated Tx buffers
Dedicated Tx buffers are intended for message transmission under complete control of the CPU. Each dedicated Tx buffer is
configured with a specific message ID. The number of dedicated Tx buffers are configured via the TXB_NUM[5:0] field in
the CFG_TXBUF register. Each dedicated Tx buffer is configured with a specific identifier to store only one element. The
transmission is requested by an add request via the CFG_TXBAR register. The requested messages arbitrate externally with
messages on the CAN bus, and are sent out according to the lowest identifier (highest priority). The memory requirements
for the dedicated Tx buffers depend on the Tx buffer element size. The Tx buffer element size defines the number of data
bytes belonging to a Tx buffer.
18.3. Functional Description 279
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 18.7: Transmission with Tx buffer mechanism
A dedicated Tx buffer allocates (n+2) 32-bit words in the message RAM, The n is stand for data-size by CFG_TX_DSIZE
register, 2 is stand for two words of ID and DLC. Therefore the start address of a dedicated Tx buffer in the message RAM
is calculated by adding n times (the number of tx can frame) the transmit buffer index (0. . . 31) to the Tx buffer start address
TXBF_ADDR.CFG_TBUF register.
The address of a dedicated Tx buffer in the CAN message RAM is calculated using the formula below:
Dedicated Tx buffer address = CAN_message_RAM_base_address + CFG_TXBUF.TXBUF_ADDR[10:0] +
Tx_buffer_index (0~32) * Tx_buffer_element_size.
Tx_buffer_element_size = (value of CFG_TXDSIZE.TXB_DSIZE[2:0] / 4) + 2.
The following flow chart is used to simplify the operation of a transmission with the Tx buffer mechanism.
Table 18.2: Each to be sent CAN message size in tx buffer
CFG_TXDSIZE[2:0] DATA FIELD(bytes) CAN message size (RAM words)
000 8 4
001 12 5
010 16 6
011 20 7
100 24 8
101 32 10
110 48 14
111 64 18
18.3. Functional Description 280
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 18.3: The TX buffer structure
BIT [31:24] [23:16] [15:8] [7:0]
MB0 EDL,IDE,RTR,ID[28:24] ID[23:16] ID[15:8] ID[7:0]

Note:
1. When RTR (MB0[29]) = 1, The XKAN transmits a remote frame according to ISO11898-1, even if
CFG_MCR.FD_EN enables the transmission in CAN FD format.
2. The ESI bit of the transmit buffer is OR-ed with the error passive flag to decide the value of the ESI bit in the
transmitted FD frame. As required by the CAN FD protocol specification, an error active node may optionally
transmit the ESI bit recessive, but an error passive node will always transmit the ESI bit recessive.

18.3. Functional Description 281
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
18.3.4.2.2 Tx FIFO
The Tx FIFO operation is configured by writing the CFG_TXBUF.TXFIFO_NUM[5:0]. The elements
stored in the Tx FIFO are transmitted starting with the element referenced by the read pointer via the
CFG_TXFIFO_STATUS.TXFF_RPTR[4:0]. After each transmission, the CFG_TXFIFO_STATUS.TXFF_RPTR[4:0] is
incremented cyclically until the Tx FIFO buffer is empty.
The Tx FIFO enables the transmission of elements in the order these elements have been written to the Tx FIFO. The
transmission is independent of the priority of the respective identifiers because the first/oldest element in the FIFO is sent
out first.
The XCAN calculates the Tx FIFO buffer free level via the CFG_TXFIFO_STATUS.TXFF_ENTRY_NUM[5:0]
as a difference between read pointer (CFG_TXFIFO_STATUS.TXFF_RPTR[4:0]) and write pointer
(CFG_TXFIFO_STATUS.TXFF_WPTR[4:0]). This value indicates the number of available (free) Tx FIFO elements.
New transmit elements must be written to the Tx FIFO starting with the Tx buffer referenced by the write pointer.
The address of the next free Tx FIFO buffer in the CAN message RAM is calculated with the below formula:
Dedicated Tx buffer address = CAN_message_RAM_base_address + CFG_TXBUF.TXBUF_ADDR[10:0] +
TX_FIFO_write_pointer (0~32) * Tx_buffer_element_size.
Tx_buffer_element_size = (value of CFG_TXDSIZE.TXB_DSIZE[2:0] / 4) + 2.
Fig. 18.8: Transmission with Tx FIFO mechanism
18.3.4.2.3 Flexible transmission configuration
An efficient XKAN supports mixed configurations to allow more flexibility on transmissions and to take the best advantage
of each mechanism benefits. The supported mixed configurations are dedicated Tx buffer + Tx FIFO.
18.3. Functional Description 282
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
18.3.4.2.4 Mixed configuration: dedicated Tx buffers and Tx FIFO
The Tx buffer section of the XKAN message RAM can be configured with a mixed configuration, where the Tx buffers
section in the XKAN message RAM is subdivided into a set of dedicated Tx buffers and a Tx FIFO.
The number of dedicated Tx buffers is configured via CFG_TXBUF.TXBUF_NUM[5:0]. The number of Tx buffers
assigned to the Tx FIFO is configured via CFG_TXBUF.TXFIFO_NUM[5:0].
The Tx handler scans all the dedicated Tx buffers with an activated transmission request and the oldest pending Tx FIFO
buffer referenced by the read pointer. The buffer with the lowest identifier gets the highest priority and is transmitted next
A use case using a mixed dedicated Tx buffer and a Tx FIFO is illustrated in the figure below.
Fig. 18.9: Tx scan with Tx buffers and Tx FIFO
In this example, the elements are transmitted in the following order (assuming that all dedicated Tx buffers requests are
enabled):
1. Tx buffer index 6 (because it is the oldest pending Tx FIFO with identifier = 0 and has higher priority between all
dedicated Tx buffers)
2. Tx buffer index 4 (identifier = 3: it is the highest priority between all other dedicated Tx buffers and it has a higher
priority than the oldest pending Tx FIFO: Tx buffer 7)
3. Tx buffer index 3 (identifier = 4: it is the highest priority between all other dedicated Tx buffers and it has a higher
priority than the oldest pending Tx FIFO: Tx buffer 7)
4. Tx buffer index 7 (because it is the oldest pending Tx FIFO with identifier = 6 and has higher priority between all
dedicated Tx buffers)
5. Tx buffer index 8 (because it is the oldest pending Tx FIFO with identifier = 5 and has higher priority between all
dedicated Tx buffers)
6. Tx buffer index 2 (identifier = 7: it is the highest priority between all other dedicated Tx buffers)
7. Tx buffer index 0 (identifier = 8: it is the highest priority between all other dedicated Tx buffers)
8. Tx buffer index 5 (identifier = 15: it is the highest priority between all other dedicated Tx buffers)
9. Tx buffer index 1 (identifier = 24: because it is the only pending dedicated Tx buffer)
18.3.4.2.5 Transmit pause
The feature is controlled by TXPAUSE bit in CFG_MCR register. If the bit is set, the XKAN will, each time it has
successfully transmitted a message, pause for two CAN bit times before starting the next transmission. This enables other
CAN nodes in the network to transmit messages even if their messages have lower prior identifiers. Default is disabled
(CFG_MCR.TXPAUSE = 0).
This feature looses up burst transmissions coming from a single node and it protects against “babbling idiot” scenarios
where the application program erroneously requests too many transmissions.
18.3.4.2.6 Transmit cancellation
The XKAN supports transmit cancellation. To cancel a requested transmission from a dedicated Tx buffer the Host has to
write a 1 to the corresponding bit position (= number of Tx buffer) of register CFG_TXABORT_REQ. Transmit cancel lation is not intended for Tx FIFO operation.
18.3. Functional Description 283
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Successful cancellation is signaled by setting the corresponding bit of register CFG_TXABORT_FINISH to 1.
In case a transmit cancellation is requested while a transmission from a Tx buffer is already ongoing, As a result, The
corresponding Tx buffer can not be cancelled and the corresponding CFG_TXREQ_PENDING bit remains set as long as
the transmission is in progress. If the transmission was successful, the corresponding CFG_TXB_TRANS bits are set.
18.3.4.2.7 Timestamp generation
For timestamp generation the XKAN supplies a 16-bit wraparound counter. A prescaler CFG_TIME_DIV can be config ured to clock the counter in multiples of CAN bit times (1 . . . 16). The counter is readable via CFG_TIME_STAMP. A
write access to register CFG_TIME_STAMP resets the counter to 0. When the timestamp counter wraps around interrupt
flag CFG_INT_STATUS.TIME_STAMP_WRAP_INT is set.
When the dlc field of reception frame is finish, the counter value of preceding frame is stored into the timestamp section
of a Rx buffer (RTS[15:0]) element.
When can frame is reception/transmission done, the counter value of current frame is stored into cfg_time_stamp[15:0]
register.
By programming bit CFG_TIME_DIV, a 16-bit timestamp can be used.
18.3.4.3 Receive process
The XKAN Rx function controls the acceptance filtering, the transfer of received messages to Rx buffers.
18.3.4.3.1 Rx handling
The Rx handler controls the acceptance filtering, the transfer of received messages to Rx buffers or to one of the two Rx
FIFOs, as well as the Rx FIFO put and get Indices.
18.3.4.3.2 Acceptance filter
The XKAN offers the possibility to configure two sets of acceptance filters, one for standard identifiers and one for extended
identifiers. These filters can be assigned to Rx buffer, Rx FIFO 0 or Rx FIFO 1. For each incoming can message, the all
list of filters will be checked one by one. Acceptance filtering stops at the first matching element. The following filter
elements are not evaluated for this message.
The main features are:
• Each filter element can be configured as:
– filter for one specific ID
– classic bit mask filter
• Each filter element is configurable for acceptance or rejection filtering
• Each filter element can be enabled/disabled individually
• Filters are checked sequentially, execution stops with the first matching filter element
Related configuration registers are:
• Global filter configuration (CFG_GLOBAL_FITLER)
• Standard ID filter configuration (CFG_ST_FILTER)
• Extended ID filter configuration (CFG_EX_FILTER)
• Standard ID AND Mask (CFG_STIDM)
• Extended ID AND Mask (CFG_EXIDM)
Depending on the configuration of the filter element (Table 6.5 and 6.7) a match triggers one of the following actions:
• Store received frame in FIFO 0 or FIFO 1
18.3. Functional Description 284
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• Store received frame in Rx buffer
• Reject received frame
Acceptance filtering is started after the complete identifier has been received. After acceptance filtering has completed,
and if a matching Rx buffer or Rx FIFO has been found, the message handler starts writing the received message data in
portions of 32 bit to the matching Rx buffer or Rx FIFO. If the CAN protocol controller has detected an error condition
(e.g. CRC error), the new data flag of matching Rx buffer is set, Rx buffer or Rx FIFO will be filled by received data. For
error type see CFG_ESR register.
18.3.4.3.3 Classic bit mask filter
Classic bit mask filtering is intended to filter groups of message IDs by masking single bits of a received message ID. With
classic bit mask filtering CFG_STIDM / CFG_EXIDM is used as filter mask.
A 0 bit at the filter mask will mask out the corresponding bit position of the configured ID filter, e.g. the value of the
received message ID at that bit position is not relevant for acceptance filtering. Only those bits of the received message ID
where the corresponding mask bits are one are relevant for acceptance filtering.
In case all mask bits are one, a match occurs only when the received message ID and the message ID filter are identical. If
all mask bits are 0, all message IDs match.
18.3.4.3.4 XKAN standard message ID filter element
Standard message ID filtering
Standard Message ID filter match sequence (page 285): shows the flow for standard message ID (11-bit Identifier) filtering.
Fig. 18.10: Standard Message ID filter match sequence
Controlled by the global filter configuration (CFG_GLOBAL_FILTER) and the standard ID filter configuration
(CFG_ST_FILTER) message ID, remote transmission request bit (RTR), and the Identifier Extension bit (IDE) of received
frames are compared against the list of configured filter elements.
18.3. Functional Description 285
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
The extended ID AND Mask (CFG_STIDM) is AND-ed with the received identifier before the filter list is executed.
Up to 128 filter elements can be configured for 11-bit standard IDs. When accessing a standard message ID filter element,
its address is the filter list standard start address CFG_ST_FILTER.STFLT_ADDR plus the index of the filter element (0
. . . 127).
Table 18.5: Standard message id filter structure
BIT Description
[31:30] Standard filter type
-00: Range filter from SFID1 to SFID2
-01: Dual ID filter for SFID1 or SFID2
-10: Classic filter: SFID1 = filter, CFG_STIDM/SFID2[10:0] = mask
-11: Reserve for future use
[29:28] Standard Filter Element Configuration
-00: Store into Rx FIFO 0 (The Rx FIFO0 should be existed in RAM)
-01: Store into Rx FIFO 1 (The Rx FIFO1 should be existed in RAM)
-10: Store into Rx buffer (The Rx Buffer should be existed in RAM)
-11: Reserve for future use
[27] Reserve for future use
[26:16] Standard filter ID 1 (SFID1)
[15:11] Reserve for future use
[10:0] This bit field has a different meaning depending on the configuration of [29:28]:
1.If message store into Rx buffer, [5:0] Defines the offset to the Rx buffer start address
CFG_RXBUF.RXBUF_ADDR for storage of a matching message.
The register CFG_STIDM is used for Standard filter mask.
So In Rx buffer mode, The Standard filter type ([31:30]) can be 2’b10 only.
2.If message store into Rx FIFO 0/1, [10:0] Defines the Standard filter ID 2 (SFID2) or used for Standard
filter mask.

18.3.4.3.5 XKAN extend message ID filter element
Extend message ID filtering
Extend Message ID filter match sequence (page 287): shows the flow for extend message ID (29-bit Identifier) filtering.

Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual

The extended ID AND Mask (CFG_EXIDM) is AND-ed with the received identifier before the filter list is executed.
Up to 64 filter elements can be configured for 29-bit extended IDs. When accessing an Extended message ID filter element,

18.3.4.3.6 Dedicated Rx buffers
The XKAN supports up to 64 dedicated Rx buffers. The start address of the dedicated Rx buffer section is configured via
CFG_RXBUF register.
After a received message has been accepted by a filter element, the message is stored into the Rx buffer in the message RAM
referenced by the filter element. In addition the flag CFG_INT_STATUS.RXB_UPD_INT (message stored in dedicated
Rx buffer) in the interrupt register is set. After the last word of a matching received message has been written to the
message RAM, the respective response data flag in register CFG_RXDATL_RSP or CFG_RXDATH_RSP is set.
Soft-ware access Rx buffer process
• Get interrupt flag CFG_INT_STATUS.RXB_UPD_INT
• Read New data registers
• Read messages from message RAM
• Clear incoming data flags of processed messages by CFG_DATL_RSP or CFG_DATH_RSP
Rx buffer structure
Up to 64 Rx buffers can be configured in the message RAM. Each Rx buffer section can be configured to store up to 64
received messages.
18.3.4.3.7 Rx FIFOs
Rx FIFO 0 and Rx FIFO 1 can be configured to hold up to 64 elements each. Configuration of the two Rx FIFOs is done
via registers CFG_RXFIFO0 and CFG_RXFIFO1.
Received messages that passed acceptance filtering are transferred to the Rx FIFO as configured by the matching filter
element.
When an Rx FIFO full condition is signaled by CFG_RXFIFOn_STATUS.RXFFn_FULL, no further messages are written
to the corresponding Rx FIFO until at least one message has been read out and the Rx FIFO read pointer has been incre mented. In case a message is received while the corresponding Rx FIFO is full, this message is discarded and interrupt
flag CFG_INT_STATUS.RXFn_LOST_INT.
To avoid an Rx FIFO overflow, the Rx FIFO watermark can be used. When the Rx FIFO fill level reaches the Rx FIFO
watermark configured by CFG_RXFIFOn.RXFFn_WM, interrupt flag CFG_INT_STATUS.RXFn_WM_INT is set.
When soft-ware reading from an Rx FIFO and send a ackonwledge by set CFG_RXFIFOn_ACK.RXFn_ACK, The Rx
FIFO read pointer will be point to next entry position.
Rx FIFO blocking mode
18.3. Functional Description 288
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
The Rx FIFO blocking mode is configured by CFG_RXFIFOn.RXFFn_OPM = 0. This is the default operation mode for
the Rx FIFOs.
When an Rx FIFO full condition is reached (CFG_RXFIFOn_STATUS.RXFFn_WPTR =
CFG_RXFIFOn_STATUS.RXFFn_RPTR), no further messages are written to the corresponding Rx FIFO until at
least one message has been read out and the Rx FIFO read ponter has been incremented. An Rx FIFO full condition is
signaled by CFG_RXFn_STATUS.RXFn_FULL = 1. In addition interrupt flag CFG_INT_STATUS.RXFn_FULL_INT
is set.
Rx FIFO overwrite mode
The Rx FIFO blocking mode is configured by CFG_RXFIFOn.RXFFn_OPM = 1.
When an Rx FIFO full condition is signaled by CFG_RXFn_STATUS.RXFn_FULL = 1, the next message accepted for the
FIFO will always overwrite the oldest FIFO message. At this condition, The write and read pointer are both incremented
by one. What’s mode, If there is no more message is coming, The rest of Rx FIFO can be fetched out by soft-ware.
18.3.4.3.8 Rx element structure
The structure of Rx buffer and FIFO element is shown in The RX buffer structure (page 289).
Table 18.8: The RX buffer structure
BIT [31:24] [23:16] [15:8] [7:0]

MB1 ANHF,HFI[6:0] RES,ESI,EDL,BRS,DLC[3:0] RTS[15:8] RTS[7:0]
MB2 DATA3[7:0] DATA2[7:0] DATA1[7:0] DATA0[7:0]
MB3 DATA7[7:0] DATA6[7:0] DATA5[7:0] DATA4[7:0]
. . . . . . . . . . . . . . .

data field via register CFG_RXDSIZE.

Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual

frames.

0: Frame received without bitrate switching
1: Frame received with bitrate switching

18.3.4.4 XKAN Enter Low Power Mode
Fig. 18.12: XKAN in low power mode
18.3. Functional Description 290
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
The XKAN can be set into sleep mode (power down) controlled by clock stop request input via register
CFG_MCR.CLK_STOP_REQ. As long as the clock stop request is active, bit CFG_MCR.CLK_STOP_REQ is read as 1.
When all pending transmission requests have completed, the XKAN waits until bus idle state is detected. Now the XKAN
acknowledges that it is ready for power down by setting CFG_MCR.CLK_STOP_ACK to 1. In this state, the module clock
inputs (icb_clk and xkan_clk) may be switched off.
To leave power down mode, the application has to turn on the module clocks before resetting register
CFG_MCR.CLK_STOP_REQ. The XKAN will acknowledge this by resetting CFG_MCR.CLK_STOP_ACK. After wards, the application can restart XKAN communication by setting configuration register
18.3.5 Test mode
18.3.5.1 Silent mode
The XKAN can be put in Silent mode by setting the SILM in CFG_TEST register.
In Silent mode, the XKAN is able to receive valid data frames and valid remote frames, but it sends only recessive bits
on the CAN bus and it cannot start a transmission. If the XKAN has to send a dominant bit (ACK bit, overload flag,
active error flag), the bit is rerouted internally so that the CAN Core monitors this dominant bit, although the CAN bus
may remain in recessive state. Silent mode can be used to analyze the traffic on a CAN bus without affecting it by the
transmission of dominant bits (Acknowledge Bits, Error Frames).
Fig. 18.13: XKAN in silent mode
18.3.5.2 Loop back mode
The XKAN can be set in Loop Back Mode by setting the LBKM bit in the CAN_TEST register. In Loop Back Mode, the
XKAN treats its own transmitted messages as received messages and stores them (if they pass acceptance filtering) in a
receive ram buffer.
This mode is provided for self-test functions. To be independent of external events, the CAN Core ignores acknowledge
errors (no dominant bit sampled in the acknowledge slot of a data / remote frame) in Loop Back Mode. In this mode, the
XKAN performs an internal feedback from its Tx output to its Rx input. The actual value of the XKAN_RX input pin is
disregarded by the XKAN. The transmitted messages can be monitored on the XKAN_TX pin.
18.3. Functional Description 291
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Fig. 18.14: XKAN in loop back mode
18.3.5.3 Loop back combined with silent mode
It is also possible to combine Loop Back mode and Silent mode by setting the LBKM and SILM bits in the CAN_TEST
register. This mode can be used for a “Hot Selftest”, meaning the XKAN can be tested like in Loop Back mode but
without affecting a running CAN system connected to the CAN_TX and CAN_RX pins. In this mode, the CAN_RX pin
is disconnected from the XKAN and the CAN_TX pin is held recessive.
Fig. 18.15: XKAN in loop back with silent mode
18.3.6 Clock Domains and Restrictions
The XKAN module has two clock domains asynchronous to each other:
• The icb bus clock(icb_clk) is used for register configuration.
• The kernel clock(xkan_clk) is used for CAN protocol generation.
When the two domains are connected to clocks with different frequencies and/or phases, there are restrictions on the
frequency relationship between the two clock domains. In the case of asynchronous operation, the icb bus clock(icb_clk)
must always be greater than the kernel clock(xkan_clk) frequency.
Note: Asynchronous operation with a 1:1 ratio between peripheral and oscilator clocks is not allowed.
18.3.7 Bit Timing Generation
The bit timing logic monitors the serial bus-line and performs sampling and adjustment of the sample point by synchro nizing on the start-bit edge and resynchronizing on the following edges.
As shown in XKAN Bit Timing Generation (page 293):, its operation may be explained simply by splitting the bit time in
three segments, as follows:
• Synchronization segment (SYNC_SEG): a bit change is expected to occur within this time segment, that has a
fixed length of one time quantum (1*tq).
18.3. Functional Description 292
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
• Bit segment 1 (BS1): defines the location of the sample point. It includes the PROP_SEG and PHASE_SEG1 of
the CAN standard, its duration is programmable between 1 and 16 time quanta, but may be automatically lengthened
to compensate for positive phase drifts due to differences in the frequency of the various nodes of the network.
• Bit segment 2 (BS2): defines the location of the transmit point. It represents the PHASE_SEG2 of the CAN stan dard, its duration is programmable between one and eight time quanta, but may also be automatically shortened to
compensate for negative phase drifts.
Fig. 18.16: XKAN Bit Timing Generation
The baudrate is the inverse of bit time (baudrate = 1/bit time), which, in turn, is the sum of three components. The XKAN
Bit Timing Generation (page 293): indicates that bit time = tSyncSeg + tBS1 + tBS2, where:
• For the nominal bit time
• tq = (CFG_NBTR.NBRP[8:0] + 1) * T_can_clk
• tSyncSeg = 1 tq
• tBS1 = tq * (CFG_NBTR.NTSEG1[7:0] + 1)
• tBS2 = tq * (CFG_NBTR.NTSEG2[6:0] + 1)
• For the data bit time
• tq = (CFG_DBTR.NBRP[4:0] + 1) * T_can_clk
• tSyncSeg = 1 tq
• tBS1 = tq * (CFG_NBTR.NTSEG1[4:0] + 1)
• tBS2 = tq * (CFG_NBTR.NTSEG2[3:0] + 1)
The (re)synchronization jump width (SJW) defines an upper bound for the amount of lengthening or shortening of the bit
segments. It is programmable between one and four time quanta.
A valid edge is defined as the first transition in a bit time from dominant to recessive bus level, provided the controller
itself does not send a recessive bit.
If a valid edge is detected in BS1 instead of SYNC_SEG, BS1 is extended by up to SJW so that the sample point is delayed.
Conversely, if a valid edge is detected in BS2 instead of SYNC_SEG, BS2 is shortened by up to SJW so that the transmit
point is moved earlier.
As a safeguard against programming errors, the configuration of the bit timing register is only possible while the device is
in INITIAL mode.

has left Sleep mode (to be synchronized on the CAN bus).

sequence of 11 consecutive recessive bits on the XKAN
RX signal.

1. The initial value shall be (0,. . . .,0) for CRC15 and shall be
(1,0,. . . ,0) for CRC17 and for CRC21; where the single “1”
is at the most significant bit position.
2. In FD Frames, the 3-bits stuff count and its parity bit shall
be at the beginning of the CRC field.
3. Include stuff bit count in CRC calculation bit stream.
In BOSCH mode:
1. The initial value shall be (0,. . . .,0) for CRC15 , for CRC17
and for CRC21.
2. In FD Frames, There is no stuff count shall be at the begin ning of the CRC field.
Reserved 31:1 / 0x0 /
Note
Note: Please configure those bits carefully, which is related to CAN Specification.
18.5.1.14 CFG_RXDATL_RSP
Address offset: 0x40.
Table 18.25: Register Format of CFG_RXDATL_RSP
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
LOW 31:0 W1C 0x0 Rx low data[31:0] response
The register holds the New data flags of Rx buffers 0 to 31. The
flags are set when the respective Rx buffer has been updated from
a received frame. The flags remain set until
the Host clears them. A flag is cleared by writing a 1 to the corre sponding bit position.
Writing a 0 has no effect. A hard reset will clear the register.
0: Rx buffer not updated
1: Rx buffer updated from new coming message
18.5.1.15 CFG_RXDATH_RSP
Address offset: 0x44.
18.5. Registers 303
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 18.26: Register Format of CFG_RXDATH_RSP
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
HIGH 31:0 W1C 0x0 Rx high data[31:0] response
The register holds the New data flags of Rx buffers 31 to 63. The
flags are set when the respective Rx buffer has been updated from
a received frame. The flags remain set until
the Host clears them. A flag is cleared by writing a 1 to the corre sponding bit position.
Writing a 0 has no effect. A hard reset will clear the register.
0: Rx buffer not updated
1: Rx buffer updated from new coming message

Address offset: 0x48.
Table 18.27: Register Format of CFG_RXFIFO0
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
RXFF0_ADDR 10:0 RW 0x0 Rx FIFO 0 start address
Start address of Rx FIFO 0 in message RAM
Reserved 11 / 0x0 /
RXFF0_NUM 18:12 RW 0x0 Rx FIFO 0 size
0: No Rx FIFO 0
1-64: Number of Rx FIFO 0 elements
The Rx FIFO 0 elements are indexed from 0 to F0S-1.
RXFF0_WM 25:19 RW 0x0 FIFO 0 Watermark
0: Watermark interrupt disabled
1-64: Level for Rx FIFO 0 watermark interrupt
>64: Watermark interrupt disabled
Reserved 29:26 / 0x0 /
RXFF0_CLR 30 WO 0x0 FIFO 0 Clear
When soft-ware write “1” to this bit, The status of Rx FIFO0 are
all return to default value.
RXFF0_OPM 31 RW 0x0 FIFO 0 operation mode
FIFO 0 can be operated in blocking or in overwrite mode.
0: FIFO 0 blocking mode
1: FIFO 0 overwrite mode

1. When Rx FIFO 0 is working in the blocking mode, If
new CAN frame is coming when Rx FIFO 0 is full, This
bit is stand for new message has lost.
2. When Rx FIFO 0 is working in the overwrite mode, If
new CAN frame is coming when Rx FIFO 0 is full, This
bit is stand for the oldest entry in Rx FIFO 0 is overwrite
by new message.

18.5.1.27 CFG_TXBAR
Address offset: 0x7C.
18.5. Registers 309
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 18.38: Register Format of CFG_TXBAR
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
TX_REQ 31:0 RW 0x0 Buffer add request
Each Tx buffer has its own add request bit. Writing a 1 will set the
corresponding add request bit;
writing a 0 has no impact. This enables the Host to set
transmission requests for multiple Tx buffers with one write to
CFG_TXBAR. CFG_TXBAR bits are set only for those Tx buffers
configured via CFG_TXBUF.
When no Tx scan is running, the bits are reset immediately, else
the bits remain set until the Tx scan process has completed.
0: No transmission request added
1: Transmission requested added
Note:
1.In normal mode, the cfg_txbar register can enable one or more
bits meantime.
2.In test mode, the cfg_txbar register can enable one bit only.
18.5.1.28 CFG_TXABORT_REQ
Address offset: 0x80.
Table 18.39: Register Format of CFG_TXABORT_REQ
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
REQ 31:0 RW 0x0 Each Tx buffer has its own cancellation request bit.
Writing a 1 will set the corresponding cancellation request bit; writing
a 0 has no impact. The bit are cleared when current frame is transmit
finish.
0: No cancellation pending
1: Cancellation pending
Note:
1. The pending transmit (corresponding
CFG_TXREQ_PENDING bit) can not be abort.
2. The abort function is not intended for Tx FIFO operation.
18.5.1.29 CFG_TXABORT_FINISH
Address offset: 0x84.
18.5. Registers 310
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 18.40: Register Format of CFG_TXABORT_FINISH
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
FINISH 31:0 W1C 0x0 Tx abort finished
Each Tx buffer has its own abort finished bit. The bits which were
requested via CFG_TXABORT_REQ are set when current frame
is transmit finish.
The bits are cleared by when a new transmission is re quested by writing a 1 to the corresponding bit of register
CFG_TXBAR or writing a 1 to the corresponding bit of register
CFG_TXABORT_FINISH.
0: No transmit buffer cancellation
1: Transmit buffer cancellation finished
18.5.1.30 CFG_TXREQ_PENDING
Address offset: 0x88.
Table 18.41: Register Format of CFG_TXREQ_PENDING
Field Bit Access Reset
Value
Description
REQ_PENDING 31:0 W1C 0x0 Transmission request pending.
Each Tx buffer has its own transmission request pending
bit. The bits are set via register CFG_TXBAR.
When a CFG_TXBAR is set, TX scan is start to check
for CFG_TXBAR, after the highest priority (Tx buffer
with lowest message ID) is scan out, The correspond
CFG_TXREQ_PENDING bit is set,
The bits are reset after a requested transmission has
completed or reset when soft-ware write 1 to this bits.
0: No transmission request pending
1: Transmission request pending
Note: The bits are set only for those Tx buffers config ured via CFG_TXBC.
18.5.1.31 CFG_TXB_TRANS
Address offset: 0x8C.
Table 18.42: Register Format of CFG_TXB_TRANS
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
TX_COMPLETE 31:0 W1C 0x0 Transmission complete.
Each Tx buffer has its own transmission occurred bit. The
bits are set after a successful transmission, which corresponding
CFG_TXBAR.TX_REQ bit .
The bits are reset when a new transmission is requested by writing
a 1 to the corresponding bit of register CFG_TXBAR or reset when
soft-ware write 1 to this bits.
0: No transmission occurred
1: Transmission occurred

Description

18.5.1.35 CFG_ESR
Address offset: 0x9C.
18.5. Registers 312
Nuclei® Xunlian Subsystem Reference Manual
Table 18.46: Register Format of CFG_ESR
Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
TEC 7:0 RO 0x0 Transmit error counter
Actual state of the transmit error counter, values between 0 and
256.
REC 15:8 RO 0x0 Receive error counter
Actual state of the receive error counter, values between 0 and
128.
ERR_ACTIVE_FLAG 16 RO 0x1 Error active flag
This bit is set by hardware when the XKAN is in the error active
state.
ERR_PASS_FLAG 17 RO 0x0 Error passive flag
This bit is set by hardware when the XKAN is in the error pas sive state.
BUS_OFF 18 RO 0x0 Bus-off flag
This bit is set by hardware when it enters the bus-off state. The
bus-off state is entered on TEC is greater than 255.
LEC 21:19 W1C 0x0 Last error code
This field is set by hardware and holds a code which indicates
the error condition of the last error detected on the CAN bus.
If a message has been transferred (reception or
transmission) without error, this field will be cleared to “0”.The
LEC[2:0] bits can be set to value 0b111 by software. They
are updated by hardware to indicate the current communication
status.
• 3’b000: No Error
• 3’b001: Stuff Error
• 3’b010: Form Error
• 3’b011: Acknowledgment Error
• 3’b100: Bit recessive Error (xkan send recessive bit, but
read back is dominant bit)
• 3’b101: Bit dominant Error (xkan send dominant bit, but
read back is recessive bit)
• 3’b110: CRC Error
Reserved 31:22 / 0x0 /

Field Bit Ac cess
Reset
Value
Description
TX_NUM 31:0 W1C 0x0 Trans error tx buffer index
This field is set by hardware when correspond tx buffer in dex meet error in its own transmit and cleared when soft ware write 1 to this bits.

18.6 Full Signal Interface
XKAN Full Signal Interface (page 316) gives the list of XKAN full signals.
Table 18.50: XKAN Full Signal Interface
Signal Name Dir Width Description
xkan_tx_i_ival Input 1 Receiver data from xkan_tx pin
xkan_tx_o_oval Output 1 Transmitter data to xkan_tx pin
xkan_tx_o_oe Output 1 The output enable to xkan_tx pin
xkan_tx_o_pue Output 1 The pull-up enable to xkan_tx pin
xkan_rx_i_ival Input 1 The input value from xkan_rx pin
xkan_rx_o_oval Output 1 The output value to xkan_rx pin
xkan_rx_o_oe Output 1 The output enable to xkan_rx pin
xkan_rx_o_pue Output 1 The pull-up enable to xkan_rx pin
icb_clk Input 1 The icb bus clock
icb_rstn Input 1 The icb bus reset
xkan_clk Input 1 The xkan kernel clock
aon_clk Input 1 The ram work clock, which is the same as icb clock
clkgate_bypass Input 1 The clkgate_bypass for xkan ram’s clk
reset_bypass Input 1 The reset bypass signal for reset sync module
xkan_int_o Output 1 The interrupt of XKAN
Note: In XKAN, there are two types of icb bus signals:
• The xkan_ram_icb* bus is used to access RAM by soft-ware.
• The icb* bus is used to configure register.
You can get description of each icb bus signal in detail by below table.
18.6. Full Signal Interface 316
19
Misc Ctrl
This section describes the soc_misc register module. Please refer to Address Map of the SoC (page 15) for the base address
19.1 Registers
19.1.1 MISC Registers
Registers and the corresponding offset in the MISC are shown in The offset address of registers in the MISC (page 317).
